### 7-1: creation beside the Lifepath (owner item 1)

- **Decision:** Three creation procedures. The **Lifepath** stands as written and is always available. The **Template Build** takes the chosen Specialty's Squadmate template array (`squadmates.yaml` `templates`: 18 points, key attribute 4). The **Free Build** places 18 points with every attribute 2 to 4, the key attribute at exactly 4, and at most two attributes at 4; the two legal shapes are 4, 3, 3, 3, 3, 2 and 4, 4, 3, 3, 2, 2, in any order that keeps the key attribute at 4. The GM decides at session zero whether the campaign allows the Template Build, the Free Build, or both, once for the campaign and for every soldier created in it, replacements included; players at one table may mix any procedures the campaign allows. Both builds: the Campaign Year is recorded first, as for the Lifepath; the player chooses an Origin row whose condition the Campaign Year meets, and records its Haven, optionally its Canon Tie, and 1 level in one of its two Talents; chooses a Drive from any Why You Enlisted row; may name the three Training Year event rows for the story with no effect; records Merit none, Class Rank none, Rank Private, and no Top 10; takes no Graduation Exam and no Exam Merit; gains 4 more Talent levels, one of them from the Specialty's list and three from any Talent, following `talent_cap` (at most one Talent at level 2, rule Talents at level 1); records Health and Resolve from the final ratings; receives Standard Issue and joins the Squad as section 2.3.5 states. Neither build reaches a key attribute of 5 or 6, a Top 10 Class Rank, or the Exam: rolling stays the only way there. The chapter frames the three as equals in one sentence: a built soldier is the tuned Rookie every Titan number was set against, with the choice of what they carry; a rolled soldier is the same soldier on average, with a chance of more and a chance of less. The Template Build is the default replacement path for a player with no Squadmate to promote (7-11).
- **Why:** The owner asked for both a template and a freer build; the research proved that choosing rows or a raw budget with a cap of 5 puts a 5 or a 6 on nearly every soldier (88.9% with chosen rows against 2.1% rolled), which would move every Severity and solo figure ADR-0014 tunes against Agility 3 and Strength 4. A cap of 4 with at most two 4s is the freest budget that stays inside the Lifepath's envelope: the second-4 array trades a 3 and a 3 for a 4 and a 2, its Health is 4 or 3, and its Resolve is at most 4, so it is a Rookie with a chosen strength and a chosen weakness, not a Veteran. A chosen Talent at 2 on the key roll is inside the envelope as well (a Clean Cut 2 Rookie's fresh cut is 18.5%, a rolled Strength 5 soldier's 18.8%), and stacking is what the one-level-2 cap forbids. "The GM allows either per campaign" is read as a session-zero choice for the campaign, because a per-soldier choice would let one player take the certainty and another the gamble at the same table by the GM's leave, which is the ruling ADR-0003 keeps out of creation. The Free Build Squad is a reported row so that the playtest's measured Squad stays the reference Rookie. Figures: none move; the Template Build is ADR-0014's template Squad, and the Free Build row is reported, not tuned.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 7): the Free Build Squad as a reported row beside the Talent-dependent targets. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Lifepath ("The rolled character creation procedure ... The only way to a key attribute of 5 or 6 or a Top 10 Class Rank"); new **Template Build** and **Free Build**; Merit and Class Rank gain "or none for a built or promoted soldier". Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 2 section 2.1 (three procedures named), 2.2 *How the Lifepath sets attribute ratings* (gains the two built shapes and the cap), 2.3 (the introduction names the alternatives and the campaign choice), 2.3.5 (Merit and Class Rank none for a built soldier), 2.4 (a built soldier takes no Exam), a new section 2.11 *Building a soldier without the Lifepath* after 2.10 (both procedures, numbered, with the framing sentence and a design note citing OQ-135), 2.10 *Promotion* step 4 (7-11); `data/character/lifepath.yaml` (a `procedures` block naming lifepath, template-build, and free-build, with `campaign_choice` and each build's steps; `record_on_sheet` gains the none values), `data/character/attributes.yaml` `creation` (a `built` block: 18 points, min 2, max 4, key 4, at most two at 4), `data/character/class-rank.yaml` (a built soldier records none), `data/character/squadmates.yaml` `templates` (a comment that the Template Build uses these arrays) and `promotion.no_squadmate` (7-11); the packet's *Creating your soldier* gains one box. New entry OQ-135; OQ-19 and OQ-20 carry `Revised by batch 7` lines.

### 7-2: the full Talent list (owner item 2)

- **Decision:** The Talent list ships in full for the first playtest: between 80 and 90 Talents. Nine Specialty lists of eight, shareable as OQ-28 (c) allows, with at most four Talents shared between two lists so that at least 68 distinct Talents sit on the lists; each list holds at least three dice Talents, one of them conditional (the Horsemanship pattern), at least three rule Talents, and at least two Talents that name a Leg, camp, Requisition, or Skirmish roll or trigger from the hook lists of the minimal rules proposal (its sections 1.3, 2.3, and 3.3, which are binding as the names a Talent may use). A general list of twelve, about temperament and survival, at most four of them dice Talents. Every Talent names live entries: the playtest rules chapter makes every dormant and reserved entry live (7-14, 7-16, 7-17), so no Talent is dormant, and the packet's dormant tag goes. The 5-level, max-2 start, single-level rule Talents, and the shareable lists stand. The Lifepath's Origin and Training Year pairs are unchanged; a Talent no row offers is reached by a build (7-1) or, in Phase 2, by XP. XP stays Phase 2. No Talent raises Health or Resolve (7-3). Capstones, a level-3 effect on a Specialty's signature dice Talent, are a new Talent shape and are logged as OQ-144 for after the playtest; no Talent has a level-3 effect or a prerequisite now.
- **Why:** The owner chose past 60. Eight per list fills the Hunter's half-grey page and gives two Slayers a different sheet without duplicating dice Talents, which the live rolled entries already saturate; the general list is where Coriolis: The Great Dark carries temperament, which WoF had in one Talent. The per-list minimums make the minimal rules pay for their cost: the owner adopted them so that Talents have something to read, and a list with no Expedition, Requisition, or Skirmish Talent would leave those rules unread. Ninety is the ceiling because every new Talent that touches a measured roll needs a sensitivity row and a review, and the packet's Talent reference must still read as one list. Capstones wait because they need the XP rules to be reachable and a measurement against the Levi-grade cut, which sits at 47.2% in a 45% to 55% band. Figures: sensitivity rows only; no target moves, and a row that does moves the Talent, not the target.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 7, with 7-3): every new Talent that names a measured entry or a fixed roll is a sensitivity row where the engine can express it, or is listed as unmeasured. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 2 section 2.6 (lists of eight), 2.7 (the counts and the shape; the dormant paragraph goes), Appendix 2A.1 and 2A.2 (the full lists); `data/character/talents.yaml` (the new rows; `talent_rules.dormant_talents` stays for later rules), `data/character/specialties.yaml` (eight per list; a `general` list of twelve); the packet's Talent reference. New entry OQ-136; OQ-28 and OQ-33 carry `Revised by batch 7` lines; new entry OQ-144 (open).

### 7-3: Talents beyond canon (owner item 2b)

- **Decision:** Non-canon Talents are accepted under the nine guardrails the research proposed, recorded as the Talent section of ADR-0016: live entries and no permission; nothing supernatural or Titan-derived; no attribute raise and no Health or Resolve raise (a Talent may count Resolve as higher for one roll, as Iron Nerve does); a sensitivity row for every Talent that touches a measured roll or a fixed roll; period technology; rule Talents over dice Talents, conditional dice Talents excepted; teamwork levers first; once per Titan Engagement, Leg, night camp, Skirmish, or Downtime as the default limit; names in the setting's register. The drafting rule: a Talent that names only a Leg, camp, Requisition, or Skirmish roll carries no dice in any ADR-0014 baseline, extending batch 3's exclusion of Expedition rolls, and needs no sensitivity row unless it also names a Titan Engagement roll. The test for a Talent is whether it could be a paragraph in a Training Corps manual.
- **Why:** The owner accepted the guardrails as recommended. Recording them in an ADR rather than a design note gives every later drafter and reviewer one test to cite, and closes the question for good. The no-dice rule keeps the reference builds where ADR-0014 fixed them: a Leg roll, a Requisition roll, and a Skirmish roll are outside every target, so a Talent on them can be as generous as the fiction wants without touching a band. The Health and Resolve line is the one guardrail that forbids rather than gates, because Health decides every Critical Injury count and Resolve every Fear Roll, and a Talent that raised either would be a lever on lethality dressed as a perk.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0016 (new), Talents section; ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 7), the Talent exclusion. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 2 section 2.7 (a design note that cites ADR-0016 as the test for every Talent, and the no-dice rule); `data/character/talents.yaml` (`adrs` gains ADR-0016). The reviewers check every new Talent against the nine guardrails.

### 7-4: left and right on the location die (owner item 3)

- **Decision:** The Injury Location roll reads 1 left arm, 2 right arm, 3 left leg, 4 right leg, 5 torso, 6 head; every location weight is unchanged. When a rule names arm or leg without a side (every Chapter 6 entry that fixes a limb), roll D6: odd is left, even is right. Worsening counts every Critical Injury the soldier holds at that Injury Location and side, treated or not, and every healed one there whose row has permanent effects. `held_injuries.sides` reads: sides are tracked; a row with permanent effects at a limb is gained at most once per side, and a repeat at that side uses its `repeat_row`; the eye keeps its single repeat. The character sheet records the side on every Critical Injury entry ("Right arm, Bite: Bitten Off at the Shoulder, untreated, turn, 28 days"), and `healed_permanent_injuries` is sided.
- **Why:** The owner asked for the six-part body, and it is ADR-0005's own founding reason, which four unsided locations underdelivered: the reviews found the same arm could be lost twice, and the `repeat_row` fix papered over the missing side. The die already split 1 to 2 and 3 to 4, so sides cost no odds on a first roll; only worsening at a limb fires half as often, which lowers deaths slightly, the direction OQ-132 needed. Figures: the Medium band, the Grab cells, and the bar are re-measured as a check; the Chapter 3 section 3.2 figures for a second limb injury (arm lethal 41.7% with one held) now apply per side and the chapter says so.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0017 (new); ADR-0005 `## Amended` (batch 7). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Injury Location lists six; Critical Injury reads "the same location and side". Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.2 *Gaining a Critical Injury* (the die, the odd-even rule, worsening by side), *Holding and treating* (sides), section 3.7 (the Grab's torso is unsided; no figure changes), section 3.17 and the example (a sided entry); `data/harm/critical-injuries.yaml` (`injury_location_table` six rows with `side`; `gaining.injury-location` gains the odd-even step; `worsening.counts` by side; `held_injuries.sides`), `data/harm/sheet-fields.yaml` (`side` on each entry; `healed_permanent_injuries` sided); `tools/render/render.py` (the location table); `tools/sim/dice.py` `gain_ci` (count by side). OQ-41 carries a `Revised by batch 7` line; new entry OQ-137.

### 7-5: Injury Type, per-type names, the Bite rider, Crush, Blast, Cut and Pierce, no mental rows (owner item 3)

- **Decision:** Five Injury Types: **Crush**, **Bite**, **Burn**, **Cut**, **Pierce**. Every rule that inflicts a Critical Injury names its type on the effect (`injury_type`), and every rule that deals damage names the type the Critical Injury at 0 Health records. Chapter 6: every Bite entry is Bite; every other harming entry (Swat, Clutch at the Legs, Crush, Trample, Headlong Lunge, Shake Off, and every fallback) is Crush; the Grab's crush is Crush; every fall is Crush; the Straggler's Critical Injury is Bite (7-14); a Blade Set, sabre, or knife is Cut, a shot is Pierce, a club or a fist is Crush (7-17); Burn has no source before the playtest (7-7). Each row of the four location tables keeps its id and every tuned field and gains `names`, one per type; the row's `name` is retired as a display name and the render prints the five names in one cell, the sheet the one that applies. The current row names that are crush results stay as the Crush names; the cut-flavoured ones (Gashed Forearm, Torn Artery, Opened Artery, Split Scalp, Torn Open) get Crush names and keep their ids. **The Bite rider:** on the arm and leg tables the 12 and 13+ rows (`arm-mangled-arm`, `arm-lost-arm`, `leg-shattered-knee`, `leg-lost-leg`) have `time_limit: turn` when the type is Bite; the torso and head tables carry no Bite rider. No other type rider ships now (7-7). Crush, never bludgeoning. Blast is a delivery, not a type: when Thunder Spears arrive, a blast rolls one Burn and one Crush at two rolled locations. Cut and Pierce are live through the Skirmish rules (damage at 0 Health carries the type). The physical tables carry no mental rows: Winded's and Dazed's Stress gains stay, and no lethal row gains Stress; lasting harm to the mind stays with Stress Responses, Fear Rolls, Scars, and Grief.
- **Why:** The owner confirmed cause variety, no mental Critical Injuries, Injury Type, Crush, Blast as a delivery, and Cut and Pierce through Skirmishes. Structure (c) of the research, one location table with the type choosing the name and a few riders, is the only one that keeps the 32 tuned rows, ADR-0012's single source, and the simulator's loader nearly intact; the alternatives cost 128 to 180 tuned entries before a first playtest. Bite is its own type because without it every Phase 1 Titan-fight Critical Injury would be Crush and the axis would change nothing until rifles; its rider is the one place a type changes a field now, and it is on the rows the fiction demands (a Bite takes the limb and bleeds fast). "Damage Type" is refused because damage already names the harm kind that marks boxes. Figures: renaming moves nothing; the Bite rider moves a turn limit onto two rare rows (arm-lost-arm and leg-lost-leg already have it, so the change is on the 12 rows only) and is re-measured as a check with the sides of 7-4.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0017 (new); ADR-0003 item 11 and ADR-0015 `## Amended` (batch 7). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** new **Injury Type** (Avoid: damage type, wound type, damage kind); Critical Injury cites it. Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.1 *The kinds of harm* (damage carries a type), 3.2 *Reading a row* (the names cell; the rider block), *The four Injury Location tables* (re-rendered), 3.17; Chapter 4 section 4.6 (falls are Crush); Chapter 5 section 5.9 (the crush is Crush); Chapter 6 every harming entry's effect text and the stat blocks' effect cells; `data/harm/health.yaml` (`harm_kinds.damage.parameters.injury_type`, `harm_kinds.critical-injury.parameters.injury_type`), `data/harm/critical-injuries.yaml` (`row_fields.names`, each row's `names`, a `type_riders` block per table with the Bite rider, `types` listing the five with Blast's note), `data/engagement/titan-format.yaml` (`effects` require `injury_type` on critical-injury), `data/engagement/grab.yaml` (crush: Crush), `data/gear/falls.yaml` (Crush), `data/titans/standard-small.yaml`, `standard-medium.yaml`, `standard-large.yaml`, `sprinting-abnormal.yaml` (every harming effect); `tools/render/render.py` (the names cell), `tools/probes/chapter-06/render.py` (the effect column), `tools/probes/chapter-06/run6.py` check (every harming effect names a type); `tools/sim/rules.py` and `dice.py` (the rider). The packet prints the Crush, Bite, Cut, and Pierce names and omits Burn until it has a source. OQ-42 carries a `Revised by batch 7` line; OQ-137.

### 7-6: graded limb loss (owner item 4)

- **Decision:** A `lost_limb_riders` block in `critical-injuries.yaml`, applied from the moment the 13+ row is gained at a limb and for as long as the soldier lives, as permanent effects are:
  - **One arm** (`arm-lost-arm` at one side): the row's 2-die penalty on Nape strike, Body Part strike, Break Free, Treat Injury, and Field Repair stands, and the soldier also takes a 1-die penalty on Fly, Fight, and Block. Everything else is unchanged: Ride, the mounted dodge, Read, Rally, Break Attention, Draw Attention, Help, and command from the saddle.
  - **Both arms** (`arm-lost-arm` at both sides): the soldier cannot take any entry whose requirements name ODM Gear, a Blade Set, a firearm, or handling an item or comrade: the strikes, Break Free, Fly, ODM moves, Field Repair, Treat Injury, Lift Comrade, Pass Item, Take Item, Swap Blade Set, Change Canister, Restock Medical Kit, Fight, Block, Shoot, Reload, and the Feint and flare decoys; the drafter enumerates the closed list from the Catalog. The soldier keeps Ride (mounted with help), the mounted dodge, Read, Call It, Rally, Draw Attention, Help, Cover, and Swap Initiative Card.
  - **One leg** (`leg-lost-leg` at one side): the row's 2-die penalty on Fly, Ride, and the dodge stands, and every move the soldier makes on foot spends their action as well, as Overloaded does for ODM moves.
  - **Both legs** (`leg-lost-leg` at both sides): the soldier cannot Fly, Ride, make an ODM move or a move on foot, or Lift Comrade; they travel across a comrade's saddle or in the wagon, hold the Position they are placed at, and keep the 2-die dodge penalty; they may still strike a Titan at In Reach, Read, Rally, Break Attention, Draw Attention, Treat Injury, and Field Repair.
  - **Medical Retirement:** a soldier who has lost both arms or both legs may retire at the player's choice when the procedure ends, at the same moment and with the same effect as Retirement at five Scars (`scars.yaml` `retirement` gains the second trigger).
- **Why:** The owner set the grade: one limb harder, both severe. One arm keeps Erwin in the saddle and out of the air by penalty rather than ban, since the owner refused a ban on one limb; both arms is the ban the handles demand. One leg makes the ground slow (the cost Levi paid) without ending flight; both legs ends movement, because ODM landing and running need legs, while the hands can still cut a Titan that comes to them. Medical Retirement is provisional: the owner was not asked, and without it a soldier who cannot fly waits for five Scars with no rule that turns them into the instructor Retirement promises; it is listed under Needs owner in the plan. Figures: no measured build loses a limb; the rows' Down and lethal fields are unchanged.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0017 (new) states that limb loss is graded. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Retirement ("at five Scars, or by choice after losing both arms or both legs"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.2 (a *Lost limbs* paragraph under *Holding and treating*), section 3.13 *Retirement* (the second trigger); `data/harm/critical-injuries.yaml` `lost_limb_riders` (four blocks with `forbids_entries`, `penalties`, and `moves`), `data/mind/scars.yaml` `retirement.trigger`; `data/harm/effect-types.yaml` (a `forbids-entries` effect type, or the existing `forbids` hook widened to entries, the drafter's choice recorded in the YAML comment); Chapter 4 section 4.7 (the on-foot move that spends the action); the packet's Critical Injuries section. OQ-137.

### 7-7: Critical Injuries step 2, logged

- **Decision:** Burn's sources (Titan steam at a Nape cut as damage, a soldier pinned under a dead Titan as the Burn Critical Injury source, fire in Chapter 4) and the Cut, Pierce, and Burn riders are logged as OQ-138 for after the first playtest, to be decided with OQ-140. Burn names are written on every row now (7-5) and rendered in Chapter 3; the packet omits the Burn names until a source exists.
- **Why:** A Burn Critical Injury at every kill adds about 0.85 Critical Injuries a fight and breaks the Medium band; steam sized as damage must be measured first, and Pinned adds a procedure to Chapter 5 after its last review round. The first playtest measures the tuned core.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** none now. New entry OQ-138 (open).

### 7-8: the Fear Roll table re-cut (owner item 5)

- **Decision:** The Fear Roll table is re-cut on Attack on Titan rows, nine or ten rows from 0 or less to 8 or more (an optional 9 or more), under placement rules that bind the drafter: totals 1 and 2 cost Stress, a next-roll penalty, or a Gas Roll and never an action; totals 3 and 4 spend the next action; 5 and 6 spend the next turn; 7 and up spend the turn and give a Scar; a forced strike goes only on a row at total 7 or more; a gear effect that costs an action to undo (a dropped Blade Set) goes only on a row that already spends the action or the turn; no row forces another Fear Roll; no row helps; each row makes at most one tracker write; no row leaves anything to the GM (it names the item, the Titan, and the step). The effect types a row may use are the existing six (stress-gain, next-roll-penalty, spend-next-action, spend-next-turn, no-reactions, gain-scar) and six new ones in `effect-types.yaml`: `draw-attention` (the soldier holds the loudest flag on the Focus Titan that caused the event, or the nearest one, under ADR-0003 item 2, only if they hold a Position other than Distant relative to it; nothing otherwise); `stress-gain-nearby` (every comrade within one Position step of the soldier, relative to that Titan, gains 1 Stress; in a Skirmish, every soldier in it; outside both, no effect); `forced-move` (at the start of the soldier's next turn, before its action, one Position step toward Distant or toward the nearest comrade as the row states, under the retreat's move rule and its exceptions; no move if Grabbed, Down, or carried; outside a Titan Engagement, no effect); `forced-action` (the soldier's next action must be a Nape strike or a Body Part strike against the Titan that caused the event, Pushed if it falls short, and until it is taken the soldier cannot Help or Cover, through the OQ-18 hook; in a Skirmish, a Fight or Shoot against the Foe; only at total 7 or more); `drop-blade-set` (the Blade Set in the handles is left at the soldier's Position, as Chapter 4's left items are, and refitting is Swap Blade Set); `gas-roll` (an immediate two-die Gas Roll on the soldier's current canister, as Chapter 4 defines it, if its Gas Rating is above 0). Contagion is Stress, not rolls: `stress-gain-nearby` on the top row only, and no row anywhere causes another Fear Roll (OQ-50 (b) revised). Every row gains a `text` field, one or two sentences of second-person fiction with no rule words, which the render prints in a "What happens" column. `effect-types.yaml` `never_used.gear_damage` is amended: a Chapter 3 row may drop a Blade Set or force a Gas Roll, both defined by Chapter 4, and Chapter 4 gains one sentence for each. The six-cell Grab test (ADR-0014 target 4) is unchanged and stays binding; because the profile per total is held by construction, the rerun is a check, and a cell that misses goes back to the decider rather than to the drafter's placement. No retune of target 4 is authorized in this batch.
- **Why:** The owner allowed gear and forced acts, and the research showed the complaint is texture, not severity: at Stress 2 WoF's table already bites harder and earlier than Alien's Panic table. Holding the action, turn, and Scar profile per total keeps every measured figure (the six cells at 33.2% named and 45.0% worst, the first-Titan-Engagement row, the Abnormal bar) within sampling, so the texture is free where it is cheap and rare (five triggers, rolled together) and the Grab rescue window is untouched. Contagion as Stress costs one sheet mark per neighbour and no roll, which is what OQ-50 (b) and OQ-97's round-time band refused rolls for. Forced strikes sit at 7 and up because a Stress 1 to 3 Rookie witness at Resolve 3 cannot reach them, so a berserk rescue cannot lower the named cell. The loudest flag from a scream reuses Draw Attention's flag and the batch 5 Distant rule, so nothing new moves Attention from beyond a Titan's reach. The gear placement rule exists because a witness who dropped blades at total 2 would lose the rescue action to Swap Blade Set, which would change the cells the placement rule promises to hold. Figures: re-measured as a check after the engine gains the six types.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0010 `## Amended` (batch 7): a Fear result may move Attention, force a move, or force a strike, and a forced strike is not a penalty for acting alone. ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 7): the re-cut is a check. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Draw Attention gains "or a Fear Roll result" and "Never from Distant". Applied. "Panic" stays avoided; row names may not use it.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.9 (the new effect types), section 3.12 *Results* (the table with its "What happens" column; the placement rules in a design note citing OQ-139); Chapter 4 sections 4.3 (a Gas Roll a Chapter 3 row forces) and 4.4 (a Blade Set a Chapter 3 row drops); Chapter 5 section 5.6 *Draw Attention* (the flag a Fear row sets) and 5.2 (a forced move outside a retreat uses the retreat's move rule); `data/mind/fear-rolls.yaml` (the rows, `text`), `data/harm/effect-types.yaml` (six types; `never_used.gear_damage` amended; `forbid_help_or_cover` now used by the forced-action row), `data/core/stress-changes.yaml` (`gains` gains `fear-contagion`, or the named-gain row cites it), `data/engagement/attention.yaml` (`loudest` gains a second `set_when`), `data/engagement/positions.yaml` (`forced_move` shared with the retreat), `data/harm/sheet-fields.yaml` (a pending forced action or move, cleared by Rally or the end of the Titan Engagement); `tools/render/render.py` (the column); `tools/sim/rules.py`, `engine.py`, `policy.py` (the six types). New entry OQ-139; OQ-50 carries a `Revised by batch 7` line.

### 7-9: the Stress Response table's names and texture (owner item 5)

- **Decision:** The Stress Response table keeps every row's total, duration, and effects. Each row gains a fiction-first name and a `text` line the render prints beside it, as the Fear rows do. Hair Trigger stays; no gear or scene row is added, because the table resolves at step 2 of *Finishing a roll* on every attribute roll in the game, including a dodge on a Titan's card.
- **Why:** The owner's complaint names the Alien Panic table, whose WoF counterpart is the Fear Roll (7-8); Alien's own Stress Response table is bland in the same way as WoF's, and a scene-changing result mid-roll stalls the card. Figures: none move; the render check covers the names.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.10 (the table with its column); `data/mind/stress-responses.yaml` (`name`, `text`); `tools/render/render.py`; the packet's Stress Responses table and the Rally paragraph that names rows. OQ-47 carries a `Revised by batch 7` line; OQ-139.

### 7-10: the canon charter (owner item 6)

- **Decision:** The charter is ADR-0016, with the owner's answers: the era lock 845 to 850 with no Marley and no door left for a Marley module; dated inventions (Thunder Spears, anti-personnel ODM, the restraining weapon) as Canon Clock Discoveries with Divergence Hooks, never Standard Issue; invented items only through Requisition or a Discovery; Titans die only at the Nape; Shifters stay Shifters; every row has a rule; Titan-fight content measured before it ships; Funding and Scarcity with no prices; small tables reviewed like chapters; glossary language; Titans before gear in Phase 2; nothing new in gear or Titans before the first playtest except the flintlock pistol, the musket, and the shot kind (7-17). One question joins the playtest debrief: what did you reach for and not have. OQ-112 gains a line that the first Phase 2 Titan batch includes an Abnormal that punishes stacking at Blind Spot as its candidate answer.
- **Why:** The owner accepted the charter as recommended and named the one exception. Recording it as an ADR gives the Phase 2 drafters and the Foundry port one place to read the boundaries.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0016 (new). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 4 section 4.9 or 4.1 (a design note citing ADR-0016 for why the list is closed and how it grows); the packet's feedback section (the debrief question). OQ-112 carries a `Revised by batch 7` line.

### 7-11: Squadmates by default, fewer allowed, and the replacement (owner Squadmates)

- **Decision:** The starting Squad rule stands: 6 minus the player characters, minimum 0, so 4 PCs start with 2 Squadmates, the ADR-0014 default. A table may start with fewer Squadmates than the rule gives, and the chapter and the packet state the two costs in one sentence: fights run about three times deadlier for player characters (0.057 against 0.022 deaths a fight on the standard Medium Titan; 0.187 against 0.058 on the Large), and a dead player character has no one to promote. The packet also says in one paragraph what Squadmates are for (faster kills, Grab rescues, decoys and screens, the replacement pipeline, named comrades) and that the easiest way to run them is on Wings. `promotion.no_squadmate` now reads: the player creates a new character by any procedure the campaign allows (the Template Build when the campaign allows it, since it takes ten minutes); the new character joins the Squad at the next Waypoint when an Expedition is under way, at the start of the next Titan Engagement when none is, or at the next Downtime, whichever comes first, with Standard Issue.
- **Why:** The owner kept the default and allowed fewer with the stated cost. The cost is the best-measured number in the project, because every reference start is four player characters alone. The replacement rule is needed by 7-12: a playtest of four player characters with no Squadmates that made a dead player wait for the next session would lose a player for the session. A Waypoint is the moment a formation regroups, and outside an Expedition the start of the next fight is the moment the interim rules already use for issue. Figures: none move.
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0014's reference Squad is unchanged.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 2 section 2.10 *The starting Squad* (the fewer-Squadmates sentence and the costs) and *Promotion* step 4; `data/character/squadmates.yaml` `starting_squad` (a `fewer_allowed` row with the costs) and `promotion.no_squadmate`; the packet's Squadmates section. OQ-31 carries a `Revised by batch 7` line.

### 7-12: lethality: A now, B after the playtest, the Death Roll (owner items 7 and 8)

- **Decision:** **A now:** the first playtest runs four player characters with no Squadmates on today's tuning, where a player character dies about every 7 typical rolled fights (0.136 deaths a fight across the interim setup mix, report section 6.9; 0.057 on the standard Medium Titan in Wooded terrain). The packet's GM section states the configuration in one line as this playtest's setup; the rules are unchanged. **B after the playtest:** logged as OQ-140, a Simulator target: retune the Titans so the default Squad of 4 PCs and 2 Squadmates loses a PC about every 8 typical rolled fights (about 0.125 PC deaths a fight on the setup mix, against 0.018 today with two helpers on the Medium), which re-sets every deaths band and bar limit when it is taken; the typical rolled fight is the interim setup mix until a Mission Brief mix exists. No retune before the playtest. The playtest debrief counts fights, Critical Injuries, and deaths per Expedition. **The Death Roll** stays as written (Strength, no Push, no Stress Dice; 48% failure at Strength 4), revisited on playtest data.
- **Why:** The owner settled item 7 as A now and B after, and item 8 as keep. B is about six times today's helpers figure and moves every band, the Abnormal bar, the Jam test, and the lone figures, so it is a decision on the full rows after the playtest count, not a batch 7 lever. ADR-0014 records it as a target in waiting so that the report can carry a row for it now.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 7): the logged target, not adopted; the reference Squad unchanged. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** none in the chapters; the packet's GM section (the configuration) and feedback section (the counts). New entry OQ-140 (open).

### 7-13: OQ-132, the Medium deaths band

- **Decision:** Option (a), provisionally: Chapter 6's Medium band reads "at most 0.06 deaths per fight, through the end of the Titan Engagement", with deaths during the fight reported beside it; the committed 0.0573 at the reference start and 0.0546 and 0.0552 in the bar's orders read Met against it. Options (b) to (d) are not taken before the playtest. The band is re-set again, with every deaths band and bar limit, under OQ-140.
- **Why:** The retreat's cost is the design's (5-10 chose the length that leaves the fewest dead), and the owner has since chosen fights that are each lethal on their own and accepted today's 4-PC rate for the playtest, so a 0.05 limit no longer states the design's intent. Re-setting it now stops the report reading Missed on a figure the owner has accepted, and it is honest about what changed: the reading, not the rows. Sides (7-4) lower deaths a little and the Bite rider (7-5) raises them a little, so the rerun may land either side of 0.05 and inside 0.06.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 7). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 section 5.13 and Chapter 6 section 6.6 (the band's wording and verdict); `data/titans/tuning.yaml` `targets.medium.at_the_reference_start.deaths_per_fight` ("at most 0.06 ..., decision batch 7, 7-13") and `verdicts.medium`; `data/engagement/tuning.yaml` `prepared_squad_kill.columns`; the report. OQ-132 is marked Decided.

### 7-14: Expeditions, the playtest minimum (owner minimal rules answers 5, 7, 8; section 6 items 2 to 6 and 15)

- **Decision:** The proposal's section 1 is adopted as written, with these settlements. The Hard Ride Leg roll is `ride` with the horse's Gear Dice, so a Pushed Ride wears the horse, and Horsemanship and Sure Seat have a home outside a Titan Engagement. The Formation Posts are as proposed: Vanguard (Spot, +1), Flank Scout (Survive, +1), Signal Relay (Spot, 0; once per Leg the Squad may spend 1 flare before the hazard roll to lower it by 2), Center (Endure, -1), Supply Wagon (Survive, 0; a supply loss row costs double), Rear Guard (Spot, 0). Rations stock by Funding is 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12; when the Squad has no rations for a Leg or a camp, every soldier gains 1 Stress with no roll, and a camp without rations gives no Camp Relief. The interim route assigns Distance Bands by position (first and last Near, second and second-to-last Far, between them Deep). The Straggler victim is rolled at random: D6, 1 to 4 a living Squadmate and 5 to 6 a player character (a player character when no Squadmate lives); then number the living soldiers of that kind in Squad sheet order and roll a die of that size, re-rolling numbers past the count, so that every soldier of that kind is equally likely and the order chooses nothing; the victim makes an Endure roll with no Help and no retry, and on a failure suffers a Bite Critical Injury at a rolled Injury Location that can be lethal, then the Titan Engagement begins with the victim at In Reach and everyone else Distant. The Leg Hazard and Night tables ship as written, because the first playtest runs without Squadmates and so every Straggler victim is a player character, and they are tuned on the playtest count. A Down soldier rides in the wagon or across a comrade's saddle and takes part in a Titan Engagement from Distant as `placement.who_takes_part` says. While an Expedition is under way the interim day and the interim issue do not happen: the night camp and the pre-Expedition issue take their place, and the night camp's day care window has the Expedition day's reading (every soldier on the Expedition; Field Repair allowed). A soldier who dies on a Leg is replaced when the Leg ends (promotion, or 7-11's replacement at the next Waypoint). The new tracked values are `leg-outcome`, `camp-outcome`, and `hazard-outcome`; `spot`, `survive`, `endure`, and `ride` lose `dormant`. The cadence for the simulator's sequence family becomes the Expedition's (OQ-116 revised): two Legs and a night camp a day, about two Titan Engagements per Expedition of 4 to 6 Legs, a day passing at each night camp, no interim issue between days.
- **Why:** The owner chose minimal rules and answered the questions; the settlements follow the proposal's own reasoning where the owner did not speak. Ride on a Hard Ride is the only place a horse's Gear Dice roll outside a Titan Engagement, which the Rider's list needs. A flat Stress gain for hunger replaces six pools for one rare row. Distance Bands by position give every interim route the same shape and let the hazard climb with the ride. A random Straggler is the owner's answer 5, and the numbered die keeps ADR-0003's rule that the sheet's order chooses nothing (batch 4). The Straggler's harm is a Bite Critical Injury rather than a fall because a Bite from the saddle is the canon image and gives Bite a second source; it is the first Titan harm not dealt by a Behavior Table entry, so ADR-0005 gains the sentence. Figures: the Expedition target of ADR-0014 gains a mechanism and no measurement; the proposal's arithmetic (about two fights per Expedition, 1.3 to 1.6 Critical Injuries, 0.06 to 0.10 PC deaths per Expedition with two Squadmates) is the estimate the playtest count checks, and OQ-140 owns the gap.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0009 `## Amended` (batch 7): the readings Chapter 7 implements. ADR-0005 `## Amended` (batch 7): the Straggler sentence. ADR-0003 `## Amended` (batch 7): item 12's new tracked values. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Pace ("a third Leg squeezed in before dark"); Waypoint (its kind sets the Anchor Rating); new **Depot**, **Lead**, **Night Camp**, **Camp Relief**. Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** new Chapter 7, section 7.1 *Expeditions* with the two hazard tables, the Posts, the Waypoint kinds, the interim route, the night camp, and rations; Chapter 3 sections 3.5 and 3.6 (the night camp's window; the interim day pauses while an Expedition runs), 3.15 (the Camp Relief row the chapter promised); Chapter 4 sections 4.9 (the pre-Expedition issue and the depot; the interim issue pauses) and 4.10 (rations' stock and uses); Chapter 5 section 5.1 (a hazard row is a rule that begins a Titan Engagement and may name Positions, an Anchor Rating, a Focus Titan, and Background clocks); new `data/expedition/legs.yaml`, `hazards.yaml`, `route.yaml` as the proposal's section 1.2 sketches them; `data/core/stress-changes.yaml` (`camp-relief` reduction; the long-hours, restless, and hunger gains as named gains); `data/gear/squad-supply.yaml` (`stock.rations`; `kinds.rations` uses); `data/character/action-catalog.yaml` (`spot`, `survive`, `endure`, `ride` live, their `rules` and `changes`; the three tracked values; `help_outside_titan_engagement` filled in); `data/harm/healing.yaml` `day_passes` (a `night-camp` row; `interim` notes the pause), `data/harm/treat-injury.yaml` `care_windows.day.scope` (the Expedition day branch is the night camp's), `data/gear/standard-issue.yaml` (`before_expedition`, `depot`; `interim_issue` notes the pause); `data/engagement/engagement-setup.yaml` (a note that a hazard row may name what the table otherwise rolls). New entry OQ-141; OQ-67, OQ-116, OQ-120, OQ-121, and OQ-130 carry `Revised by batch 7` lines.

### 7-15: Downtime, the playtest minimum (owner minimal rules answer 7; section 6 items 11 and 12)

- **Decision:** When the Squad rides through the gate, Downtime begins and lasts seven days. The infirmary: one care window with every soldier in the Squad in scope, then for each of the seven days every still-untreated day-limit lethal Critical Injury gets one infirmary roll before its Death Roll, four base dice, needs 1, no Push, no Help, no Stress Dice, a success stabilizing it; Health lost comes back and seven days of healing pass. Each player character takes one Downtime Action: Recover (lose 2 Stress), Visit Haven (lose 2 Stress and 2 Grief), Requisition (7-16), or Maintain Gear (every item of the soldier's returns to its rating; a lame horse recovers). Squadmates take none and each loses 2 Stress and 1 Grief. The Squad takes one Squad Action: Honoring the Fallen (every soldier loses 1 Grief) or Recruit (new Squadmates from the templates until the Squad has 6 minus the player characters). Train, Investigate, Contribute to Research, Lobby, HQ Upgrades, XP, Commendations, and Faction Standing wait for Phase 2. OQ-52's relief target (at least 2 Grief per Downtime for a soldier who uses the relief) is met by Visit Haven. Stress never falls below the soldier's minimum.
- **Why:** The owner chose seven fixed days. The amounts are the proposal's, checked against OQ-52; the infirmary roll gives a day-limit injury inside the Walls a rule where none existed. A stopgap that Phase 2's Downtime must replace without changing the healing count, which counts days.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** Downtime ("Seven days, until the full Downtime rules are written"); Downtime Action (the four now, the three later). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 7 section 7.2 *Downtime*; new `data/campaign/downtime.yaml` as the proposal's sketch; `data/core/stress-changes.yaml` `reductions.downtime` (amount set: 2 for Recover and Visit Haven; Squadmates 2); `data/mind/grief.yaml` (Visit Haven 2, Honoring the Fallen 1, Squadmates 1); `data/harm/healing.yaml` (the seven days; the infirmary roll cited), `data/harm/death-rolls.yaml` (the infirmary roll before a day-limit Death Roll in Downtime), `data/gear/horses.yaml` and `items.yaml` (Maintain Gear); Chapter 3 sections 3.4 and 3.6 (pointers); Chapter 4 section 4.1 *Restoring* (Maintain Gear). OQ-141.

### 7-16: Requisition, the playtest minimum (owner minimal rules answers 3 and 4; section 6 item 10)

- **Decision:** The proposal's section 2 is adopted with one change: the "before a campaign's first Expedition" sentence is dropped, so Requisition is a Downtime Action taken once per player character per Downtime, and the first playtest runs on the reference kit with Requisition starting at the first Downtime. The roller chooses Persuade (Empathy) or Recall (Wits) before rolling; the needs are the row's Scarcity (Standard 1, Limited 2, Rare 3) plus the ledger (+1 for every Requisition Command has granted the Squad this Downtime); Funding gates the tiers (1 or 2 Standard; 3 or 4 Standard and Limited; 5 or 6 all three); up to three comrades may Help, a player character spending their own Requisition and a Squadmate once per Downtime; Push and Cover as Chapter 1; no retry this Downtime; failure means no. Size Up (Instinct, no Help, no retry) once per Downtime before any Requisition roll lowers the ledger by 1 for the Squad. A granted item arrives before the next Standard Issue, counts as items do, is passed, taken, and shared out like its kind, and is a kept item Standard Issue never takes away; a Blade Set above rating 1 is still ruined by any wear. The playtest list is the proposal's 14 rows (spare canister, Blade Set 1, medical kit 1, tool kit 1, 2 flares or 2 rations or 1 medical supplies unit, 6 shot: Standard; Blade Set 2, medical kit 2, tool kit 2, flintlock pistol 1, musket 1: Limited; ODM Gear 3, horse 3, Blade Set 3: Rare), every row canon, none locked. Phase 2 rows carry a canon label and a Discovery gate as the charter states. The ledger and Size Up stay. `persuade`, `recall`, and `size-up` lose `dormant`; the tracked values are `requisition-grant` and `ledger-lower`.
- **Why:** The owner said no Requisition before the first Expedition and accepted the pistol and musket as the one exception to the charter. Two entries for one roll is deliberate: both Talents get a home and no Specialty owns Requisition; the ledger and the Funding gate are the brake. Figures: no target moves; a soldier who later Requisitions ODM Gear 3 or a Blade Set 2 plays above the reference Rookie, which the Veteran build already assumes, and the Jam, gas, and fragility figures for those ratings are on record (section 4.9's table, OQ-63).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0016 item 8 states the rule's principle. No other ADR.
- **Glossary change:** Requisition (the Funding gate, no prices); new **Ledger**, **Kept Item**. Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 7 section 7.3 *Requisition* with the list; Chapter 4 section 4.9 *Receiving it* (the decline step cites kept items) and 4.12 (the Blade Set rating field); new `data/campaign/requisition.yaml` as the proposal's sketch; `data/gear/items.yaml` (`kept_item`), `data/gear/sheet-fields.yaml` (`blade_sets_carried` ratings; `kept_item`); `data/character/action-catalog.yaml` (`persuade`, `recall`, `size-up` live; the two tracked values). New entry OQ-142.

### 7-17: Skirmishes, the playtest minimum (owner minimal rules answers 1, 2, 4, 6, 9; section 6 items 7 to 9 and 13)

- **Decision:** The proposal's section 3 is adopted as written under ADR-0018, with these settlements. The foe table is as proposed: Bandit (Attack 4, Guard 3, club Crush 1 or knife Cut 1, Health 3, Grit 1, Watch 1, groups of 3), Military Police trooper (5, 4, sabre Cut 2 or flintlock pistol Pierce 2, Health 4, Grit 2, Watch 2, groups of 2 to 4), Garrison sentry (4, 3, musket Pierce 3, Health 4, Grit 1, Watch 2, groups of 2), the sentry kept as the musket's source. Weapons: Blade Set (Fight, Cut, damage 2), bare hands or any object that is not a gear item (Fight, Crush, damage 1), flintlock pistol (Shoot, Pierce, damage 2, one shot, rated 1, 1 item, any range), musket (Shoot, Pierce, damage 3, one shot, rated 1, 2 items, Apart only, Reload spends the move too); firearms give no Gear Dice and have no use in a Titan Engagement. The musket's reading: damage 3 leaves a Health 4 Rookie one box from Down, and one extra success puts them Down with a Pierce Critical Injury; that is what "a landed musket hit puts a Rookie Down" means, and the owner's "guns as lethal as Titan fights" is met by the shot's Severity being its successes. Bare hands never kill: Crush damage puts a Foe at 0 Health out cold; only Cut or Pierce kills. A Skirmish's end gives the 1 Stress relief as a `skirmish-ends` reduction row, mirroring `engagement-ends`, and runs the engagement-end steps with "Skirmish" in place of "Titan Engagement". `first-human-kill` joins the closed Fear Roll trigger list: the soldier who kills a person for the first time rolls when the kill resolves, recorded as `killed_a_person` like `faced_a_titan`, and a Drive may shrug it off; `comrade-dies` in a Skirmish is rolled by every soldier in it who is not Down. Talent readings: Hand-to-Hand's live entry reads "adds its dice to Fight and Block, with a Blade Set, bare-handed, or as a Grapple"; Blade Discipline also names `fight`; Carrying Voice's Position step reads as any soldier in the Skirmish; Pry Loose stays Titan-only (a Grabbed comrade); Wide Awareness and Shoulder the Load change nothing in a Skirmish, since it has no Positions; Slip Away, Horsemanship's mounted dodge, Steady Voice, and Field Medicine are live in a Skirmish as their entries allow. The Catalog: `fight` and `block` lose `reserved`; new `shoot` (action, Agility, firearms, `requires_gear: true`) and `reload` (an option, no roll); `sneak` loses `dormant`; `break-free`'s requirements add "or Held in a Skirmish"; `dodge` may be made against a shot; the tracked values `foe-harm`, `held-set`, `held-end`, `firearm-load`, `foe-yield`, `foe-intent-reveal`, and `ambush-set`; `help_outside_titan_engagement` filled in on every entry a Skirmish uses (Help spends the helper's action and needs no range). Bonus Dice: an `ambush` row (2 dice, an attack on a Foe that has not acted, while the Squad has the ambush). The shot kind of Squad Supply is stocked 0, 0, 6, 6, 8, 10 by Funding.
- **Why:** The owner put human conflict in scope with these foes, guns as lethal as Titan fights, a first-kill Fear Roll, and bare hands that never kill, and accepted the two firearms and the shot kind as the charter's one exception. The needs-equals-successes Reaction reuses ADR-0015's shape and Chapter 1's Reaction rule, so no opposed roll enters the game; damage in front of the tables is what lets a Skirmish end in flight or surrender; Grit is a one-number dial. Figures: no Titan target moves; the proposal's arithmetic (a Rookie's cut lands on a trooper about 1 time in 2, a trooper's sabre about 1 in 4 for 2 to 3 damage) is a starting value the Skirmish probe reports.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0018 (new); ADR-0003 `## Amended` (batch 7): item 1's list gains the trigger, item 8 covers the foe rule, item 12 the tracked values; ADR-0015 `## Amended` (batch 7): a person can be blocked. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Military Police ("and the commonest human Foe"); Reaction (a Block against a person); Squad Supply (shot); a new **Skirmish** section with Skirmish, Foe, Engaged, Apart, Held, Grit, Watch, Ambush, Parley, and Shot, each with its Avoid list. Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 7 section 7.4 *Skirmishes* with the foe table; Chapter 1 section 1.9 (a Block is possible against an enemy whose rules allow it: Chapter 7's Foes); Chapter 2 section 2.8 and Appendix 2A.3 to 2A.5 (the Catalog rows, `shoot`, `reload`, the tracked values); Chapter 3 section 3.12 (the trigger), 3.16 (the Skirmish end pointer); Chapter 4 sections 4.1 (the two firearms as gear items, with no Gear Dice in a Titan Engagement) and 4.10 (shot); new `data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml` and `foes.yaml` as the proposal's sketch; `data/character/action-catalog.yaml`, `data/character/talents.yaml` (the readings above), `data/gear/items.yaml` (`flintlock-pistol`, `musket`), `data/gear/squad-supply.yaml` (`shot`), `data/gear/carrying.yaml` (`items_counted`), `data/core/bonus-dice-sources.yaml` (`ambush`), `data/core/stress-changes.yaml` (`skirmish-ends`), `data/mind/fear-rolls.yaml` (`first-human-kill`; `comrade-dies` in a Skirmish), `data/harm/health.yaml` (damage's `injury_type`), `data/harm/sheet-fields.yaml` (`held`, `killed_a_person`), `data/harm/engagement-end.yaml` (`procedure: skirmish`). New entry OQ-143. The Skirmish probe is a new simulator case, reported and not tuned.

### 7-18: numbering and placement (section 6 items 1 and 14)

- **Decision:** ADR-0016 *Content beyond canon follows the charter* (the canon charter, with the Talent guardrails as its second section), ADR-0017 *Critical Injuries carry a sided Injury Location and an Injury Type*, ADR-0018 *Humans roll; Titans do not*. The three minimal subsystems form one chapter, `docs/rules/07-playtest-rules.md` (*Chapter 7: Playtest rules: Expeditions, Downtime, Requisition, and Skirmishes*), with sections 7.1 Expeditions, 7.2 Downtime, 7.3 Requisition, 7.4 Skirmishes, 7.5 Who uses these rules, and one example, and its YAML in `data/expedition/`, `data/campaign/`, and `data/skirmish/`. Every paragraph of it that Phase 2 replaces carries the playtest tag. Pointer sentences go into Chapter 1 section 1.9, Chapter 3 sections 3.5, 3.6, 3.15, and 3.16, Chapter 4 sections 4.1, 4.9, and 4.10, and Chapter 5 section 5.1. The packet gains one section, *Playtest rules: Expeditions, Requisition, Skirmishes*, after Gear and before the GM section, about four to five packet pages, with the two hazard tables, the Requisition list, and the foe table as its only tables. PROGRESS.md gains a Chapter 7 row (Drafting) and the Chapter scope list gains its line.
- **Why:** One chapter, because the three subsystems share the Catalog changes, the Downtime that Requisition lives in, and the Prowlers row that joins the night to the Skirmish; three chapters would triple the pointer sentences. The charter and the guardrails merge because both are one test for what may be added.
- **ADR change:** the three new ADRs. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** the new chapter and the pointers above; `docs/rules/PROGRESS.md`.

### Chapter impacts of batch 7

**Chapter 1:** section 1.9 (a Block against a person, 7-17).

## Batch 8: Titan attack resolution, Burn, the falling Titan, prosthetics, and the drafters' queue

Rulings on the owner's decisions of 2026-09-15 recorded in `docs/playtest/feedback/round-1/OWNER-DECISIONS.md` under *Titan attack resolution* and *Batch 7 "Needs owner" answers* (binding), on the research in `opposed-rolls.md` (Option C, rounds 1 to 3) and `critical-injuries.md` (step 2), on the Titan World 3rd Edition rules for steam, burns, and falling Titans that the owner cites as the model, and on the seventeen drafter questions in `DECIDER-QUEUE.md`. Made 2026-09-15 by the Fable decider. Items 8-16 to 8-19, added the same day, settle the round 2 queue (`DECIDER-QUEUE.md` items 18 to 25, raised by WP-D, WP-S1 part 3, and WP-R while applying this batch). Items 8-20 to 8-30, added the same day, settle the round 3 queue: the six readings WP-C2 logged as OQ-156 to OQ-161 while drafting the falling Titan, the corpse, the lost-limb grades, and the Burn rider; `DECIDER-QUEUE.md` item 26 from WP-B; and WP-F's four provisional entries OQ-152 to OQ-155 with the coordinator's three open points on them (a Foe that Grapples, Downtime Actions in the Catalog, Burn on a Foe); OQ-162 indexes the round. Items 8-31 to 8-33, added the same day after the full rerun (WP-S2), record the owner's decisions under *After the full rerun* in `OWNER-DECISIONS.md` (the Medium deaths band, and the Skirmish retune with the values of the simulator agent's sweep) and settle the run-on loop the rerun found at its safety cap; OQ-163 and OQ-164 index them. Item 8-34, added the same day, settles WP-T's provisional reading on a soldier who cannot move past the retreat's stay limit; OQ-165 indexes it. Items 8-35 to 8-42, added the same day, record the owner's choices on the eight decisions of the round 1 final review (`REVIEW-FIX-PLAN.md` section 2, D1 to D8, each taken as recommended, with simulator option (b); `OWNER-DECISIONS.md`, *After the full rerun*), and an erratum line under 8-3 records the review's R43; OQ-166 indexes them. Where an owner decision contradicts batch 7 or an ADR, the item says so and the ADR is amended below. WP-0, WP-C, WP-A, and WP-S1 parts 1 and 2a are applied on the tree this batch reads; nothing in the chapters, the YAML, the tools, or the packet changes by this batch itself. `IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md` is revised for the new scope.

Measurement: 8-1 to 8-4 re-shape every Titan attack and every human attack, so every target, band, bar limit, and reported row of ADR-0014 is re-measured under Attack Dice, as a check against the matched figures of the research (round 3: the Medium band 0.050 during and 0.058 through the end, the first-Titan-Engagement row 0.082, the setup mix 0.076, the Grab cells within sampling, the Large 0.156 against 0.185). 8-7 to 8-9 add Burn and the falling Titan, measured against the Critical Injuries bands with the expected direction stated (8-14). 8-10 to 8-13 move no figure. Every figure quoted below is the research files' and the committed report's; no new probe was run for this batch.

One new ADR: ADR-0019 *Titan attacks are rolled, and the Reaction cancels*, which supersedes ADR-0015 in full. Six ADRs are amended: ADR-0001 (the table picks, the entry rolls), ADR-0003 (items 2, 8, and 11), ADR-0005 (the rider; hazard harm), ADR-0014 (Attack Dice, the checks, the Health 2 row, the Critical Injuries bands with steam), ADR-0017 (Burn's sources and rider; prosthetics; the Cut and Pierce riders, added in 8-15 after the owner overrode 8-8's default), and ADR-0018 (humans share the shape; the title is history).
