### 8-1: Titan attacks roll Attack Dice, and the Reaction cancels (owner, Option C)

- **Decision:** Every Behavior Table entry whose effects include stress, knock-loose, critical-injury, or grab carries **Attack Dice** in place of Severity: 3 dice for a former Severity 1 entry, 6 for 2, 9 for 3, and 12 for 4, so the Small table rolls 3, 6, 6 by tier, the Medium 3, 6, 9, the Large 6, 9, 12, Thrash the control pool, and the Sprinting Abnormal's Grab and Headlong Lunge 6 and its Pitch Headlong 3 (`size-classes.yaml` `attack_dice`; each Abnormal stat block lists its own). A telegraph-only entry rolls nothing. When a Focus Titan's card resolves a behavior (Chapter 5, section 5.5, step 5), the GM rolls the entry's Attack Dice in the open as **Titan Dice**, a die kind that succeeds on 5 or 6, never Pushes, and never takes Bonus Dice, Help, Stress Dice, or Gear Dice; the successes are the card's **Severity**, announced with the behavior and its targets. Every target of the card compares against the same roll. Each target may make their Reaction, the dodge, as Chapter 1 gives it, knowing the number: each dodge success cancels one Titan success, and the card lands on that target if 1 or more **net successes** remain; it whiffs on 0 net. A roll of 0 Titan successes is a whiff against every target, including a soldier who cannot react (Down, Grabbed, the Unguarded row and the other no-Reactions rows): against such a soldier the card lands on 1 or more Titan successes, and its net successes are the Titan's. No dodge is rolled against a whiff, so the soldier keeps their Reaction for a later card. One Reaction per Titan per round stands (OQ-14): the dodge is rolled against the first card of that Titan the soldier chooses to answer, and its successes cancel against each later card of that Titan this round separately, never used up (Chapter 1, section 1.4, item 4). The dodge stays Pushable, with Help, Covering, and Call It's 2 Bonus Dice as today, and it spends the turn Chapter 1 names; the Grab's turn-back rule stands. Effects on a landed card: stress adds 1 Stress, once, whatever the net; telegraph reveals the new Next Behavior whether the card landed or whiffed, since it is information; knock-loose is the fall as written; critical-injury takes the rider of 8-2; grab lands Grabbed with today's crush and no rider. A whiffed card still resolves a behavior: it becomes the previous behavior, the flags clear, and the Next Behavior is re-rolled (ADR-0003 item 2). A Read that reveals the Next Behavior reveals its Attack Dice, which the public stat block prints in any case. The Grab countdown, the lift, and the devour are unchanged and always land (the owner's fixed input; the research measured a devour whiff and it is not taken). Hazard-named Titan harm, the Straggler's Bite (7-14), has no Attack Dice: its Endure roll is its whole test. The pools are keyed to the former Severity values, not to tiers, so a Large Titan's control tier rolls 9 (the research measured tier-keyed pools at half the Large's deaths).
- **Why:** The owner chose Option C after three research rounds and fixed its inputs: Titan Dice on 5 or 6, pools of 3, 6, 9, and 12, the rider, the devour always landing, the whiff against anyone. The research measured this shape at today's Health reproducing every row the targets read (Medium 0.050 and 0.058 against 0.054 and 0.061; the first-Titan-Engagement row 0.082 against 0.083; the setup mix 0.076 against 0.083; every Grab cell within sampling; the Large 0.156 against 0.185, the one row that reads lower) with the narrowest spread per hit of any finalist. Severity stays as the name of the number a Reaction must cancel, because it is the sentence ADR-0018 already uses and fourteen packet lines read it; the pool gets its own term, Attack Dice, so that a stat block and a rolled number are never confused. The 5-or-6 die keeps pools inside Alien's range and needs one differently coloured die, which the packet's GM tables print. The whiff against a soldier who cannot react costs nothing measurable (every tier within 2 standard errors) and is the owner's wish. Figures: every target, band, bar limit, and reported row is re-measured as a check (8-14).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0019 (new), superseding ADR-0015 in full; ADR-0001 `## Amended` (batch 8: the table picks, the entry rolls, the Titan chooses nothing); ADR-0003 `## Amended` (batch 8: item 2, a whiffed card resolves a behavior; item 8, the Titan's roll is open and the GM re-rolls nothing; item 11, Attack Dice in place of Severity). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Severity re-read ("the successes a Titan's or a Foe's attack roll scored, which a Reaction must cancel"); new **Attack Dice**, **Titan Dice**, **Net Successes**; Reaction (cancels one for one; no Reaction against a whiff); Behavior Table (its entries carry Attack Dice); Next Behavior (a Read reveals its Attack Dice). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 section 1.9 *Reactions* (the cancellation procedure, 8-5; "Against a Titan" rewritten; the Grab turn-back sentence kept); Chapter 2 section 2.8 and Appendix 2A.3 (the dodge entry's needs text); Chapter 3 section 3.1 *Titan attacks bypass Health* (lands on 1 or more net successes; a whiff) and 3.7 (the Grab model's dodge reads Attack Dice); Chapter 4 sections 4.2 and 4.3 (the Jam design notes that cite "Severity 3" read "the kill pool of 9"); Chapter 5 section 5.4 *Stat blocks* and *Behavior Tables* (the Attack Dice column, the Titan Dice legend), 5.5 *Resolving a card* (step 5 becomes *Roll and announce*; step 6 *Reactions* cancels; a whiff) and *Dodging a Titan*, 5.8 *Read* (Attack Dice revealed), 5.9 (a Grab lands on net 1 or more, no rider), 5.13 (the verdict sentences); Chapter 6 every stat block (the column), every design note that cites Severity, section 6.6; `data/engagement/size-classes.yaml` (`severity` becomes `attack_dice`, `measured` follows), `titan-format.yaml` (`tier_rules`, the column, `titan_dice`), `behavior-procedure.yaml` (steps 5 and 6), `grab.yaml` (lands on net 1 or more; the crush takes no rider), `read.yaml` (facts), `data/titans/standard-small.yaml`, `standard-medium.yaml`, `standard-large.yaml`, `sprinting-abnormal.yaml` (the column), `data/engagement/tuning.yaml` and `data/titans/tuning.yaml` (`medium_severity` rows become dice rows; the dodge policy reads "dodge any card with 1 or more successes whose effects harm, Push when short"; every verdict sentence), `data/titans/probe-figures.yaml` (every committed figure is stale and re-measured); `tools/render/render.py` and `tools/probes/chapter-06/render.py` (the column); `tools/sim/` (WP-S1 part 3). New entry OQ-145; OQ-14, OQ-80, and OQ-96 carry `Revised by batch 8` lines.

### 8-2: the Critical Injury rider, and ADR-0005 kept (owner)

- **Decision:** When a Titan attack with a critical-injury effect lands, the Critical Injury roll is 2D6 plus worsening plus 1 for each net success beyond the first (`gaining`, a `net_success_rider` step after worsening). The entry's cannot-be-lethal flag still caps the row at the table's `non_lethal_cap`, and the instant-death rows still kill when reached. The rider applies against a soldier who cannot react, counting every Titan success beyond the first. A Grab's crush, a knock loose, and a Stress gain take no rider. The devour takes none, since it rolls nothing. Critical Injuries stay direct and skip Health: ADR-0005 is kept as amended, and Titan attacks cause no Health loss of their own.
- **Why:** The owner fixed the rider and kept Critical Injuries direct; Option D and the hybrid were rejected on the round 3 measurements (D overshoots at Health 4 and needs Health 6 to come back; both spread harm four to six times as widely as C). The rider is the owner's wanted spike: a 5-success Bite against a 2-success dodge leaves 3 net successes and rolls 2D6+2 on the torso table, where the lethal rows begin (corrected in 8-19; the first record said 2D6+3, which counted the first net success), and an instant-death total is reachable through the rider on a kill entry. No rider on the Grab, because with it the lone Grab drifts to 73 to 81% and the worst rescue cell crosses 50% (research Table 3), while without it every cell sits within sampling. Figures: included in every re-measured row (8-14).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0005 `## Amended` (batch 8). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Critical Injury (the rider sentence). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.2 *Gaining a Critical Injury* (the rider step, after worsening, with the example of the spike); `data/harm/critical-injuries.yaml` `gaining` (the step; `worsening` unchanged). OQ-145.

### 8-3: Health unchanged, and the Free Build's Health 2 (owner; queue item 16)

- **Decision:** Health stays half of Strength plus Agility, rounded up (Rookie 4). The Free Build's 4, 4, 3, 3, 2, 2 array may place its 2s on Strength and Agility, giving Health 2 and Resolve up to 4; the chapter's "2 to 4" stands and 7-1's "Health 4 or 3" is amended to "Health 2 to 4". The chapter calls a Health 2 build fragile in one sentence and gives the measured reason (0.076 deaths a fight with helpers against 0.018 at Health 4). The simulator reports a Free Build Squad of that array with Strength 2 and Agility 2 (a Tactician or Leader shape, key attribute Wits or Empathy at 4) beside the Health-dependent targets, not tuned, next to the existing Health 2 row.
- **Why:** The owner allowed it after seeing the figure. Health 3 doubles deaths under every shape and Health 6 exists only to make Option D fit, so the formula stands. Figures: a reported row.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 8: the Free Build Health 2 row). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none (Free Build's entry already reads "each attribute 2 to 4").
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 2 section 2.11 (the fragile sentence and its figure); `data/character/attributes.yaml` `creation.built` (a note that Health may be 2). OQ-135 carries a `Revised by batch 8` line.
- **Erratum (round 1 final review, R43):** the parenthesis "a Tactician or Leader shape, key attribute Wits or Empathy at 4" does not describe the row the simulator runs. That row is four Free Build Medics at Health 2 (key attribute Wits at 4, Empathy the second 4, so Resolve 4; `data/character/attributes.yaml` `reported_squad_health_2`) with two helper Squadmates on the standard Medium Titan (`tools/sim/cases.py`). No figure, value, or rule changes.

### 8-4: humans share the shape; the musket at 4 (owner; amends 7-17 and ADR-0018)

- **Decision:** A human attack is resolved by the same cancellation: the attacker rolls (a soldier a full pool, a Foe its Attack dice as base dice), the attack's successes are its Severity, the target's Reaction (Block with a Blade Set or Dodge with ODM Gear or the horse, one per Foe per round, spending a turn as Chapter 1 gives) cancels one success per success, and the attack lands on 1 or more net successes. Damage is the weapon's base plus 1 per net success beyond the first, so a partial Reaction cuts damage. A Foe's Guard dice are its cancelling roll against each attack on it, rolled as base dice, never Pushed, and spending nothing. The ambush denies the Reaction, so its net successes are the attack's. Weapon damage: musket 4 (7-17's 3 is amended), flintlock pistol 2, Blade Set 2, sabre 2, knife 1, club 1, bare hands or any object that is not a gear item 1; 8-7 adds the firebrand and the fired flare at Burn 1. A landed musket ball at net 1 deals 4, which puts a Health 4 Rookie at 0: Down, with the immediate Pierce Critical Injury. Bare hands never kill, as ADR-0018 keeps.
- **Why:** The owner chose one shape for Titans and people and answered the musket question at 4: "a landed musket hit puts a Rookie Down" now means what it says, on net 1, rather than one box short as 7-17 read it. The partial-defence reading is the Alien and Coriolis damage rule, which the research recommended. ADR-0018's needs-equals-successes sentence was the same landing condition, so no Skirmish figure changes except through the musket and the extras; the Skirmish probe reports both. Figures: the Skirmish probe is reported, not tuned.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0018 `## Amended` (batch 8: the first sentence and the Considered Options paragraph superseded; the rest stands). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Reaction (a Block or Dodge cancels); Skirmish ("hurt with damage that grows with net successes"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 section 1.9 (the shared procedure, 8-5); Chapter 7 section 7.4 (WP-F drafts it under this item directly: the attack, the Reaction, damage plus extras, Guard); `data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml` (the resolution block), `foes.yaml` (Guard as the cancelling roll), `data/gear/items.yaml` (`musket` damage 4). OQ-143 carries a `Revised by batch 8` line.

### 8-5: Chapter 1: item 5 goes, and the cancellation procedure is defined once (owner)

- **Decision:** Section 1.1 item 5 ("No opposed rolls") is deleted and the list ends at item 4. Section 1.9 *Reactions* gains one procedure, *Attack and Reaction*, used by every attack a Reaction answers, Titan or human: (1) the acting side rolls and finishes its roll first, Push included if it may Push (a soldier attacking a person may; a Titan and a Foe never do); its successes are the attack's Severity, announced; (2) each target decides whether to make a Reaction, knowing the number, and rolls it as an attribute roll; the reacting side Pushes last, and a Reaction is never re-rolled after the attacker's roll is known except by its own Push; (3) each Reaction success cancels one attack success; (4) the attack lands on 1 or more net successes and its effect reads the net; on 0 it whiffs; (5) a target who cannot make a Reaction cancels nothing. Section 1.4 item 4 (comparing the same successes with a later roll does not use them up) stands and is cited for the one-Reaction-per-round rule. Chapter 5 section 5.5 and Chapter 7 section 7.4 cite the procedure and add only what is theirs (the Titan Dice face, the one-per-Titan rule, Guard).
- **Why:** The research showed item 5 was a scope note from the Chapter 1 round 1 review, not a principle, and keeping a sentence that has to be explained is worse than a five-step procedure. The Push order follows both parent games (the attacker's roll is final before the defence is declared) with the one change the owner accepted, that the target sees the number before deciding, which costs nothing because Titans and Foes never Push. Figures: none move.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0018 (Considered Options, the "Chapter 1 forbids opposed rolls" reason withdrawn). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none beyond 8-1.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 sections 1.1 and 1.9; the example if it dodges. OQ-145.

### 8-6: the ADRs (research section 6)

- **Decision:** ADR-0019 *Titan attacks are rolled, and the Reaction cancels* is the new decision record. It supersedes ADR-0015 in full and carries the sentences of ADR-0015 that survive: one Reaction per Titan per round, Titans cannot be blocked, the Grab counts down on the victim's turns, the holding arm's Toughness is 1, the crush cannot be lethal, the mounted dodge uses the horse. ADR-0015 gains a `## Superseded` section pointing to ADR-0019 and is not edited otherwise, so its history stays readable; nothing cites ADR-0015 for a live rule after WP-R, and every `adrs:` list that names it adds ADR-0019. ADR-0018 keeps its file name and title, *Humans roll; Titans do not*, as history, with the amendment saying so; its content on typed damage, Health in front of the tables, closed foe rules, and bare hands stands. ADR-0014's body reads Attack Dice where it read Severity, with the amendment recording why. ADR-0001 gains one paragraph. ADR-0003 items 2, 8, and 11 are edited in the list, as items 9 and 2 were before, with the amendment recording it.
- **Why:** The existing pattern appends amendments and never renames a file; a superseded ADR is new here, and a section rather than a deletion keeps the review history that cites ADR-0015 readable. Superseding rather than amending ADR-0015 heavily, because its first sentence ("Titan behaviors do not roll attack dice") is the whole of what changed, and a record that says the opposite of its title is worse than a new record.
- **ADR change:** as listed. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** every `adrs:` list in `data/` that names ADR-0015 (WP-R adds ADR-0019 beside it); chapter sentences that cite "ADR-0015" for the attack shape cite ADR-0019.

### 8-7: Burn's sources ship now (owner; reverses 7-7; decides OQ-138)

- **Decision:** Burn has sources before the first playtest, every one data-shaped with no Reaction and no GM choice:
  - **Steam at the kill.** A new step in `titan_death`, after the Nape-kill Stress relief and after a Grabbed soldier is freed: every soldier holding On Body or Blind Spot relative to the dying Titan rolls D6 on the steam table (`titan-harm.yaml` `steam`): 1 to 3 nothing, 4 or 5 1 damage, 6 2 damage; Injury Type Burn; the Injury Location of a Critical Injury it inflicts at 0 current Health is rolled. It is damage in Chapter 3's sense, so it reaches the tables only at 0 Health. A soldier at In Reach or Distant takes nothing.
  - **Steam at a Regeneration fill.** When a Titan's Regeneration clock fills and a Body Part recovers a state, every soldier holding On Body relative to it rolls on the same steam table. A fill that recovers nothing releases nothing.
  - **Corpse heat.** A soldier Pinned under a dead Titan (8-9) gains one Burn Critical Injury at the start of each of their turns, beginning with their first turn after the fall, at the pinned Injury Location (the pinned limb, or the torso when the body is pinned), which can be lethal and takes worsening as any Critical Injury does. A soldier who makes a Heave against a corpse takes 1 Burn damage after the roll, whatever its result; the Critical Injury it inflicts at 0 Health lands at an arm, side rolled. A Pinned soldier under a living grounded Titan takes no heat.
  - **Fire in a Skirmish.** Bandits at the Night table's Prowlers row, and any Bandit group met at night, carry a firebrand: Fight, Burn, damage 1, in place of the club. A soldier may fire a flare at a person: Shoot with no Gear Dice, Burn, damage 1, Engaged or Apart, spending 1 flare from Squad Supply and no shot; the flare is a signal first, and the packet says so. No burning terrain ships now: a fire that spreads is logged as OQ-149 for Phase 2 with the Urban Waypoint.
  - `types.burn.named_by` lists these four. Steam and corpse heat are Titan harm not dealt by a Behavior Table entry, as the Straggler's Bite is, and ADR-0005 names them.
- **Why:** The owner's words: "steam and corpse heat must come right now as well as anything else that should deal burn damage". Titan World has no steam rule at the kill but the owner cites its *Titan Bodies* rule for corpse heat (a burn each time a player acts while pinned; rescuers burned lifting) and its four injury types for Burn, and both are kept in WoF's shapes: steam as damage because a Critical Injury at every kill would add about 0.85 Critical Injuries a fight and break the Medium band (the research's warning), corpse heat as the Critical Injury source because it is rare and rescued. The Regeneration steam is the second canon image (a regenerating Titan smokes) and falls only on soldiers who stay On Body through a fill, which the baseline policy seldom does; it is the first lever to remove if the band misses (8-14). The firebrand and the fired flare give Burn a Skirmish source without a new gear item: a flare is Squad Supply and a firebrand is a Foe's weapon, inside ADR-0016's exception as read for the two firearms. Figures: measured against the Critical Injuries bands with the expected direction in 8-14.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0017 `## Amended` (batch 8: Burn's sources); ADR-0005 `## Amended` (batch 8: steam and corpse heat named). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Injury Type (Burn's sources named); new **Steam** and **Corpse Heat**. Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 section 5.7 *A Titan's death* (the steam step), *Regeneration* (the steam sentence), and the new *Pinned* subsections of 8-9; Chapter 3 section 3.1 (steam and corpse heat named as Titan harm outside the Behavior Tables); Chapter 7 section 7.4 (the firebrand and the fired flare; WP-F); `data/engagement/titan-harm.yaml` (`steam` table and its two triggers; `titan_death` step), `data/harm/critical-injuries.yaml` (`types.burn.named_by`), `data/harm/health.yaml` (`harm_kinds.damage.named_by` gains steam and the Heave), `data/skirmish/foes.yaml` (the firebrand), `data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml` (the fired flare), `data/gear/squad-supply.yaml` (`kinds.flares` gains the use). OQ-138 is decided; new entry OQ-149 (open).

### 8-8: the Burn rider; no Cut or Pierce rider; the Burn names in the packet (owner; research step 2)

- **Decision:** Every location table's `type_riders.burn` carries three riders: every row's `healing_days` is doubled; every lethal row's `time_limit` becomes `day` (a Burn kills by infection, not blood loss; a row already at day is unchanged); and Treat Injury on a Burn Critical Injury needs a medical kit or one unit of medical supplies, so the "without one, Wits alone" reading of `treat-injury.yaml` does not apply to Burn and the roll is not made without either. No Stress and no Scar are added by the rider (7-5's no-mental-rows rule). Cut and Pierce carry no rider: the types differ by name and source, and their lethality lives in damage (ADR-0018). The packet prints the Burn names in every Critical Injury table, and 7-7's omission ends.
- **Why:** Titan World's Burn is stacking, bleeding, and slow certain death for the chest and head; WoF's worsening already stacks, and the day limit is the slow death, which also keeps Burn out of the fight-end Death Rolls the Medium band counts and puts it where the sequence cases and the playtest's Expedition count see it. The medical kit rule is the one cost that makes Burn feel different at the table. Cut and Pierce riders would touch only Skirmish figures, which are unmeasured, and the Bite rider already gives the severing image; deciding "none" now is cleaner than a second deferral. Figures: the day limit lowers deaths through the end of the Titan Engagement from Burn and raises the day's Death Rolls; reported in the sequence cases.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0017 `## Amended` (batch 8: the Burn rider; no Cut or Pierce rider). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.2 *Reading a row* (the Burn rider), 3.5 *Uses* (a Burn needs a kit or supplies); `data/harm/critical-injuries.yaml` (`type_riders.burn` on all four tables; the rider fields `healing_days_multiplier`, `time_limit`, `treat_injury: requires_kit_or_supplies`), `data/harm/treat-injury.yaml` (`gear_requirement` reads the rider); the packet's Critical Injury tables (WP-P). OQ-138; OQ-137 carries a `Revised by batch 8` line.

### 8-9: the falling Titan, Pinned, Heave, and the corpse (owner; Titan World *Titan Bodies*)

- **Decision:** A `falling_titan` block in `titan-harm.yaml`, cited by Chapter 5:
  - **When.** A Focus Titan's body comes down when it dies (a new step of `titan_death`, after the steam) and when it becomes grounded (`grounded`, `when`). A Titan already grounded when it dies falls no second time; the soldiers it pinned stay Pinned and corpse heat begins.
  - **The path.** Every soldier holding On Body or Blind Spot relative to the Titan is in its path unless airborne (Chapter 4). An airborne soldier swings clear with no roll. In Reach and Distant are outside the path.
  - **Leap Clear.** Each soldier in the path who is not Down and not carried makes a Leap Clear roll: a new Catalog entry `leap-clear`, marked not an action, Agility with Gear Dice from ODM Gear or from the horse while mounted, needs 1, Help from a comrade at the same Position, Push allowed, spending nothing. A success clears: the soldier holds In Reach relative to the Titan (or relative to the corpse, below). A failure pins. A Down soldier in the path is pinned with no roll; a carried soldier follows the carrier's roll.
  - **Pinned.** The soldier gains one Crush Critical Injury at a rolled Injury Location that cannot be lethal and takes no rider; a limb result pins that limb, and a torso or head result pins the body. The state `pinned` (a new `sheet-fields.yaml` value naming the Titan or corpse and the pinned location): the soldier is not airborne, holds In Reach relative to the pinning body, cannot move or be moved, cannot Fly, Ride, or Mount, and cannot make a Reaction (the OQ-18 hook, `forbids: [reaction]`); a body-pinned soldier also has no action; a limb-pinned soldier keeps their action and may take Heave, a Body Part strike against the pinning limb, Read, Call It, Rally, Draw Attention, Help, and Swap Initiative Card, and nothing else (a closed list in `effect-types.yaml`, `forbids-entries`). Under a living grounded Titan the Titan's cards land on a Pinned soldier who holds its Attention as on any soldier who cannot react.
  - **Freeing.** (1) **Heave:** a new Catalog entry `heave`, an action, Strength, no Gear Dice, Help allowed from the same Position, from In Reach or On Body relative to the pinning body; each success adds 1 to the body's heave count, and when the count reaches the Size Class's **Heave rating** (`size-classes.yaml` `heave`: Small 2, Medium 3, Large 4; an Abnormal lists its own) every soldier it pins is freed at In Reach; the count never resets. (2) **Cut free:** a limb-pinned soldier is freed when the pinning Body Part is Broken by Body Part strikes; a corpse's Body Parts keep their Toughness and counts and never regenerate; the body-pinned are not freed this way. (3) A living Titan that stops being grounded frees everyone it pinned, at In Reach. (4) The Titan Engagement's end (below) frees no one.
  - **The corpse.** A dead Focus Titan's body stays on the field as a corpse for the rest of the Titan Engagement. Every soldier's last Position relative to it (Chapter 5, section 5.2) becomes their Position relative to the corpse, except that On Body and Blind Spot read In Reach; the corpse counts as a grounded Titan for Position steps (`anchor-ratings.yaml` `grounded_titan`), and moves relative to it are made as moves are; Positions relative to a corpse count for Help, Heave, Body Part strikes on the pinning limb, and corpse heat, and for nothing else: it holds no Attention, plays no cards, has no clock, and is no fall's reference Titan.
  - **Ending.** `engagement-flow.yaml` `ending`, the no-focus-titan test reads "No Focus Titan is alive and no soldier is Pinned". While a soldier lies Pinned under a corpse the rounds go on: the Background Titans' clocks and the retreat clock fill as they do, a Background Titan may enter, and the end steps run when the last Pinned soldier is freed or dies. The no-soldier-standing test is unchanged and a body-pinned soldier is not standing.
  - No Fear Roll trigger is added; the list stays closed (ADR-0003 item 1).
- **Why:** Titan World's *Titan Bodies* is the model the owner cites: an Agility roll to evade, a limb or the body trapped, a burn each time the trapped player acts, rescuers lifting with Body rolls that may be pooled. WoF keeps each part in its own shapes: Leap Clear as an Agility roll a rule calls for (not a Reaction, so it spends no turn and a Down soldier is not asked to dodge), the pin as a capped Crush Critical Injury (the lethality is the heat and the other Titans), the lift as a count toward a Size Class rating (the Toughness pattern), and Positions relative to the corpse by reusing the grounded-Titan steps. The path is On Body and Blind Spot because a soldier at In Reach stands beside the legs and steps aside, and because the baseline policy's cutters at In Reach are on foot at Wooded, so a wider path would put a Leap Clear on two cutters at every grounding and press the Small table's Critical Injuries band (at most 1.0, today 0.86); the striker at Blind Spot is airborne and swings clear, which is what canon shows, so on the baseline the rule bites the Jammed striker who cut a grounded Titan's Nape on foot, the soldier freed from a dying Titan's hand, and whoever climbed On Body: rare, and the dread scene each time. The Titan Engagement running on while a comrade lies under the corpse is the rule that makes the rescue a scene with the retreat clock and the Background Titans still ticking, rather than an end step. Figures: pins per fight, Burn Critical Injuries per pin, and deaths from corpse heat are new reported rows; the direction on the bands is stated in 8-14.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0019 (the falling Titan named as harm outside the Behavior Tables); ADR-0003 `## Amended` (batch 8: item 12's new tracked values `heave-count` and `pinned`, with the entries that change them). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** new **Falling Titan**, **Pinned**, **Heave**, **Leap Clear**, **Corpse**. Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 section 5.2 (a corpse's Positions; a Pinned soldier cannot move), 5.7 *A Titan's death* (the steam and fall steps), *A grounded Titan* (the fall), new subsections *The falling Titan*, *Pinned*, *Heave and cutting free*, *The corpse* under 5.7, 5.11 *Ending* (the Pinned test), the GM's tracking list in 5.3 (heave counts, pins); Chapter 3 section 3.9 (Pinned forbids Reactions), 3.16 (the end waits for the Pinned); Chapter 2 Appendix 2A.3 (`leap-clear`, `heave`); Chapter 4 section 4.7 (a Pinned soldier cannot be lifted); `data/engagement/titan-harm.yaml` (`falling_titan`, `steam`, `titan_death`, `grounded`), `positions.yaml` (`corpse`), `engagement-flow.yaml` (`ending`), `size-classes.yaml` (`heave`), `anchor-ratings.yaml` (`grounded_titan` applies to a corpse), `round.yaml` (tracking); `data/character/action-catalog.yaml` (`leap-clear`, `heave`; tracked values `pinned`, `heave-count`); `data/harm/effect-types.yaml` (the Pinned lists), `sheet-fields.yaml` (`pinned`), `data/gear/carrying.yaml` (a Pinned soldier is not lifted); `data/titans/sprinting-abnormal.yaml` (its Heave rating); `tools/probes/chapter-06/run6.py` (every stat block lists `heave`). New entry OQ-146.

### 8-10: prosthetics (owner; queue item 17)

- **Decision:** A **Prosthetic** is a gear item in `items.yaml`: a workshop-made arm (a hook or an iron hand on a socket) or leg (a peg or a jointed wooden leg), rated none, giving no Gear Dice, never wearing, counting as 0 items, and never taken by Standard Issue. Two kinds, `prosthetic-arm` and `prosthetic-leg`, each fitted to one lost side. **Obtaining:** a Requisition row, Scarcity Limited (Funding 3 or more), one per Requisition; a player character may Requisition one for a Squadmate; or a Discovery in Phase 2 (an improved prosthetic is Canon Clock content under ADR-0016 item 2). **Fitting:** during a Downtime, once the 13+ row at that side has healed (28 days, 56 for a Burn), so a limb lost on one Expedition is at best replaced at the second Downtime after it. **What it restores:** the lost-limb grade is read with one fewer side lost per prosthetic fitted, so a soldier with one lost arm and a prosthetic has no arm grade, and a soldier with both arms lost and one prosthetic has the one-arm grade; two prosthetics of a kind never lower the grade past the one-limb grade, so both arms lost with two prosthetics reads as one arm lost (Fly, Fight, and Block at a 1-die penalty; the forbid list gone), and both legs lost with two prosthetic legs reads as one leg lost (moves on foot spend the action; Ride and Fly allowed at the rows' penalties). **What it never restores:** the 13+ rows' own permanent penalties (the arm row's 2-die penalty on the strikes, Break Free, Treat Injury, and Field Repair; the leg row's 2-die penalty on Fly, Ride, and the dodge), which stay and stack per side (8-11). **Limits:** a prosthetic is never a weapon, gives no dice to anything, cannot be Requisitioned before the injury heals, and is lost with the soldier's items when they die. Medical Retirement stays available beside it (8-11).
- **Why:** The owner asked for prosthetics as an alternative to retiring, inside ADR-0016's era: 845 to 850 has hooks, sockets, and peg legs, and nothing that grips an ODM trigger with two iron hands, which is why two prosthetics stop at the one-limb grade. Reading the grade with one fewer side lost is one sentence on top of `lost_limb_riders`, which the drafter's `sides_lost` field already counts, and it makes every combination closed with no GM choice. Requisition at Limited puts it behind Funding 3 and the ledger, as ADR-0016 item 8 asks, with no price. Figures: none; lost limbs are unmeasured (8-13).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0017 `## Amended` (batch 8: a prosthetic lowers the grade). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** new **Prosthetic**. Applied.

### 8-11: lost-limb readings and medical Retirement (queue items 10 to 13)

- **Decision:** (10) The rows' permanent effects stack, as `held_injuries.stacking` says: two lost legs dodge, Fly, and Ride at a 4-die penalty, and two lost arms strike at 4; 7-6's "keep the 2-die dodge penalty" described one row and is read that way; the YAML comment and the chapter's "keep the 2-die penalty" say "the rows' penalties, which stack". (11) Both arms lost: Shed Load is allowed (a strap is shrugged off); Mount or Dismount is allowed when a comrade at the same Position who is not Down spends their action to help, and never otherwise; the thrown-cloak decoy is forbidden and the riderless-horse decoy allowed; the dodge takes no Gear Dice from ODM Gear (the grade forbids ODM use) and takes the horse's while mounted. (12) A soldier the both-legs grade or Pinned forbids to move may be lifted and carried by Lift Comrade as a Down comrade is, at their own consent, and a carried soldier who is not Down keeps their action for entries that need no move (`lifting_a_comrade` requirements widened; `carrying_a_comrade.turns`). (13) Medical Retirement is open to Squadmates, chosen by the players as one table since any player directs a Squadmate; declining is not final: the choice is offered again at the end of every Downtime while the grade applies, so a soldier may try a prosthetic first and retire later.
- **Why:** Stacking is the general rule and "severe" is the owner's word for both limbs. The both-arms list follows 7-6's own line (hands are what the grade removes; a shrug, a shout, and a helped mount need none). A soldier who cannot move but is not Down needed a way to be carried, and Lift Comrade is the rule that exists. The Retirement choice recurring costs one sentence and makes prosthetics a real alternative rather than a race. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** Retirement ("by choice ... at the end of any Downtime after losing both arms or both legs"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.2 *Lost limbs* (the rendered table's Keeps and Cannot take cells; stacking) and 3.13; Chapter 4 section 4.7 (lifting a soldier who cannot move); `data/harm/critical-injuries.yaml` `lost_limb_riders` (both-arms `forbids_entries` unchanged, `forbids_decoys: [feint, flare, thrown-cloak]`, `keeps` and a `mount_with_help` field; both-legs `keeps`; the stacking comment), `data/gear/carrying.yaml`, `data/mind/scars.yaml` (`retirement.trigger.lost-limbs`: Squadmates; re-offered each Downtime). OQ-137.

### 8-12: Catalog and Skirmish readings (queue items 1 to 7)

- **Decision:** (1) 7-17's "firearms give no Gear Dice" applies inside a Titan Engagement only; Shoot rolls Agility with the firearm's Gear Dice (rated 1), a Pushed 1 wears it, and a firearm at 0 cannot be fired until Field Repair or Maintain Gear restores it. (2) A firearm arrives empty; outside a Skirmish and a Titan Engagement it may be loaded at any time, spending 1 shot and nothing else, and stays loaded until fired. (3) At a Standard Issue exchange the soldier may hand a kept item in and take the issue in its place; the default is to keep it. (4) A new Catalog option `release` (no roll, spends nothing, on the holder's turn) ends a hold the soldier has on a Foe and changes `held-end`; a Foe never releases. (5) A landed Grapple deals no damage; it sets Held, and a Held Foe is Fought bare-handed on later turns. (6) Confirmed: each roll changes only the tracked values its calling rule reads, and the proposal's tracked-values sketch is a list of what those rules read, not a wider grant. (7) The rations stock renders as a column of the Squad Supply stock table; WP-F extends `tools/render/render.py` to do it.
- **Why:** Each is the reading the drafter proposed or the narrower one, and each keeps ADR-0003's rule that an act changes only a tracked value through an entry. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0003 item 12 gains `release`).
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 4 sections 4.1 *Firearms* (Gear Dice outside a Titan Engagement; loading outside a Skirmish), 4.9 *Receiving it* (the exchange), 4.10 (the rations column); Chapter 7 section 7.4 (Release; the Grapple); `data/character/action-catalog.yaml` (`shoot` gear, `reload` outside a Skirmish, `release`), `data/gear/items.yaml` (firearms' wear), `data/gear/standard-issue.yaml` (the exchange); `tools/render/render.py`. OQ-141, OQ-142, and OQ-143 carry `Revised by batch 8` lines.

### 8-14: the rerun's checks, expected directions, and the first levers

- **Decision:** WP-S2 reads these verdicts, in this order, and a miss goes to the decider, never to a drafter's value change:
  1. **The attack shape (8-1, 8-2, 8-4).** Every target, band, bar limit, and reported row of ADR-0014 under Attack Dice, judged as today: the Medium band at most 0.06 through the end (research 0.058, at the edge, so a miss by sampling is judged on a second seed as 5-11 allows); the kill median 3; the first-Titan-Engagement row and the setup mix reported; the six Grab cells and the lone Grab; the Small and Large rows; the Abnormal's bar in both orders; the Jam test re-read with the holder dodging Attack Dice (every card at the kill pool is the upper bound); the lone and Levi-grade cuts; the gas medians. Expected: within sampling of the research's round 3 figures. The Large's deaths (0.156 against 0.185) have no band and are reported.
  2. **Steam (8-7).** The Medium Critical Injuries band (0.5 to 0.9, today 0.68) and the Small's (at most 1.0, today 0.86) with steam on, and a "without steam" row beside each. Expected: up by less than 0.05 on the Medium, since steam is damage and reaches the tables only at 0 Health; the Small nearer its limit. Deaths through the end: within sampling.
  3. **The falling Titan and corpse heat (8-9).** Pins per fight, Burn Critical Injuries per pin, deaths from corpse heat, and the share of Titan Engagements that run on for a Pinned soldier, on the reference start and the Jam-heavy rows. Expected: within sampling on the baseline (the path excludes In Reach and the airborne), and a real figure on the mounted and Jam rows.
  4. **The Burn rider (8-8).** The sequence cases' day Death Rolls with Burn on a day limit; reported.
  5. **The Skirmish probe (8-4)** with the musket at 4 and the extras rule; reported.
  6. **The Free Build Health 2 row (8-3)**; reported.
  If a band misses, the levers are taken in this order, each measured alone: remove the Regeneration steam trigger; soften the steam table (1 to 4 nothing, 5 1, 6 2); narrow the falling Titan's path to On Body; then, and only then, re-set the band under OQ-140's process. No Attack Dice count changes before the playtest.
- **Why:** The owner wants Option C and Burn before the playtest with one full rerun, so the rerun must say which figure moved and why; ordering the checks and the levers now keeps the rerun from becoming a retune. The expected directions are the research's (steam at every kill breaks the band only as a Critical Injury; the falling Titan's path is drawn to miss the baseline's cutters).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 8: the checks and the lever order). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** `data/titans/tuning.yaml` and `data/engagement/tuning.yaml` (the with-steam rows; the lever note); the report's sections; Chapter 5 section 5.13 and Chapter 6 section 6.6 (verdict sentences after the run). OQ-145, OQ-146.

### 8-15: the Cut and Pierce riders (owner; overrides 8-8's default)

- **Decision:** The owner overrode 8-8's "no Cut or Pierce rider" (the `IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md` *Needs owner* item 5): both types carry a rider, in the Bite rider's shape (`type_riders.<type>` under each table, rows plus `sets`), on all four tables.
  - **The Cut rider: a cut bleeds.** Every lethal row whose `time_limit` is `engagement` reads `turn` for a Cut Critical Injury: `arm-torn-artery` and `arm-mangled-arm`; `leg-opened-artery` and `leg-shattered-knee`; `torso-punctured-lung`; `head-fractured-skull` and `head-lost-eye`. Rows already at `turn` are unchanged, and no non-lethal row changes. A lethal Cut therefore causes a Death Roll at the end of each of the soldier's turns, Down turns included, until a Treat Injury roll stabilizes it, and at a Skirmish's end the limit becomes `engagement` with the patient's one aftermath roll, as 7-17's end steps already read. A Skirmish's turns count for `turn` limits: `death-rolls.yaml` `time_limits.turn.runs_out` and Chapter 3 section 3.4 read "in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish" (WP-F), since a `turn` limit that no Skirmish turn ran out would never fall before the end step turned it into `engagement`. Nothing else: no Death Roll penalty, no Down, no Stress.
  - **The Pierce rider: the ball lodges.** On every row from total 7 up that is not instant death (`arm-dislocated-elbow`, `arm-fractured-arm`, `arm-torn-artery`, `arm-mangled-arm`, `arm-lost-arm`; `leg-wrenched-knee`, `leg-fractured-leg`, `leg-opened-artery`, `leg-shattered-knee`, `leg-lost-leg`; `torso-deep-bruising`, `torso-crushed-ribs`, `torso-caved-chest`, `torso-punctured-lung`, `torso-internal-bleeding`, `torso-torn-open`; `head-concussion`, `head-head-blow`, `head-fractured-skull`, `head-bleeding-skull`, `head-lost-eye`), the rider sets two fields. `treat_injury: {penalty: 1}`: every Treat Injury roll made on this Critical Injury, in a fight, as the aftermath roll, or in a care window, takes a penalty of 1 base die, read as the `penalty` effect (Chapter 1, section 1.3, step 5: never below 1 base die, never removing Gear Dice) and added to the self-treatment penalty when the patient treats themselves. `heals_untreated: false`: while the Critical Injury is untreated, its remaining healing time does not drop when a day passes (`healing.yaml` `each_day`, step 4), so it heals only after a Treat Injury roll has succeeded on it and until then keeps its effects and its Health box crossed off; a lethal Pierce that Death Rolls stabilize is still untreated and still does not heal. The 4-or-less and 5-6 rows (grazes and clips; the ball did not stay) carry no rider. No infection clock: a death by the day is Burn's identity (8-8), and the wound that will not close on a lodged ball is the infection risk in the healing rules' own terms.
  - **No Titan source.** Both riders reach a soldier only through damage at 0 current Health from a Foe's blade or shot in a Skirmish (7-17, 8-4). No Titan harming entry, the Grab's crush, a fall, steam, corpse heat, or the Straggler's Bite carries Cut or Pierce (`data/titans/`, `data/engagement/`, and `data/gear/falls.yaml` name Crush and Bite only), there is no blade friendly fire (`blade-sets.yaml` ruins gear, never a soldier), and firearms have no use in a Titan Engagement. No Titan target, band, or bar limit moves.
  - **The packet** prints one line per rider under the tables, beside the Bite and Burn lines: "Cut: every lethal row has a `turn` limit. The wound bleeds, and the Death Roll comes at the end of each of your turns until it is treated." "Pierce: from the 7 row up, the ball is in the wound. Treat Injury on it takes a 1-die penalty, and it does not heal until it is treated."
- **Why:** The owner's examples (a Cut bleeds, so the Death Roll comes sooner unless treated; a Pierce lodges, so Treat Injury is harder) are taken as the two images, and each rider is the smallest field change that plays them. Cut uses the Bite rider's field on more rows: Bite severs (the arm and leg 12 and 13+ rows), Cut bleeds (every lethal row on every table, the artery, lung, skull, and eye rows included), so the two feel different and overlap only on the limb 12 rows. Pierce takes the aftermath rather than the fight: a lodged ball is dug out or it stays, which is why its rider touches treatment and healing and not the limit, and why the graze and clip rows are left plain. Refused: Treat Injury needing 2 successes on a Pierce (a Rookie Medic with a rated-1 kit would treat one 26% of the time and a Wits 2 treater with a kit 7%, which makes the Medic pointless rather than the wound harder); a Death Roll penalty on Pierce's lethal rows (Pierce would become a second fast type beside Cut); a Pierce that festers into a `day` limit (Burn's death, twice). Both riders use existing readings only: a `time_limit` field, the `penalty` effect, and the treated flag the sheet already records. **Lethality.** The riders push Skirmish lethality up, and only there. A lethal Cut on a row with no Death Roll penalty, held untreated by a Strength 3 soldier, kills at a fight's end about 58% of the time today (one Death Roll); under a `turn` limit it rolls at the end of each of the soldier's turns, so about 80% after two turns and 85% after three, and the share of Cut Critical Injuries that are lethal (15.3% at a rolled Injury Location) is unchanged; per Skirmish the push depends on how many turns follow a Down before the Skirmish ends, which the probe reports. Pierce moves less: the 1-die penalty lowers a Wits 2 treater's aftermath roll with a rated-1 kit from 42% to 31% and a Rookie Medic's from 67% to 60%, about 1 death more in 100 Pierce Critical Injuries; its cost is care-window rolls and days. Guns stay as lethal as Titan fights by the musket at 4 (8-4); the riders add the bleed and the ball on top. Figures: the Skirmish probe is report-only (8-4; 8-14 item 5); every Titan figure is untouched.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0017 `## Amended` (batch 8, 8-15: the Cut and Pierce riders; "Cut and Pierce carry no rider" withdrawn). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Injury Type (one clause each for Cut and Pierce). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.2 *Reading a row* (the two riders, one line each; "Only the Bite rider exists now" goes), 3.4 *Time limits* (a Skirmish's turns count; WP-F), 3.5 *Uses* (a Pierce's penalty), 3.6 (an untreated Pierce does not heal); Chapter 7 section 7.4 (one sentence: a Cut bleeds by the turn and a Pierce lodges, pointing at Chapter 3; WP-F); `data/harm/critical-injuries.yaml` (`type_riders.cut` and `type_riders.pierce` on all four tables; `table_fields.type_riders` names the rider fields `treat_injury.penalty` and `heals_untreated`; WP-C2), `data/harm/treat-injury.yaml` (the rider's penalty beside the self penalty and the Burn requirement; WP-C2), `data/harm/healing.yaml` (`each_day` step 4's exception and `healing_time`; WP-C2), `data/harm/death-rolls.yaml` (`turn.runs_out` in a Skirmish; WP-F); `tools/render/render.py` (the riders line under each table prints every type rider); `tools/sim/` (WP-S1 part 2b: the two riders in the Skirmish probe, with a "without the riders" row; part 3 reads the fields). New entry OQ-150; OQ-138 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-15)` line. `IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md` *Needs owner* item 5 is struck.

### 8-16: the Fear result readings (queue items 18 to 20)

- **Decision:** (18) A forced strike the soldier cannot legally make does not lapse. It stays pending, as `effect-types.yaml` `forced-action` reads it, until the soldier makes the strike, Rally clears it, the event's Titan dies, or the Titan Engagement ends. Two readings are added to `lasts` and `reading`. It waits while the soldier is Pinned exactly as it waits while they are Grabbed or Down, except that a limb-pinned soldier's Body Part strike against the pinning limb of the event's Titan is the forced strike and satisfies it. While it is pending, "no other action" allows one exception: Swap Blade Set, so that a soldier whose handles are empty or whose blades are ruined can refit and strike; nothing else (Break Free while Grabbed and the Pinned list's entries are their own states' rules, not this one's). The soldier's moves stay their own, so a soldier at Distant walks or flies toward the Titan on their move and strikes when a strike is legal. A soldier who cannot make the strike by their moves and a Swap Blade Set (both arms lost; no Blade Set left) stays as they are until a comrade Rallies them, the Titan dies, or the fight ends; that is the row's cost. (19) Confirmed: for `first-titan-engagement` and for a death no Focus Titan's card or Grab caused, the event's Titan is the nearest living Focus Titan by the fall rule's order (On Body, Blind Spot, In Reach, Distant), ties to the earliest label, as `fear-rolls.yaml` `event_titan.nearest` says; if the soldier holds Distant relative to it they hold Distant relative to every Focus Titan and the draw-attention effect does nothing by its own rule, which is the intended reading. No change. (20) "Hesitate included" stays where it stands in Chapter 5 section 5.13 and `data/engagement/tuning.yaml` until WP-S2. It records the measurement as it was made, on the old table, and the figures beside it are that measurement's. WP-S2's verdict pass, which already rewrites every verdict sentence from the report and re-commits the figures, replaces it with "witnesses' Fear Rolls with every row of the re-cut table" in both places. WP-D does not touch it.
- **Why:** A lapse rule needs a test of whether any strike could become legal by the soldier's own moves, which is a search over Positions and gear that a GM would have to judge and ADR-0003 refuses; a pending result is one sheet mark, and Rally by a comrade is the release the row was written with. Kill It is a total-8 row, one of the two rarest, and already spends two turns and gives a Scar; a soldier who cannot strike for the rest of the fight is the fiction of the row, not a hole in it. Swap Blade Set is the one action that makes an illegal strike legal by itself, so it is the one exception; a wider list would let the row be waited out by Field Repair or a Gas Roll. The Pinned wait mirrors the Grabbed and Down waits that 7-8 wrote, and the pinning-limb strike counting keeps a Pinned berserker doing the one thing the state allows. The nearest reading reuses the fall rule's order so that no second nearest rule exists. Figures: none; the six cells read rescuers at 7 or more who strike anyway, and the placement rules are unchanged.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.9 (the forced strike waits while Pinned; Swap Blade Set allowed; the pinning-limb strike counts) and 3.12 (no change to the table); `data/harm/effect-types.yaml` `forced-action` (`reading`, `lasts`); `data/harm/sheet-fields.yaml` unchanged. WP-C2 applies it, since it edits `effect-types.yaml` and section 3.9 for Pinned; WP-D need not reopen its files. Chapter 5 section 5.13 and `data/engagement/tuning.yaml`: WP-S2's verdict pass. OQ-139 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-16)` line; OQ-151 indexes the item.

### 8-17: cutting free: the pinning Body Part (queue item 21)

- **Decision:** `falling_titan.pinned` gains a `pinning_body_part` reading, and `cut_free` cites `body_part_strikes` with no new roll.
  - **Which part pins.** A limb pin (an arm or leg result on the Injury Location roll) names the Titan's Body Part of the same kind and side as that result, by the stat block's ids: a left arm result is pinned by the Titan's `left-arm`, a right leg result by its `right-leg`. If that Body Part is Broken, the other Body Part of the same kind pins instead. If both of the kind are Broken, no Body Part pins: the limb lies under the trunk, the pin stays a limb pin (the soldier keeps their action and the Pinned list), and only Heave or a living Titan standing frees them. A body pin (a torso or head result) names no Body Part. Position at the fall plays no part. The sheet's `pinned` value records the body and the pinning Body Part, or none.
  - **What cutting it takes.** The existing Body Part strike (`body_part_strikes`; Strength with a Blade Set's Gear Dice, needs 1, the action), against the pinning Body Part, from In Reach or On Body relative to the pinning body: the pinned soldier holds In Reach and may strike it themselves, and a comrade strikes from In Reach or On Body. A corpse counts as grounded, so the strike needs no working ODM Gear; under a living grounded Titan the grounded rule already says the same. Each success adds 1 to the Body Part's count toward its Toughness (the Size Class's or the Abnormal's; a corpse keeps every count and Toughness and never regenerates); when it reaches Broken, every soldier that Body Part pins is freed at In Reach relative to the body. On a living Titan the strike sets the just-hurt flag, extra successes create Openings, and a Broken part's `when_broken` applies (a Broken leg grounds it; a second changes nothing more). On a corpse the strike sets no flag and creates nothing, since a corpse holds no Attention and plays no cards. Help as on any Body Part strike. No penalty for striking with the pinned arm; the row's own effects already apply.
- **Why:** The location die is already rolled at every pin, so the pinning part costs no second roll and no choice, and sides give the map its one-to-one shape (7-4's die has a side for every limb). The Broken fallbacks are the Grab's "first arm that is not Broken" pattern in the other order. Using the strike and the Toughness as they stand keeps the two ways out in their own shapes: a Medium leg at Toughness 2 falls to a Blade Set's dice faster than a Heave count reaches 3 on Strength alone, so the blade is the pinned soldier's own way out and the Heave is the comrades' when the pin is the body or the blades are gone. Nothing new is valued or tuned. Figures: pins per fight and the share freed by cutting, Heave, or death are the report rows 8-14 item 3 names; within sampling on the baseline.
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0003 item 12, as amended in batch 8, already names the Body Part strike as a changer of `pinned`).
- **Glossary change:** Pinned ("the pinning limb" reads "the pinning Body Part, the Titan's limb of the same kind and side as the pinned one"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 section 5.7 *Heave and cutting free* (the part, the fallbacks, the strike, the corpse's no flag and no Openings); `data/engagement/titan-harm.yaml` `falling_titan.pinned.pinning_body_part`, `cut_free`; `data/harm/sheet-fields.yaml` `pinned` (the Body Part or none); `tools/sim/engine.py` (cut free as a Body Part strike on the named part; the freed counter split). WP-C2 and WP-S1. OQ-146 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-17, 8-18)` line; OQ-151 indexes the item.

### 8-18: Pinned and Lift Comrade; the Grab and the falling Titan (queue items 22 and 23)

- **Decision:** (22) 8-9 governs: a Pinned soldier cannot be lifted or carried until freed. 8-11 (12)'s "or Pinned" is an error and is struck; the widened `lifting_a_comrade` row names only a soldier the both-legs grade forbids to move, and Chapter 4 section 4.7 and `carrying.yaml` keep "a Pinned soldier is not lifted", as WP-C2 has drafted them. One row is added to `carrying_a_comrade.ends`: the carrier becomes Pinned. Carrying ends, and the comrade is Pinned too, with their own Crush Critical Injury at their own rolled Injury Location and their own pinning Body Part (this is what 8-9's "a carried soldier follows the carrier's roll" means on a failure); a carrier who clears carries the comrade to In Reach. (23a) A Grabbed soldier is outside the falling Titan's path in two cases and inside it in one. In another Titan's hand, they are outside it, whatever Position they hold relative to the falling Titan. In the falling Titan's own hand at its grounding, they stay Grabbed and are outside it (the hand holds them off the ground; the grounded Titan's Grab runs on). At the holding Titan's death they are freed at the `freed` step, before the steam and the fall, and for those two steps they hold On Body relative to the dying body (their last Position relative to it, as the corpse rule reads last Positions; `grab.yaml` `release.titan_dead` gains the clause) and are not airborne: they take the steam and are in the path, rolling Leap Clear if not Down, pinned with no roll if Down, and a release that makes them fall resolves first, so a fall that Downs them pins them. The engine's reading that a Grabbed soldier is never in the path is amended for this case. (23b) A Grab cannot take hold of a Pinned soldier. When a grab effect lands on a Pinned target, from the pinning Titan or any other, the failed-dodge, hold, attention, and witnesses steps do not happen; the crush lands, a torso Crush Critical Injury that cannot be lethal, as the Grab's Titan attack (ADR-0019; 8-2's no-rider stands). `grab_lands` gains a `pinned_target` reading. The soldier stays Pinned and no countdown starts.
- **Why:** Lift Comrade has no roll. If it took a soldier from under a Titan's body it would free them for one action with no Heave count and make Heave and cutting free dead rules, and 8-9's "cannot move or be moved", its fight that runs on for the Pinned, and the corpse heat that makes it a scene all assume the body comes off first; 8-11 answered a question about the both-legs grade and reached for Pinned by analogy without reading 8-9's list. The freed soldier is in the path because 8-9's own reasoning names "the soldier freed from a dying Titan's hand" as one of the three the rule bites; the hand goes slack and the body comes down where they drop, which is the owner's model and the dread scene. A soldier in another hand is lifted away, and a grounding does not open the hand. A Grab that pulled a Pinned soldier out from under a body would be a free Heave; keeping the crush keeps ADR-0019's rule that a landed card harms. Figures: the freed victim's Leap Clear and pin are counted in 8-14 item 3's rows (pins per fight, corpse-heat deaths); expected within sampling, since the case needs a Nape kill on a holding Titan. If the Medium band misses, 8-14's lever order applies first; the freed soldier's reading is not a lever.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** Pinned ("never lifted or carried until freed"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 4 section 4.7 (as WP-C2 drafted: a Pinned soldier is not lifted; the both-legs grade only); Chapter 5 section 5.7 *The falling Titan* (the Grabbed cases; the carrier who fails), *A Titan's death* (the freed soldier's Position for the steam and the fall), 5.9 (a Grab on a Pinned soldier: the crush only); `data/gear/carrying.yaml` (`lifting_a_comrade` requirements: the both-legs grade only; `carrying_a_comrade.ends`: the carrier Pinned), `data/engagement/titan-harm.yaml` `falling_titan.path` (the Grabbed cases; the carrier), `data/engagement/grab.yaml` (`release.titan_dead`; `grab_lands.pinned_target`); `tools/sim/engine.py` (the fall path reads the freed soldier; a Grab on a Pinned soldier lands its crush only; the carrier's failure pins both; Lift Comrade never takes a Pinned soldier, as followed). WP-C2 and WP-S1. OQ-137 and OQ-146 carry `Revised by batch 8 (8-18)` lines; OQ-151 indexes the item.

### 8-19: the rider example, and telegraph-only entries (queue items 24 and 25)

- **Decision:** (24) The rule stands as 8-2 and ADR-0019 state it: the Critical Injury roll adds 1 per net success beyond the first. 8-2's worked example miscounted: a roll of 5 against a Reaction of 2 leaves 3 net successes and rolls 2D6+2, not 2D6+3. The example is corrected in place in 8-2 (with a note), ADR-0019 carries no example and is unchanged, the glossary's Critical Injury entry already reads "per Net Success beyond the first", and Chapter 3's example, which WP-R wrote to the rule, stands. The research file's "2D6+3" is the research's own text and is left as history. (25) Confirmed: an entry whose only effect is telegraph omits `attack_dice` rather than listing 0, as `titan-format.yaml` `every_entry` reads it; the render's column shows no pool for it, and `run6.py check` reads the absence as telegraph-only. No decision needed, noted here: the Grab table in Chapter 5 section 5.9 now prints Attack Dice beside cells measured under Severity; WP-S2's rerun refreshes the cells, and until then the `PROVISIONAL` marker of 8-1 covers them.
- **Why:** A worked example that contradicts its rule is a trap for the next drafter; the rule is the owner's fixed input and the research measured it. A 0 in the pool column would invite a roll of no dice. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** none beyond this file. OQ-145 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-19)` line; OQ-151 indexes the item.

### 8-20: the run-on with no living Focus Titan: a corpse's Positions (WP-C2 reading a)

- **Decision:** Confirmed, with the reference amended. A Position relative to a corpse is a Position in the Titan Engagement: `positions.yaml` `holding_a_position` reads "relative to at least one Focus Titan or corpse", so a soldier who holds one is in the fight for the ending tests (a limb-pinned soldier is standing, a body-pinned one is not, and a comrade at In Reach relative to a corpse holds a Position, so the no-soldier-standing `empty` row is not met while anyone does), for witnesses, the care-window scope, Stress relief, and Grief, for the retreat's forced moves (a step toward Distant relative to the corpse, then leaving, as toward a Focus Titan), and for returning (a returner holds Distant relative to every Focus Titan and every corpse). This is not limited to the run-on: it holds whenever a corpse lies on the field, and while a Focus Titan is alive it changes nothing, since every soldier in the fight holds a Position relative to that Titan too. 8-9's and `corpse.counts_for`'s "and for nothing else" is withdrawn: a corpse's Positions count for every rule that reads a Position. What a corpse does not do is its `nothing_else` list, which gains one line: Heave and the Body Part strike against a pinning Body Part are the only acts made against a corpse (no Nape strike, no other Body Part strike, no Read, no Break or Draw Attention, no Openings, and no ladder reads a Position relative to it). The comparison: an act against a body (Heave, the strike on the pinning Body Part, and Help or Covering on either) compares relative to that body, corpse or Titan, as `comparison.titan_named` reads; every other comparison (Treat Injury, Rally, Help, Covering, Lift Comrade, Pass Item, Take Item, Field Repair, Pry Loose) is relative to the living Focus Titan with the earliest label and, when none is alive, relative to the corpse of the Focus Titan that died last, to which the drafter's "the corpse with the earliest label" is amended. The end steps read the same body: the aftermath roll's `who_rolls` "relative to the Focus Titan alive last" reads the Positions relative to that Titan's corpse as they stand when the Titan Engagement ends, run-on or not; 5-4's last Positions are those Positions, kept live by `corpse.positions` rather than frozen at the death, and `engagement-end.yaml` `positions_read` says so.
- **Why:** 8-9 wrote "for nothing else" to keep the corpse from holding Attention or playing cards, and its own ending rule needs more than that from the corpse's Positions: if a Pinned soldier held no Position in the fight, the no-soldier-standing `empty` row would end it the moment the last Focus Titan died, the reading 8-9 refused. One test for being in the fight (a Position relative to a Focus Titan or a corpse) is smaller than a list of the rules a corpse's Positions count for. The reference is the body that fell last because labels order arrivals, not deaths: when A died first and B was fought afterward, Positions relative to corpse A are those of the moment it fell (only a move that names A changes them, and nobody makes one), so comparing by them would let a soldier who flew to B's Blind Spot Rally a comrade "at the same Position" beside A; the aftermath roll already reads "the Focus Titan alive last" (5-4), so the run-on adds no second reference. Reading the corpse's Positions at the end rather than last Positions frozen at the death differs only by the fall, which puts every soldier who was On Body or at Blind Spot at In Reach, and the engine already applies the fall before its end steps, so the aftermath rows are read as measured. Figures: none move; 8-14 item 3's rows count the run-on.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** Corpse ("keeps its Positions, and a soldier who holds one is in the Titan Engagement; Heave and the strike on a pinning Body Part are the only acts against it"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 sections 5.2 (a corpse's Positions; the comparison rule's "when none is alive" clause), 5.7 *The corpse*, 5.11 (a returner holds Distant relative to every corpse); Chapter 3 sections 3.5 (the aftermath roll's reference) and 3.16; `data/engagement/positions.yaml` (`holding_a_position`, `comparison.otherwise`, `corpse.counts_for` and `nothing_else` rewritten, `run_on` folded into them and its marker removed, `leaving.returning`), `engagement-flow.yaml` (`run_on`, the returner), `data/harm/engagement-end.yaml` (`positions_read`), `treat-injury.yaml` (`aftermath_rolls.who_rolls`); `tools/sim/engine.py` (as followed). WP-C2 and WP-S1. OQ-156 indexes the item; OQ-122 and OQ-146 carry `Revised by batch 8 (8-20)` lines.

### 8-21: still Pinned when the Titan Engagement ends (WP-C2 reading b)

- **Decision:** Confirmed, with its scope fixed and the retreat's moves made consistent with it. Only the no-soldier-standing test can end a Titan Engagement while a soldier is Pinned, since the no-focus-titan test waits for the last Pinned soldier and the retreat ends by no-soldier-standing. When it does, every Pinned soldier dies, left under the body, whether or not a Focus Titan is alive: a `left_behind` death, Down first, with no witnesses and no Fear Roll, counted for Grief, their left items leaving play. Every other soldier who still holds a Position dies only while a Focus Titan is alive, as `left_behind` reads; with none alive, a Down comrade lying beside the corpse goes through the end steps as after any kill. `left_behind` gains the clause, and `pinned_at_the_end` cites it. The retreat: `background-titans.yaml` `retreat.effects.moves` options 3 and 4 read a Pinned comrade beside a Down or Grabbed one. Option 3 allows a step toward a Pinned comrade; option 4 allows staying at a Pinned comrade's Position after a Heave against the pinning body or a Body Part strike against the pinning Body Part, taken earlier in the same turn for that comrade; Positions are compared relative to the pinning body; Lift Comrade stays off the list, since a Pinned soldier is never lifted (8-18). A Pinned soldier's own forced move changes nothing, as `moves.pinned_soldier` already reads.
- **Why:** The drafter's reading is the one the ending rule implies: the fight ran on so that the body could be lifted, and if it ended because no one is standing, no one is left to lift it; a soldier under a Titan corpse with the next Titan coming dies as a Down comrade left to the Titans does. Killing the Down comrades beside a corpse when no Titan is alive would punish a kill, and the ordinary kill ending leaves them alive. The retreat clause is needed for 8-9's scene to exist under the retreat clock: without it the retreat's first turn marches every comrade away from a Pinned soldier who is not Down (a comrade at In Reach can always take option 1), and OQ-87's promise, that a retreat never makes a Squad abandon a comrade, would fail for the one state that most needs it. Figures: none move; corpse-heat deaths and fights that ran on are 8-14 item 3's rows.
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0010's retreat promise is kept, not changed).
- **Glossary change:** Pinned ("if the Titan Engagement ends while the soldier is still Pinned, they die under the body"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 sections 5.10 *The retreat* (options 3 and 4) and 5.11 *Ending* (the Pinned die); Chapter 3 section 3.16; `data/engagement/engagement-flow.yaml` (`left_behind`; `pinned_at_the_end`, its marker removed), `background-titans.yaml` (`retreat.effects.moves`); `tools/sim/engine.py` and `policy.py` (the Pinned die at the end; the retreat policy may step toward and stay beside a Pinned comrade). WP-C2 and WP-S1. OQ-157 indexes the item; OQ-126 and OQ-146 carry `Revised by batch 8 (8-21)` lines.

### 8-22: the heave count after a re-grounding (WP-C2 reading c)

- **Decision:** Amended. The heave count is cleared when a living Titan stands: `grounded.ends` frees every soldier it pinned (8-9) and returns its heave count to 0, so a body that comes down again is a fresh fall and no Heave frees on 0 successes. Nothing else clears it: not a Regeneration fill, not the Titan's death (a grounded Titan that dies falls no second time, and its count carries to the corpse), not the interval between Heaves by different soldiers. `heave.count` reads "is cleared only when a living Titan stands", and `count_already_reached` goes. The engine sets the count to 0 where the Titan stops being grounded.
- **Why:** A count that outlives the freeing it caused has nothing left to measure: it stood for the body's being levered up, and the body then stood on its own. The provisional reading kept the words "never resets" at the price of a rule under which a Heave that rolls no success frees a comrade, which a player would read as a misprint. One clause and one engine line. Figures: none; a re-grounding after a stand needs a second leg Broken after the first healed, and no target reads it.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** Heave ("the count is cleared only when a living Titan stands"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 section 5.7 *Heave and cutting free* and *A grounded Titan*; `data/engagement/titan-harm.yaml` (`falling_titan.heave.count`; `count_already_reached` removed; `grounded.ends`), `round.yaml` (the tracker's heave count, cleared at a stand); `tools/sim/engine.py` (`free_pinned` on standing clears `heave_count`). WP-C2 and WP-S1. OQ-158 indexes the item; OQ-146 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-22)` line.

### 8-23: lost limbs with Leap Clear and Heave (WP-C2 reading d)

- **Decision:** Both arms lost: Leap Clear takes no Gear Dice from ODM Gear and takes the horse's while mounted, as the dodge does under 8-11 (confirmed); Heave is forbidden, so the both-arms grade's `forbids_entries` gains `heave` (amended). One arm lost: the `arm-lost-arm` row's permanent 2-die penalty list gains `heave`, beside Break Free and the strikes; it stacks per side and survives a prosthetic (8-10, 8-11). Both legs lost: Leap Clear is allowed, since it is not a move (confirmed); the `leg-lost-leg` row's permanent 2-die penalty list gains `leap-clear`, beside Fly, Ride, and the dodge, so a soldier with both legs lost leaps clear at a 4-die penalty, as they dodge (amended). Heave with a lost leg takes no penalty and is allowed from wherever the soldier is placed. Prosthetics change none of this: a prosthetic lowers the grade and never the rows' penalties (8-10), so a both-arms soldier with one prosthetic reads the one-arm grade and Heaves at the row's penalty.
- **Why:** The grade's test is what needs hands (8-11), and a Heave is a lift, as Lift Comrade and Break Free are, both already on the both-arms list; Help stays open to the both-arms soldier, so a shoulder set to the body is still theirs to give. The arm row penalizes the grip motions and the leg row the mobility rolls; Leap Clear is the fall's dodge, and 8-11 already stacks the dodge, so a soldier with no legs who leapt clear at full dice while dodging 4 short would be the odd row. Each change is one id in an existing list. Figures: none; lost limbs are unmeasured (8-13).
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0017's amendment already reads the rows' penalties as stacking and surviving a prosthetic).
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.2 *Lost limbs* (the rendered table's cells; the 13+ rows' penalty lists); `data/harm/critical-injuries.yaml` (`arm-lost-arm.permanent_effects` gains `heave`; `leg-lost-leg.permanent_effects` gains `leap-clear`; `lost_limb_riders.grades.both-arms.forbids_entries` gains `heave` and its `keeps` names Leap Clear with the horse's dice; `both-legs.keeps` names Leap Clear and Heave); `tools/render/render.py` if the cells are rendered. WP-C2. OQ-159 indexes the item; OQ-137 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-23)` line.

### 8-24: the Burn rider's "one unit of medical supplies" (WP-C2 reading e)

- **Decision:** Confirmed. The only medical unit spent on a roll is the care-bonus use (`squad-supply.yaml` `medical_uses.care-bonus`; `bonus-dice-sources.yaml` `medical-supplies`), made in a care window, and that spend meets the Burn rider's requirement while giving its Bonus Die. So a Burn Critical Injury is treated in a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish only by a treater whose medical kit counts as had (a spent kit at rating 0 does not; `items.yaml` `medical-kit`), and in a care window by such a kit or the care-bonus spend. The aftermath roll never reaches a Burn: the rider puts every lethal Burn row on a `day` limit and the aftermath roll's patient holds an `engagement` limit, so the requirement there is the kit by construction and needs no sentence. No new spend is added: a medical unit spent in a fight for no die would be a new use of Squad Supply, and the day limit is the Burn's clock, meant to run to the window.
- **Why:** The drafter's reading uses the one spend that exists and adds nothing. A Burn treated in the fight gives back its Health box or ends Down, which a kit does; the rider's cost is that a Squad without a kit waits for the window and pays a unit there. Figures: none; the sequence cases' day Death Rolls under the Burn rider are reported (8-14 item 4).
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.5 *Uses* (the Burn sentence names the kit that counts as had and the care-bonus spend); `data/harm/treat-injury.yaml` (`type_riders.requires_kit_or_supplies`, the marker removed). WP-C2. OQ-160 indexes the item; OQ-138 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-24)` line.

### 8-25: Mount or Dismount with both arms lost, outside a Titan Engagement (WP-C2 reading f)

- **Decision:** Confirmed, with the tests named. In a Skirmish, as in a Titan Engagement: a comrade taking part who is not Down spends their action to help, with no range test, since a Skirmish has no Positions and its Help rule already lets any comrade taking part Help whether Engaged or Apart. Anywhere else (a Leg, a night camp, a Chase, Downtime, or no procedure): any comrade in the Squad who is not Down and is on the same Expedition as the soldier, or in the Squad when no Expedition is under way (the test the `outside-harm` care window's scope already uses), helps, spending nothing, as Help on a Leg roll spends nothing. With no such comrade the soldier is not mounted, and on a Leg travels as the both-legs grade's soldier does, across a comrade's saddle or in the wagon. `lost_limb_riders.grades.both-arms` carries the three readings in a `mount_with_help` field, and `horses.yaml` `mounted.mount.requirements` points at it.
- **Why:** Each setting reuses a test it already has: the Titan Engagement's action and Position, the Skirmish's action and its "taking part, not Down" Help test, and the Expedition's scope test from the care window. Spending nothing outside a fight follows Chapter 7's Leg Help, which spends nothing, and there is no action to spend. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.2 *Lost limbs* (the both-arms cell); Chapter 4 section 4.5 (one pointer sentence); `data/harm/critical-injuries.yaml` (`both-arms.mount_with_help`), `data/gear/horses.yaml` (`mounted.mount.requirements`). WP-C2. OQ-161 indexes the item; OQ-137 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-25)` line.

### 8-26: a Heave Talent (queue item 26, WP-B)

- **Decision:** Neither list gains a Talent. Grip Breaker, the Brawler's dice Talent on Break Free, names `heave` as a second entry, so the Brawler list keeps its eight and the general list its twelve. The row:

  ```yaml
  - id: grip-breaker
    name: Grip Breaker
    description: "Tearing free of a grip, even a Titan's, and heaving its body off a comrade."
    type: dice
    max_level: 3
    names: [break-free, heave]
    specialties: [brawler]
  ```

  No Talent adds dice to Leap Clear (the WP-B constraint stands), and no rule Talent touches Heave. The simulator can measure it: `dice.roll` adds a dice Talent's dice by Catalog entry (`s.talents.get(entry)`), and `heave` is a registered entry, so WP-S1 part 2b's sensitivity row gives Grip Breaker 2 to the Heaver on the pin rows of 8-14 item 3 (the Jam-heavy and mounted rows, where pins occur) and reports pins freed by Heave, turns spent Pinned, and corpse-heat deaths beside the row without it. Report only: no target reads Heave.
- **Why:** Heave is Strength, the Brawler's key attribute, so a Brawler is already the Heaver; what was missing was the Talent line that says so, and Grip Breaker is that Talent (tearing loose from a Titan, by the hand or by the body). Widening it costs no list slot, no new name, and no replacement of a Talent that fires more often than a pin does; a new Heave Talent on a full list would replace a live Talent with one that waits for a scene the baseline sees within sampling. ADR-0016's guardrails: a live entry with no permission (1), no new dice Talent (6), a rescue as the teamwork lever (7), a sensitivity row (4), the name unchanged (9); 7-2's counts stand (eight per list, four dice and four rule Talents on the Brawler's, the shared-Talent limit untouched). Refused: a Heave Talent on the general list, which is temperament and survival with its dice Talents capped at four; a rule Talent that spares the heaver the corpse's Burn damage, since that damage is the scene's cost (8-7); Shoulder Charge widened to a Heave that frees a comrade from a living Titan, since an Opening from a lift is a new mechanic. Figures: a reported sensitivity row.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 2 Appendix 2A.1 (Grip Breaker's row); `data/character/talents.yaml` (`grip-breaker.names`, `description`); the packet's Talent reference; `tools/sim/` (WP-S1 part 2b's row). WP-B, after WP-C2 adds `heave` to the Catalog. OQ-162 indexes the item; OQ-136 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-26)` line.

### 8-27: the Expedition readings (OQ-152, WP-F)

- **Decision:** All six provisional readings are confirmed as WP-F drafted them. (a) A Hard Ride is ordered per day, after the day's second Leg, when a Leg is left on the route: on a day the Mission Brief lists, or on the interim route when a D6 rolled after the second Leg shows 5 or 6; a day's first or second Leg is never a Hard Ride. (b) A Leg ridden again after a retreat counts toward the day's Legs. (c) A Squad that holds fewer rations than a Leg or a camp spends meets hunger: it spends what it holds and the hunger row applies.
