(d) The Straggler's pool is the living soldiers of the rolled kind who are not Down; with none, the other kind; with neither, the row reads as titan-on-the-formation; the Bite Critical Injury counts as gained as the row's Titan Engagement begins, so no outside-harm care window is held for it and its limit runs as one gained in that fight. (e) On a Leg every soldier who is not Down and whose own horse is not lame is mounted; at a night camp everyone dismounts; a Down soldier and one with a lame horse ride in the wagon or across a comrade's saddle; a Hard Ride with no soldier who can make the Ride roll fails its Leg roll. (f) The night hazard roll adds the day's last Leg's Distance Band, and a Titan Engagement the Night table begins takes the Anchor Rating of the Waypoint that Leg reached.
- **Why:** Each reading is the proposal's own sentence or ADR-0009's: a Hard Ride is "a third Leg squeezed in before dark", which is a day's shape, not a Leg's; ADR-0009's batch 7 amendment already says the Leg is ridden again after a retreat, and counting it is the retreat's cost in daylight; hunger is running short, and a spend the Squad cannot meet is the row's own trigger; a Down soldier makes no roll but the Death Roll (Chapter 3), so the pool must exclude them, and the fallback row is the table's nearest; the Straggler's fight begins at once, so its windows are that fight's; the camp is at the Waypoint the day's last Leg reached, so the night reads that Leg and that Waypoint. None needs a judgment, and none moves a figure, since no target reads an Expedition. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** the `PROVISIONAL (OQ-152, ...)` markers in `data/expedition/legs.yaml`, `hazards.yaml`, and Chapter 7 section 7.1 are replaced by citations of this item; no text changes. WP-F tail. OQ-152 is decided.

### 8-28: the Downtime readings, and Downtime Actions in the Catalog (OQ-153, WP-F; coordinator item 3)

- **Decision:** (a) Confirmed: Downtime runs until the next Expedition begins; its seven days pass at the infirmary step, nothing else in it takes days, and neither the interim day nor the interim issue happens while it runs. (b) Confirmed: Downtime holds one care window, at the infirmary, and each of the seven days gets the infirmary roll instead of a window; a Critical Injury the window leaves untreated, a Pierce included, which does not heal until treated (8-15), waits for the next window, and that is the stopgap's cost until Phase 2's Downtime. (c) Amended: the after-actions step reads, in order, granted Requisitions arrive, prosthetics are fitted, medical Retirement is offered again, then retirements and promotions due resolve. (d) Amended: Downtime Actions and the Squad Action get Catalog entries. Two entries of kind `option`, `downtime-action` (`option_of`: the downtime-actions step of Downtime, `data/campaign/downtime.yaml` `steps`; one per player character per Downtime; `requirements`: `downtime_actions.who`; `rolled: never`) and `squad-action` (`option_of`: the squad-action step; one per Squad per Downtime; `requirements`: `squad_actions.who`; `rolled: never`), each with its rows kept in `downtime.yaml`, as Break Attention keeps its decoys in `attention.yaml`. Three new tracked values: `stress-lower` (lower a soldier's Stress; Recover, Visit Haven), `grief-lower` (lower a soldier's Grief; Visit Haven, Honoring the Fallen), `squad-recruit` (bring a Squadmate into the Squad Pool; Recruit); Maintain Gear changes the existing `gear-restore` and `gas-restore`; the Requisition row spends the Downtime Action on `persuade`, `recall`, or Help, which change `requisition-grant` already. The Squadmate relief and the infirmary roll are results of the procedure, not acts, and get no entry. `downtime-action` `changes: [stress-lower, grief-lower, gear-restore, gas-restore, requisition-grant]`; `squad-action` `changes: [grief-lower, squad-recruit]`.
- **Why:** (a) and (b) are 7-15's sentences. (c) The drafter's own reason for the order, a limb that heals during the seven days taking a prosthetic the same Downtime, needs the item to arrive before the fitting: 8-10 forbids Requisitioning a prosthetic before the row heals, so the Requisition is made in this Downtime's actions step and the grant must land before the fitting or the fit waits a whole Expedition; and the Retirement offer belongs after the fitting, so the soldier chooses knowing the prosthetic is on. (d) ADR-0003 item 12 asks for a tracked value and an entry for every act that changes one, and item 9 says an act outside the Catalog changes a value only through an entry; a Downtime Action is a soldier's act, chosen, that lowers Stress or Grief, restores gear, or fills the Squad Pool. Chapter 4 set the precedent with `mount-or-dismount`, an option with no roll (ADR-0003 item 12's own example). Two option entries with their rows in `downtime.yaml` cost two Catalog rows and three tracked values, and give the Foundry translation the act as a thing the player does. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** none in text; ADR-0003 item 12 is met by the two entries and three values, recorded here.
- **Glossary change:** Downtime Action ("a Catalog option taken once per Downtime"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 7 section 7.2 (the after-actions order; the two entries named in *Downtime Actions and the Squad Action* and in 7.5's acts table); Chapter 2 Appendix 2A.3 (the two rows) and 2A.5 (the three values); `data/campaign/downtime.yaml` (`steps.after-actions`; `downtime_actions.entry`, `squad_actions.entry`; the markers replaced), `data/character/action-catalog.yaml` (`downtime-action`, `squad-action`; `tracked_values` gains the three), `data/campaign/requisition.yaml` (`grant.arrives` cites the new order). WP-F tail. OQ-153 is decided.

### 8-29: the Requisition readings (OQ-154, WP-F)

- **Decision:** All three provisional readings are confirmed. (a) The ledger never falls below 0, so Size Up's success counts once a grant has raised it; any soldier in the Squad who is not Down may roll Size Up, a Squadmate included, directed as Chapter 2 states, and it spends no Downtime Action. (b) A granted item arrives at the after-actions step of the Downtime, before the next Standard Issue, and under 8-28 before prosthetics are fitted. (c) The prosthetic row carries the label canon-adjacent.
- **Why:** A needs of 0 is no roll, and 7-16 names one soldier and no cost for Size Up; 7-16 says the item arrives before the next Standard Issue, and 8-28 fixes where in the step; the show gives no Survey Corps soldier a prosthetic, 8-10 calls it an era item a workshop can make, and no playtest rule reads the label. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** the `PROVISIONAL (OQ-154, ...)` markers in `data/campaign/requisition.yaml` and Chapter 7 section 7.3 are replaced by citations of this item; `grant.arrives` cites 8-28's order. WP-F tail. OQ-154 is decided.

### 8-30: the Skirmish readings, no Foe Grapples, and Burn kills (OQ-155, WP-F; coordinator items 2 and 4)

- **Decision:** Nine of the ten provisional readings are confirmed and one is amended. (a) One Foe group per playtest Skirmish, at the start's number or the kind's group size, the trooper's rolled at D3+1. (b) Each Bandit rolls D6 as the Skirmish begins, 1 to 3 a club (a firebrand at night) and 4 to 6 a knife; a Garrison sentry Engaged fights bare-handed; a Foe's firearm starts loaded; a Foe's Reload spends its turn and no shot. (c) A Foe never attacks a Down soldier, and a Down soldier's move changes nothing. (d) The Squad may roll Sneak before round 1 of any Skirmish whose start does not give the Foes the ambush; a failure gives no side the ambush. (e) **Amended: Burn kills.** A Foe brought to 0 Health by Cut, Pierce, or Burn damage is dead; Crush alone puts a Foe out cold. `damage.on_a_foe.killed_by` reads `[cut, pierce, burn]` and `out_cold_by` reads `[crush]`; the first-human-kill trigger reads "damage of Injury Type Cut, Pierce, or Burn brings a Foe to 0 Health", so a soldier who kills with a flare makes the Fear Roll; the weapons table's flare and firebrand rows say they kill; ADR-0018's "only Cut or Pierce kills" is amended. (f) The Squad may leave at the end of a round in which every soldier not Down is Apart from every Foe, not Held, and holding no Foe, and every Down soldier is carried; when every soldier is Down or dead, the Foe group leaves and takes nothing. What a winning Foe group takes, an MP arrest or a Bandit's loot, is Phase 2's, noted in OQ-155. (g) A hold ends when the holder becomes Down or dies; a Held Foe of a broken group stays Held and leaves at the end of the round its hold ends. (h) Treat Injury and Rally are actions in a Skirmish, used as in a Titan Engagement with no Position requirement, the patient or the Rallied comrade any soldier taking part, tried again on a later turn. (i) A Foe with the ambush rolls 2 more Attack Dice against a soldier who has not yet acted. (j) **No Foe Grapples in the playtest**, and Held stays soldier-only: the Held-soldier rules stay written, with Break Free's "or Held in a Skirmish", for Phase 2's Foes, where an MP arrest party is the canon image; a Grappling Foe needs a holding step in the foe rule and an outcome for a Squad that is taken, and neither is a playtest minimum.
- **Why:** The confirmed readings each reuse a test that exists (the setup table's rolls, the Down soldier's limits in Chapter 3, the Titan Engagement's end shape) and keep the GM choosing nothing. Burn kills because ADR-0018's principle is that killing a person is a choice made by drawing a weapon, and bare hands are the exception because they are what a soldier uses when they mean not to kill; a firebrand to the face and a flare fired at a person are that choice made with fire, and Burn kills a soldier at 0 Health through a lethal Critical Injury, so a Foe out cold from the same fire would be the one asymmetry between the sides' damage. The owner wants lethal fights, and Titan World's Burn is its slow certain death; the playtest's firebrand Bandits become killers, which is the night's dread, and a soldier's flare into a person is a first kill with the Fear Roll it deserves. No Foe Grapples because the rule that would make Held live costs a holding step, a rule for what a holding Foe does each turn, and what happens to a Squad whose last soldier is Held, and the playtest's three Foes are a musket's source, an MP sabre, and a Bandit's knife; the Held-soldier rules are two lines and cost nothing to keep. Figures: the Skirmish probe's Bandit case with firebrands reports kills where it reported out cold; report only.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0018 `## Amended` (batch 8, 8-30: Burn kills; "only Cut or Pierce kills" withdrawn). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Foe ("at 0 Health, dead by a blade, a shot, or fire, out cold by a blow"); Held ("no playtest Foe holds a soldier"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 7 section 7.4 *Damage* (Burn kills), the weapons table (the flare and firebrand rows), *Fear Rolls, Drives, Talents, and Squadmates* (a first kill by fire), the example's Bandit night if a kill by firebrand appears; Chapter 3 section 3.12 (`first-human-kill` names the three types); `data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml` (`damage.on_a_foe.killed_by`, `out_cold_by`; `fear_rolls.first-human-kill`; the markers replaced), `foes.yaml` (the markers replaced), `data/mind/fear-rolls.yaml` (`first-human-kill.event`), `data/harm/treat-injury.yaml` (`in_skirmish`, the marker replaced); `tools/sim/` (the Skirmish probe reads `killed_by`; WP-S1 part 2b). WP-F tail. OQ-155 is decided.

### 8-31: the Medium deaths band at 0.08, the owner's chosen lethality (owner, after the full rerun; decides OQ-132 anew)

- **Decision:** Chapter 6's Medium band reads "at most 0.08 deaths per fight through the end of the Titan Engagement". Deaths during the fight are reported beside it and never judged. It is read at the standard Medium Titan's reference start and on the bar's two runs of that start, each judged on its own as today. It is recorded as the owner's chosen lethality for the first playtest (`OWNER-DECISIONS.md`, *After the full rerun*). No lever of 8-14 is taken, and no value or rule changes for it. On the committed rerun every row is Met:
  - the reference start, 0.0754 (standard error 0.0034, 1.4 inside the limit; during the fight 0.0663);
  - the bar's cutters-first run, 0.0687, and its strikers-first run, 0.0693 (standard error 0.0010; during the fight 0.0606 and 0.0614).
  The reading rules stand: the limit is exact (5-11), and a figure past it by at most 2 standard errors is judged on a second seed pooled with the first.
  - **The first-Titan-Engagement row** rose from 0.073 to 0.095 through the end (0.066 to 0.086 during the fight). No target reads it. It is a sensitivity row, reported and not tuned (5-14). The Abnormal's bar compares its reference deaths with the standard Medium Titan's reference start, not with this row, and OQ-140's target B reads the setup mix. The row passes the new limit as it passed the old, so the sentence that its deaths pass the Medium band's limit stays true.
  - **The verdict sentences that change** (WP-T):
    - `data/titans/tuning.yaml`: `targets.medium.at_the_reference_start.deaths_per_fight` reads at most 0.08, its comment citing 8-31 with 7-13's 0.06 and 5-19's 0.05 as history. `verdicts.medium`'s deaths sentence gives the limit ("re-set by decision batch 8, 8-31"), the three through-the-end figures, Met, and the three during-the-fight figures. Its first-Titan-Engagement clause gives 0.086 during the fight and 0.095 through the end, still past the limit, reported and not tuned.
    - Chapter 5 section 5.13, *The deaths column*: the note still names the 0.05 limit and the miss logged as OQ-132. It reads at most 0.08 and Met, with the rerun's figures.
    - Chapter 6 section 6.6: the *Deaths per fight* bullet reads at most 0.08, re-set from 0.05 by 7-13 and from 0.06 by 8-31 as the owner's chosen lethality, with the rerun's figures in place of the pre-batch-7 ones and their "predate" sentence. The design note under the table gives the first Titan Engagement's new figures and keeps "its deaths pass the Medium band's limit".
  - **Not a verdict sentence, but the run refuses without it:** `tools/sim/targets.py`'s guard on the Medium tolerance reads (0.08, True) with its message, and `report.py`'s comment follows.
- **Why:** The owner accepted the measured lethality and named the number, which binds. The rise is the round 1 package the owner chose: steam about 0.010, and the rest Attack Dice with the rider, sides and the Bite rider, the re-cut Fear table, and the falling Titan. Each lever of 8-14 would remove a rule the owner asked for. "About 0.08" is written as an exact limit because every band reads its stated limit exactly (5-11). It is 0.08 and not the measured 0.075, because a limit set at the figure would be a band drawn around a result. The reference start sits 1.4 standard errors inside, so the final rerun may land past it by sampling, which the second seed judges; a figure past it beyond that goes back to the owner, not to a lever. The band stays below OQ-140's target B (about 0.125 a fight on the setup mix with two Squadmates), which still re-sets every deaths band after the playtest. Figures: none move.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 8, 8-31). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 section 5.13; Chapter 6 section 6.6; `data/titans/tuning.yaml` (`targets.medium`, `verdicts.medium`); `tools/sim/targets.py`, `report.py`; the report's missed results, regenerated by the final rerun. WP-T. OQ-132 is decided anew; OQ-140 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-31)` line.

### 8-32: the stay beside a comrade ends when no Focus Titan is alive (the rerun's run-on loop; decides OQ-163)

- **Decision:** In a retreat, while no Focus Titan is alive, options 3 and 4 of the forced moves (`background-titans.yaml` `retreat.effects.moves`) are open only on the retreat's first rounds, as many as the retreat clock has segments (8 on the interim setup). From the next round on:
  - no soldier may step toward or stay with any comrade, whether Pinned, Down, or Grabbed;
  - each standing soldier's move is option 1 or 2, or, if they can make neither, letting go or changing nothing, as today;
  - the tracker's Engagement line records the round the retreat began (`round.yaml` `gm_tracker.engagement_line`).
  While a Focus Titan is alive nothing changes. No Nape strike is made in a retreat, so no Focus Titan dies in one, and the limit binds only a retreat that began during the run-on after the last Focus Titan died (8-9).
  - **When the stay ends, the Pinned soldier is left, as 8-21 reads.** Each comrade's move takes them toward Distant relative to the corpse and out. An action their Position still allows on the way may yet free the Pinned soldier, and a freeing ends the Titan Engagement at once by the no-focus-titan test. Otherwise, once no standing soldier holds a Position, no-soldier-standing ends it at once, since no returner exists in a retreat. Every Pinned soldier then dies under the body, a `left_behind` death: Down first, no witnesses, no Fear Roll, counted for Grief, their left items leaving play. Down comrades who still hold a Position live and go through the end steps, since no Focus Titan is alive.
  - **A limb-pinned soldier who is not Down** is standing, so the rounds go on for them alone. They may Heave or strike the pinning Body Part on their own turns, and corpse heat crosses off a Health box at the start of each turn. Once it makes them Down, no soldier stands, and they die under the body unless they have freed themselves first. That takes at most as many of their turns as they have Health boxes left.
  - **The rules text.** `engagement-flow.yaml` `always_ends` gains the clause: in a retreat with no Focus Titan alive, the fallen-comrade options close after the retreat's first rounds, so every standing soldier walks out, and a limb-pinned soldier left alone goes Down to corpse heat, after which no-soldier-standing ends it. Chapter 5 sections 5.10 and 5.11 say the same. The 5.10 design note's promise that a retreat never makes a Squad abandon a comrade reads "while a Focus Titan lives; over a corpse, the Squad stays as long as the retreat clock ran".
  - **The simulator follows the limit.** Past it, the retreat policy takes options 1 and 2 only. The retreat order guard raises when a policy steps toward or stays with a comrade past the limit. A counter reports the retreats in which the limit ended a stay, and the Pinned who then died. The safety-cap count is expected to read 0.
- **Why:** The loop is real under the rules as written. With no Titan alive, corpse heat never kills a limb-pinned soldier during the fight, because Burn's lethal rows go to a `day` limit (8-8) and the arm and leg tables have no instant-death row. A heave count and a Body Part's count rise only on successes, so a comrade whose Stress Responses cancel every success can stay by option 4 on every turn. `always_ends` fails, and the rerun hit the safety cap in 29 fights of about 1.9 million.
  - **The proposed fix does not close it.** The proposal counts a soldier's option 4 turns for a Pinned comrade. A soldier past the count can step out by option 1, step back by option 3, and make the same Heave or strike from In Reach on each return, because actions follow the move and are not limited. And while a Pinned soldier keeps the run-on open, a stay with a Down comrade by a Treat Injury whose successes are cancelled loops the same way.
  - **What this ruling closes, and at what cost.** It closes both options for every comrade and counts the retreat's rounds. That costs one field on a line the tracker already keeps, not a count per soldier and per comrade.
  - **The length.** It is the retreat clock's, so no number is new: the setup already says how long a Squad holds before the Titans close, and the Squad gives a comrade under the body as long again.
  - **Only while no Focus Titan is alive.** A living Titan is its own clock: its cards land, its Grab counts down, and a grounded Titan that stands frees the Pinned. No reference or helper row reached the safety cap (the report's section 6.12), and a limit there would move the Medium band that 8-31 just set.
  - **The scene.** The carcass steams, the withdrawal signal is already fired, the Squad heaves until it cannot stay, and the soldier under the body is left to it.
  - **Refused:**
    - corpse heat that kills during the fight, which reverses 8-8's day limit and makes every pin lethal on the reference rows;
    - a run-on round limit that ends the Titan Engagement outright, a third ending test that skips the walk out and 8-21's reading;
    - a retreat clock that empties during a retreat, a new mechanic and an edit to "no clock fills".
  Figures: within sampling (29 fights in about 1.9 million).
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0010 states no retreat promise; the promise is OQ-87's, in Chapter 5's design note, narrowed there.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 sections 5.10 *The retreat* (the limit under options 3 and 4; the design note's promise) and 5.11 *Ending* (the always-ends paragraph); section 5.3 if its tracking list names the Engagement line's fields; `data/engagement/background-titans.yaml` (`retreat.effects.moves`), `engagement-flow.yaml` (`always_ends`), `round.yaml` (`gm_tracker.engagement_line` fields and example); `tools/sim/engine.py`, `policy.py`. WP-T. OQ-163 indexes the item; OQ-87, OQ-126, and OQ-157 carry `Revised by batch 8 (8-32)` lines.

### 8-33: the Skirmish retune: a Military Police patrol of four that fights to the last (owner, after the full rerun; decides OQ-164)

- **Decision:** The Skirmish probe gets three reported figures. Each is read with the Cut and Pierce riders on, each range is read exactly, and each is reported beside its row. None is an ADR-0014 target, since the probe stays report-only (7-17):
  1. **The reference Squad against a Military Police patrol** (the main figure): 0.02 to 0.04 deaths per Skirmish through the end steps, with the Squad winning at least 90% beside it.
  2. **The reference Squad against three Bandits with their ambush at night, with firebrands:** 0.01 to 0.03 deaths.
  3. **One Rookie against one trooper:** the Rookie wins 50% to 70%.
  - **Levers.** Only the Foe fields that exist: `attack_dice`, `guard_dice`, `health`, `grit`, `group_size`, and the damage of a weapon only Foes wield. No rule changes, and 8-30's readings stand, including that a Foe never attacks a Down soldier.
  - **Values.** From the simulator agent's sweep (8,000 Skirmishes a row, riders on, fixed seeds), recorded in `foes.yaml`:
    - the Military Police trooper's `attack_dice` goes from 5 to 7, `grit` from 2 to 4, and `group_size` from D3+1 to a fixed 4;
    - its Guard 4, Health 4, Watch 2, sabre, and pistol are unchanged;
    - the Bandit and the Garrison sentry are unchanged.
  - **Measured.** The Squad against four troopers: 0.024 deaths and a 97.6% win (today, against three: 0.001 and 100%). The lone Rookie wins 56.2% (today 74.5%). The Bandit ambush: 0.015, unchanged.
  - **The main row.** It becomes "the reference Squad against four Military Police troopers", the kind's group, so the row is the fight a start that names no number gives. 8-30 (a)'s trooper roll is amended to 4, and a start that names a number still uses it.
  - **The Bandits take no change.** 0.015 sits inside their figure. If the owner reads the ambush as a squad fight that should sit inside 0.02 to 0.04, the smallest change to measure is the Bandit's `attack_dice` from 4 to 5. The row's deaths are the knife's lethal Cuts bleeding by the turn (0.016 with the riders, 0.005 without). A firebrand's Burn kills by the day, after the end steps, so firebrand damage is no lever on this figure.
- **Why:** The owner wants Skirmishes somewhat deadlier, a notch below Titan fights, at about 0.02 to 0.04 deaths a squad fight, against the Medium's 0.075.
  - **The setting.** In the sweep, changing a single field tops out near 0.005, so the fight has to last. Troopers who never break (Grit 4 in a group of 4) and hit harder (7 Attack Dice) keep sabres and pistols in play until the last one falls, which is how a Military Police patrol on orders fights in the coup arc. The setting changes the fewest fields, and the lone Rookie still wins more often than not, so a Survey Corps soldier stays the better fighter one against one.
  - **Refused:**
    - the sabre 3 variant (0.030, a 96.0% win, the Rookie at 53.0%). Its extra margin is not needed at the full probe's 120,000 Skirmishes (standard error near 0.0005 on 0.024), and its sabre would out-cut the Blade Set's 2, the blade that cuts Titans;
    - the three-trooper settings (Attack Dice 8, sabre 3, pistol 3, with or without Health 5), which change four or five fields and put the lone Rookie at 45.5% or 34.7%.
  - **The ambush figure** sits a step below the patrol's, because Bandits are the weakest Foe by design (Health 3, Guard 3, Attack Dice 4) and surprise with fire is what makes them dangerous.
  - **Parley.** Grit 4 also raises a patrol's Parley needs: in a Skirmish, 5 minus 1 for each trooper out; outside one, 4 plus the ask. A patrol under orders is hard to talk down, and Size Up still tells the Squad its Grit.
  - **A Foe that attacks a Down soldier** would add deaths too. 8-30 (c) keeps the Down soldier out of reach so that a Skirmish ends in flight, surrender, or a carried comrade, and nothing in the owner's decision asks for Foes finishing the fallen.
  Figures: the probe rows, reported.
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0014's amendment for 8-31 notes that these are reported figures, not targets.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 7 section 7.4: the rendered Foe table's trooper row, every "D3+1" for the trooper, and the design note's probe sentence ("three troopers" becomes "four"); `data/skirmish/foes.yaml` (`military-police-trooper`: `attack_dice`, `grit`, `group_size`; the three reported figures with their source); `data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml` wherever it names the trooper's roll; `tools/sim/cases.py` (the main row's number 4), `report.py` (the figures beside the rows). WP-T. OQ-164 indexes the item; OQ-143 and OQ-155 carry `Revised by batch 8 (8-33)` lines.

### 8-34: a soldier who cannot move, past the stay limit (WP-T's provisional reading; decides OQ-165)

- **Decision:** OQ-165 (a) is confirmed, with its test named by the grade and the moment it is checked fixed.
  - **The reading.** In a retreat with no Focus Titan alive, from the first round past the stay limit (8-32), a soldier is not standing for the no-soldier-standing test if a lost-limb grade forbids them every kind of move and they are not Down, Pinned, or carried. Today that grade is only both-legs, whose `moves` read `forbidden` on foot, mounted, and by ODM Gear. A body-pinned soldier is not standing on the same footing (8-20). A soldier who loses one turn's move to a Reaction or a result is not this soldier: the test reads the grade, not the turn.
  - **When it is checked.** The start of the first round past the limit is an event for `checked`, so the test is checked as that round begins, before its wings step, and after every event from then on. No returner exists in a retreat, so once the test is met it ends the Titan Engagement at once.
  - **What the soldier keeps.** Their Position, their turns, and every action their Position allows, such as Heave, the strike on a pinning Body Part at In Reach, Treat Injury, and Rally. Their forced move changes nothing. If they free a Pinned comrade, no-focus-titan ends the Titan Engagement at once.
  - **The way out is still the lift.** Past the limit, a comrade who shares their Position may still take Lift Comrade for them, at their consent, and carry them out by option 1 or 2, with the move after the lift (5-13). The limit closes options 3 and 4, not the lift, and no rule obliges anyone to take it.
  - **At the end.** They live and go through the end steps from the Positions they hold, as a Down comrade beside a corpse does (8-21). Every Pinned soldier still dies under the body, a `left_behind` death.
  - **Scope.** A soldier who has lost both legs and is also Pinned is read as Pinned (8-20, 8-21). While a Focus Titan is alive the reading does not apply: the stay limit does not bind, and the Titan's cards are the clock (8-32).
- **Why:**
  - **`always_ends` needs it.** Past the limit, a soldier with both legs lost can make neither option 1 nor option 2. They hold a Position and are not Down, so as written they keep no-soldier-standing false for as long as a Pinned comrade lives. With both arms also lost they can change nothing at all, and corpse heat kills only by the day (8-8). Before the limit the retreat clock bounds the fight; past it, only this soldier is unbounded.
  - **The lift cannot be the rule.** 8-11 lets a comrade carry them, but whether that happens rests on things no rule controls. The soldier must consent, and a comrade must already share their Position, since option 3 never names a comrade who is not Down, Grabbed, or Pinned. That comrade needs arms and no other burden. Requiring the lift would add a forced action to a retreat that forces only moves, and it would still need a fallback when no carrier exists. That fallback is either this reading or the death below. So the lift stays what 8-11 made it: the Squad's way to bring the soldier home, by choice.
  - **Left behind to die (b) is refused.** Nothing kills them: no Titan is alive, and they are not under the body. 8-21 refuses that death to a Down comrade beside a corpse, and killing the soldier who is awake while the one who is Down lives would make being Down the safer state. The scene is grim enough already. They cannot follow the Squad out, they watch the comrade under the carcass burn, and they live only because no Titan came.
  - **Carried out with no action (c)** moves a soldier no one lifted, against 5-13 and 8-18. **Naming the exception in `always_ends` (d)** leaves the loop open.
  - **The grade, not "can make no move of any kind".** The provisional wording also caught a soldier who lost a single turn's move. That would end the fight a round early while that soldier still stood and could have walked out. In a Titan Engagement, the grade is the only lasting state besides Pinned that forbids every move, and it is data (`lost_limb_riders`, `moves`). The test reads that data and the retreat's round, so no one chooses anything (ADR-0003).
  - **The stay beside a limb-pinned comrade still ends.** The soldier may treat the corpse-heat Burns of a limb-pinned comrade who shares their Position. Corpse heat crosses off a box at the start of each of that comrade's turns, and each success gives back at most one. Every round carries a chance that every treatment fails, and a run of such rounds as long as the comrade's remaining boxes puts them Down. A Push to hold the level wears the kit, and a kit at 0 no longer meets the Burn rider's requirement in a fight (8-24). The comrade goes Down unless freed, and then no one stands.
  - Figures: none. Lost limbs are unmeasured (8-13) and no reference build loses both legs, so the final rerun cannot exercise the reading. The argument above is the check.
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0003 is met: the test reads the grade's `moves` and the tracker's retreat round.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 section 5.11 *Ending*. In the no-soldier-standing bullet, the `PROVISIONAL (OQ-165)` sentence becomes this reading: it names the grade that forbids every move, the check as the first round past the limit begins, and the lift left open. In the *always ends* paragraph, the marker becomes a citation of 8-34. `data/engagement/engagement-flow.yaml`: `ending`, the `no-soldier-standing` test's `past_the_stay_limit` is reworded the same way, with the marker removed, and `always_ends`'s marker becomes a citation. `tools/sim/report.py`: the unmeasured list's "PROVISIONAL, OQ-165" becomes a citation of 8-34. No change to section 5.10, `background-titans.yaml`, `critical-injuries.yaml`, `carrying.yaml`, or `tools/sim/engine.py`, which models no lost limbs. A drafter applies it after the full simulator run in progress. OQ-165 indexes the item; OQ-163 carries a `Revised by batch 8 (8-34)` line.

### 8-35: an Expedition with every living soldier Down (final review D1, R01; amends 8-27 (e))

- **Decision:** `REVIEW-FIX-PLAN.md` D1, option 1, **the roll fails**, as the plan words it: "Extend 8-27 (e) to every Leg roll and to the camp roll: with no soldier who can make it, it fails. ADR-0009 still brings the Squad to the Waypoint, and `expedition.mounted` already puts Down soldiers in the wagon. A failed Leg spends 2 rations and adds 1 to the hazard. A failed camp gives no Camp Relief and adds 1 to the night hazard. The care window makes no rolls, `day` Death Rolls fall, and healing counts down."
  - 8-27 (e)'s last clause now reads for every Leg roll, Steady or Hard Ride, and for the camp roll. When no soldier on the Expedition can make the roll, it fails. That happens when every living soldier is Down, or, on a Hard Ride, when no soldier who is not Down is mounted on their own horse. With no roller there is no Help and no Push.
  - Nothing else in the Leg or the camp changes. If a hazard row then begins a Titan Engagement, its ending tests apply as written.
- **Why:** It is one clause with 8-27 (e)'s shape, and it keeps ADR-0009's guarantee. It is grim without a new death rule: a Squad that cannot stand pays double rations, faces worse hazards, and gets no Camp Relief, and a hazard row that starts a Titan Engagement ends it at once with the Focus Titan alive, where `left_behind` kills everyone. Option 2 (the Expedition is lost) contradicts 8-21, 8-34, and the Skirmish's no-soldier-standing ending, which spare Down soldiers when nothing kills them. Option 3 (carried home) invents a rescue ADR-0009 does not have and makes losing a fight a free trip home. The owner chose the recommendation (`OWNER-DECISIONS.md`, *After the full rerun*). Figures: none; no Expedition case.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0009 `## Amended` (batch 8, 8-35 and 8-37). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none. Camp Relief already follows only a successful camp roll.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 7 section 7.1 (plan R01: lines 97, 152, 207); `data/expedition/legs.yaml` (`expedition.down_soldiers`; `rolls.leg-roll.notes`, which drops "On a Hard Ride"; `rolls.camp-roll`, a matching note), each citing this item. `data/harm/treat-injury.yaml` needs no change, since no Down soldier rolls in a care window already. Package D. Packet: the Expedition and Night Camp procedures (package P). Simulator: none.

### 8-36: the stay limit counts per pin under a corpse (final review D2, R03; amends 8-32 and the scope of 8-34)

- **Decision:** `REVIEW-FIX-PLAN.md` D2, option 1, **limit per pin**, as the plan words it: "A stay (option 3 or 4) for a comrade Pinned under a corpse is limited in every retreat, counted as 8-32 counts it, whether or not a Focus Titan is alive. Stays for a Down or Grabbed comrade keep 8-32's reading."
  - **The limit.** Options 3 and 4 toward or beside a comrade Pinned under a corpse are open only on the retreat's first rounds, as many as its retreat clock has segments, counted from the round after the one in which the retreat began (the tracker's Engagement line, 8-32). From the next round on, no soldier makes option 3 or 4 for such a comrade, in any retreat.
  - **Unchanged.** A comrade Pinned under a living grounded Titan, and a Down or Grabbed comrade, keep 8-32's reading: limited only while no Focus Titan is alive.
  - **8-34 follows the limit.** 8-34's reading (a soldier whom a lost-limb grade forbids every move, and who is not Down, Pinned, or carried, is not standing for no-soldier-standing) also applies from the first round past this item's limit while a Focus Titan is alive, checked as that round begins, as 8-34 fixes. Such a soldier could otherwise hold the retreat open beside the corpse under a Titan whose Distant entries never reach them. When no-soldier-standing then ends the Titan Engagement with a Focus Titan alive, `left_behind` applies as written (8-21).
  - Applied in the same edit as R04, which goes first: a Down comrade is compared as any other comparison (`positions.yaml` `comparison.otherwise`; 8-20).
- **Why:** 8-32's reason was that a corpse is no clock. A Titan that never reaches the soldiers beside the corpse is no clock either, and the pin is what keeps the run-on open. Background Titans are always standard, and every standard table's Distant entries give only Stress or knock loose (the triage checked all three), so a Focus Titan that enters during the run-on reopens 8-32's loop. The test reads the pin and the tracker's retreat round, so it adds no tracking. It moves no measured value: with one Focus Titan, a pin under a corpse exists only after that Titan dies, where this limit equals 8-32's, so the Medium band of 8-31 stands. Option 2 (limit per reach) reads every soldier against every living Titan each round. Option 3 (fewer staying actions) leaves 8-32's own loop open, since a strike whose successes are all cancelled still allows the stay. The 8-34 clause closes the same loop for the one soldier the limit cannot move. Grim enough: the Squad heaves as long as the retreat clock ran, then walks out under a new Titan's grin. The owner chose the recommendation. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0010 states no retreat promise (8-32).
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 section 5.10 *The retreat* (the stay limit reads per pin under a corpse; the design note's promise reads "while a Focus Titan lives, except beside a comrade under a corpse, where the Squad stays as long as the retreat clock ran") and section 5.11 *Ending* (the *always ends* paragraph; 8-34's reading past this item's limit); `data/engagement/background-titans.yaml` `retreat.effects.moves` (the stay limit clause, citing this item); `engagement-flow.yaml` (`always_ends`; `ending.tests.no-soldier-standing.past_the_stay_limit`). Package B, after R04 and R02. Packet: the retreat text (package P). Simulator: none (one Focus Titan, no Background Titans); snapshot notes only.

### 8-37: a retreat from a Titan Engagement the Night table begins (final review D3, R05; reads 8-27 (b) and (f))

- **Decision:** `REVIEW-FIX-PLAN.md` D3, option 2, **fall back overnight**, as the plan words it: "The Squad falls back to the Waypoint the day's last Leg started from. The camp goes on at step 5 there, with no new camp roll, ration, or night hazard. The next day's first Leg rides that Leg again, counted among that day's Legs (8-27 (b))."
  - Falling back is not riding a Leg. It makes no Leg roll, spends nothing, and reads no Leg hazard, so ADR-0009's "Legs are never ridden at night" holds.
  - Only a retreat moves the camp. A Titan Engagement the Night table begins that ends any other way leaves the camp where it was.
- **Why:** ADR-0009 says a Squad that retreats from a Titan Engagement falls back to the previous Waypoint, and its batch 7 amendment says the Leg is ridden again. This option applies both without riding at night. The ground the day won is lost, and the rations are already spent. Option 1 (stay) breaks ADR-0009's fall-back. Option 3 (fall back and camp again) charges one night twice and can chain night Titan Engagements with no Leg between them. The owner chose the recommendation. Figures: none; no Expedition case.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0009 `## Amended` (batch 8, 8-35 and 8-37). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 7 section 7.1 (plan R05: lines 147, 213 to 214); `data/expedition/legs.yaml` (`night_camp.steps`, `leg_steps.titan-engagement`); `data/expedition/hazards.yaml` row `abnormal-at-night` only if its text names the retreat. Package D. Packet: 3797 and the Night Camp procedure (package P). Simulator: none.

### 8-38: the playtest configuration row; Recruit brings no one while it holds (final review D4, R06; amends 7-11)

- **Decision:** `REVIEW-FIX-PLAN.md` D4, option 1, **a data row**, as the plan words it: "`squadmates.yaml` gains `playtest_configuration: {player_characters: 4, squadmates: 0}`, tagged Playtest rule. While it holds, Recruit brings no Squadmate and is not offered, so the Squad Action is Honoring the Fallen. Squadmates stay the default rule (7-11)."
  - Promotion stays as 7-11 wrote it: a dead player character is replaced by a new character. While the row holds, no Squadmate serves, so no one is promoted.
  - The rules that read owner item 7's "A now" cite the row: the Straggler victims (7-14), the timed rounds (R30, `round.yaml` `round_time`), and Formation Drill's note (R45).
  - `squad-recruit` (8-28) stays a tracked value, changed by nothing while the row holds.
- **Why:** Owner item 7 ("A now") is read by rules at the table, so under ADR-0003 and ADR-0012 it belongs in data. Option 2 (a packet note) leaves the chapter and the YAML contradicting the packet, and the packet must not change a rule. Option 3 (Recruit left live) abandons the owner's configuration after the first Downtime. During the playtest, no new faces fill the ranks. The owner chose the recommendation. Figures: none; ADR-0014's reference Squad is unchanged, and its batch 7 paragraph already records that the first playtest runs 4 player characters alone.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** new **Playtest configuration**. Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** `data/character/squadmates.yaml` (`playtest_configuration`), Chapter 2 section 2.10 (package C, first); `data/campaign/downtime.yaml` `squad_actions.recruit`, Chapter 7 lines 255 and 313 (package D, citing C's landed wording); `round.yaml` `round_time` (R30, package B); `talents.yaml` Formation Drill (R45, package C). Packet: 916, 920, 3272, 3747 (package P). Simulator: none.

### 8-39: a fight nested in a Leg or a Night Camp has its own once-per uses (final review D5, R07; amends 7-3's guardrail 8 reading)

- **Decision:** `REVIEW-FIX-PLAN.md` D5, option 1, **its own use**, as the plan words it: "A Titan Engagement or Skirmish that begins inside a Leg or a Night Camp is its own procedure, with its own use. A use in it does not spend the outer one, and the outer one does not spend it."
  - Every limit in `talent_rules.limit_terms` counts per procedure. The rule is stated once, in `limit_terms`; `legs.yaml` `drives_and_talents` and `skirmish.yaml` `drives_and_talents` cite it.
  - OQ-36's reading stands: a once-per-Titan-Engagement limit reaches another procedure only where that procedure extends it, and each procedure it reaches keeps its own count.
- **Why:** The simulator measured every once-per-Titan-Engagement Talent with a fresh use in each fight (report section 7.1), so this option keeps the fight as measured (ADR-0014). Under option 2 (one shared use), a Talent spent on a Leg roll would be missing from the fight that follows, a case nothing measured. Counting per procedure is what `limit_terms` already says. The fight itself is unchanged, so the game is no less grim. The owner chose the recommendation. Figures: none move.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 8, 8-39 and 8-40). Applied. ADR-0016 guardrail 8 is unchanged in text.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** `data/character/talents.yaml` `talent_rules.limit_terms` (package C, first); `data/expedition/legs.yaml` `drives_and_talents`, `data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml` `drives_and_talents.once_per_titan_engagement`, Chapter 7 line 100 (package D, citing C's landed wording). Packet: 3078, 3569 (package P). Simulator: none.

### 8-40: one event's Fear Rolls are read from a snapshot (final review D6, R14; amends 7-8)

- **Decision:** `REVIEW-FIX-PLAN.md` D6, option 1, **snapshot**, as the plan words it: "For one event, find every rolling soldier's total from their Stress and Resolve as they stood when the event resolved. Then apply every result, Drive decisions first. No result of this event changes another soldier's total for it. 'The order changes nothing' stays."
  - 7-8's contagion (`stress-gain-nearby`) lands with the event's other results, after every total is found, and counts on the next event.
  - The snapshot covers everything a total reads, including a comrade's Resolve that a Talent reads (Unshaken Command) and a Scar a result gives.
  - Grief still applies after every Fear Roll a death causes and every Drive decision (`data/mind/grief.yaml`, unchanged).
- **Why:** It keeps the rule the YAML and packet already print ("everyone who rolls for one event can roll together"), so a Grab round stays quick, and it reads values fixed at one moment. Option 2 (a fixed order) adds a sheet order to every event with several witnesses. Option 3 (contagion last) leaves Scars, and Talents that read a comrade's Resolve, dependent on order. Contagion still lands, and it counts on the next event. The owner chose the recommendation. Figures: the engine applies contagion at once, in witness order (`engine.py` `fear()`), and a snapshot can only lower later witnesses' totals, so deaths fall or hold. The Grab cells do not move, since each has one comrade witness or none.
- **Simulator (owner, simulator option (b); `OWNER-DECISIONS.md`, *After the full rerun*):** no full rerun. After the engine change (`fear()` finds every witness's total before any result of the event applies), in-memory checks on the committed seeds and case sizes, against the committed figures: the Medium reference start (deaths through the end and during the fight, and Critical Injuries), the first-Titan-Engagement row, and the Sprinting Abnormal's bar in both orders. The engine change and its figures are named in the report's engine changes. Every file the review fixes change gets a `report.SNAPSHOT_CHANGE_NOTES` entry. A checked row that moves beyond sampling, or a Medium band past 0.08, brings back one full rerun, and a figure past 0.08 beyond the second seed goes to the owner (8-31).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0014 `## Amended` (batch 8, 8-39 and 8-40). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** `data/mind/fear-rolls.yaml` `limits.timing`; `data/harm/effect-types.yaml` `stress-gain-nearby`; Chapter 3 section 3.12 (package A). Packet: 1677, 1695 (package P). `tools/sim/engine.py` and the checks above belong to the simulator step (`IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md` step 8), not to package A.

### 8-41: a pinned limb lost to corpse heat stays pinned (final review D7, R21; confirms 8-9 and 8-17)

- **Decision:** `REVIEW-FIX-PLAN.md` D7, option 1, **still pinned**, as the plan words it: "A soldier whose pinned arm or leg is lost at the 13-or-more row stays limb-pinned. The freeing list stays closed (8-9, 8-17)." The packet's feedback page adds "limbs lost to corpse heat" beside pins and Burn Critical Injuries.
  - The pin keeps its pinning Body Part (8-17). Heave and cutting that Body Part still free the soldier, and the lost-limb grade applies at once.
- **Why:** It changes no decided list and no simulator counter, and it is the grim reading: the arm burns away and the body still holds the stump. The playtest count then tells the owner whether it is too harsh; option 3 (cap the heat) would decide that in advance. Option 2 (freed by the loss) creates a freeing route the simulator cannot count, since lost limbs are unmeasured (8-13). The owner chose the recommendation. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** `data/engagement/titan-harm.yaml` `falling_titan` (one sentence at `corpse_heat` or `freeing`, citing this item; package B); Chapter 5 section 5.7 the same sentence. Packet: the feedback page line (package P). Simulator: none.

### 8-42: the default limit on three Talents, and Sure Hands unlimited (final review D8, R34; applies 7-3's guardrail 8)

- **Decision:** `REVIEW-FIX-PLAN.md` D8, option 3, **the default on the three that live inside one procedure**, as the plan words it: "Quick Refit and Rescue Ride once per Titan Engagement, and Stay With the Column once per Leg. Sure Hands keeps no limit, with a design note."
  - `limit` reads `once_per_titan_engagement` on `quick-refit` and `rescue-ride`, and `once_per_leg` on `stay-with-the-column`.
  - Sure Hands' design note: guardrail 8 is a default. Sure Hands also reaches aftermath rolls and care windows, where `once_per_titan_engagement` would forbid it outright, and its second Push already costs Stress Dice.
- **Why:** Guardrail 8 is a default. The three change an action's cost or a roll count inside one procedure, so the default fits them. For Sure Hands, the limit would be a bigger change than a limit. Option 1 (the default on all four) forbids Sure Hands outside a fight. Option 2 (no limit on all four) drops the default where it fits. The owner chose the recommendation. Figures: none; none of the four is modelled in `tools/sim/`.
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0016 guardrail 8 reads "default", and Sure Hands' note records the exception.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** `data/character/talents.yaml` (`quick-refit`, `rescue-ride`, `stay-with-the-column` `limit`; `sure-hands` design note); Chapter 2 Appendix 2A.1 (the rendered rows and the note) (package C). Packet: the Talent reference rows (package P). Simulator: none.

## Batch 9: GM judgment, the called roll, and the Circumstances ladder

Rulings on the owner's decisions of 2026-09-16 recorded in `docs/research/gm-judgment/OWNER-DECISIONS.md` (binding), on the research in `docs/research/gm-judgment/ripple-audit.md` (the element-by-element map, E1 to E26) and `docs/research/gm-judgment/difficulty-mechanics.md` (exact odds; needs against dice), and on the ADRs, Chapter 1 sections 1.1 to 1.3, `CONTEXT.md`, and the YAML `gm:` fields the audit lists. Made 2026-09-16 by the Fable decider. Where the two research files disagree (the audit set difficulty through needs, the difficulty research through dice), the owner chose dice, and this batch follows the owner. Where the owner's answers are silent (the widening of two Talents, found supplies, off-list Requisition rows, the Parley fix), this batch decides, and `IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md` in the research folder lists the two readings the owner may still veto. Items 9-21 to 9-24, added the same day, settle the four readings the drafting packages logged while applying this batch (OQ-173 to OQ-176), and an erratum under 9-14 corrects its *Why*. Items 9-25 to 9-34, added the same day, settle the findings of the batch's first review round (`docs/reviews/gm-judgment-review-1.md` and `docs/reviews/gm-judgment-review-1-astra.md`, triaged in `docs/research/gm-judgment/REVIEW-FIX-PLAN-round-1.md`; OQ-178), with a second erratum under 9-14. Item 9-35, added the same day, records the owner's answer on the one finding that waited for them, damage a ruling names that brings a wounded soldier to 0 Health: the rule stands as written, and the GM states the soldier's current Health with the stakes (OQ-177). Items 9-36 and 9-37, added the same day by the Fable decider, settle the two findings of the batch's second review round that needed a ruling (`docs/reviews/gm-judgment-review-2.md`, Opus M1 and M3; OQ-179): a called roll inside a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, and the bounds of the route a Mission Brief sets.

The direction, in the owner's words: GM judgment "is what TTRPG is and what makes it flexible and creative and makes TTRPG special, which is having the GM help adjudicate things. We can trust the GM." It replaces Chapter 1 section 1.1 item 3 ("No dice by judgment") and the founding sentence of ADR-0003 ("GM discretion may appear as flavor but never as the rule itself"). Everything below draws one line: **the GM rules before the dice, never after them.** Whether to roll, what the roll needs from the fiction, how the circumstances tilt the pool, and what failure costs are the GM's calls; a rolled die, a table row, a Titan's card, and a Foe's open dice stand as they fell. The Titan Engagement keeps its tables, its open Titan Dice, and its tuned numbers, and the GM's judgment reaches it only through the soldiers' own pools.

Measurement: no closed number changes in this batch. Every ADR-0014 target, band, bar limit, and reported row is defined at Standard with no rulings (9-17), so the simulator is not rerun. The Skirmish probe never Parleys (`tools/sim/families.py`), so 9-14 moves no figure. The playtest logs every non-Standard step named in a Titan Engagement and every ruling-caused harm, for the post-playtest retune (OQ-140, target B).

One new ADR: ADR-0024 *The GM rules where the rules are silent; procedures stay data*, which supersedes ADR-0003 in full and carries its surviving drafting items as limits. Seven ADRs are amended: ADR-0005 (a ruling's harm is damage or a fall), ADR-0006 (the reason sentence; Circumstances under the cap), ADR-0009 (Waypoint scenes), ADR-0012 (rulings need no row; every `gm:` field names what it allows), ADR-0014 (targets at Standard; the playtest log), ADR-0016 (table-made content is unmeasured; a Talent never changes a Circumstances step), and ADR-0018 (the foe rule is the default; the rejected option reversed). ADR-0010 and ADR-0013 carry one sentence each.
