### 9-1: the called roll: Chapter 1 section 1.1 replaced (owner; answers 4 and 9)

- **Decision:** Section 1.1, retitled **When to roll, and what stands**, has six items and replaces all four of today's.
  1. **Roll when it matters.** A soldier rolls when a rule or a table result calls for a roll, or when the GM calls one: the soldier attempts something no rule covers, its outcome is in doubt, and failure would cost something. If failure would change nothing, or the soldier has the time, the tools, and the training to do it unpressed, it happens without a roll. If it cannot be done, the GM says so before anyone rolls. The GM rolls sparingly: a roll is for a moment with something at stake.
  2. **Name the roll and the stakes first.** For a called roll the GM names, before the pool is built: the Catalog entry closest to the act, from the entries marked `when_called` (9-12), or an attribute alone if none fits (Chapter 2, section 2.9); the Circumstances (9-2), Standard if the GM names none; what failure costs, from the menu of 9-5; and, where it is not obvious, what success gives. The player may change their approach or withdraw before the dice are rolled. Once the dice are rolled, none of this changes. A roll a rule calls for takes only the Circumstances from this item; its entry, its needs, its Help, and its failure are the rule's.
  3. **Results stand.** Once the dice are rolled, the GM never overrides, re-rolls, or ignores a die or a table result, and never changes the stakes. A roll changes only through a Push or through a rule that names the change. Titan Dice, a Foe's Attack Dice and Guard, the hazard and Night tables, the setup table, and every table roll are made in the open. The one hidden roll, the Next Behavior, is rolled once and stands (Chapter 5).
  4. **Trying again.** In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, a soldier can attempt an action again on a later turn. Outside one, a rule that says a roll is never tried again is final. Otherwise a soldier tries the same thing again only when something has changed: a new approach, a better tool, another soldier, or time spent at a cost the GM names from the menu of 9-5. Each soldier gets one attempt at a given obstacle per changed situation.
  5. **Secret rolls.** The GM rolls out of sight only for passive noticing (9-10). Every other roll is made where the table can see it.
  6. **Where the GM does not rule.** The list of 9-9, in one paragraph.
  The Push, Cover, and Help rules of sections 1.5 and 1.8 apply to a called roll as to any attribute roll: every called roll can be Pushed and Covered, and Help on one outside a Titan Engagement is as the GM rules (9-12).
- **Why:** The owner's direction, with both research files' recommendations that the owner did not contradict: rolls only with stakes (Alien p. 42, Coriolis p. 47), stakes stated first (Coriolis p. 250), results that stand (both files reject Coriolis's permission to adjust a result), retries gated on change (Coriolis p. 66), secret rolls for passive detection only (Alien p. 44). The stakes-first rule is the brake on roll proliferation and on the Stress the extra Pushes would grind; each failed called roll pays what was staked, which is what makes a chain of attempts costly. Figures: none; called rolls were never measured (OQ-168 asks the playtest to count them).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 (new); ADR-0003 (superseded). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** new **Called roll**, **Stakes**, **Ruling**. Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 section 1.1 (whole); section 1.3 step 1 (the called roll's entry); Chapter 2 section 2.9 (9-12); `data/core/dice-pool.yaml` (a `called_roll` block: who calls, what is named first, what stands); the packet's `core-dice` section (9-19).

### 9-2: Circumstances: the seven-step ladder (owner; answers 2 and 3)

- **Decision:** Every attribute roll has **Circumstances**: the one step of a seven-step ladder that the GM names for the situation the roll is made in. If the GM names none, they are Standard.

  | Circumstances | Effect on the pool |
  |---|---|
  | Effortless | +3 Bonus Dice |
  | Routine | +2 Bonus Dice |
  | Easy | +1 Bonus Die |
  | Standard | nothing |
  | Hard | a 1-die penalty |
  | Harsh | a 2-die penalty |
  | Desperate | a 3-die penalty |

  Rules of the ladder, each closed:
  1. **One step per roll.** The GM weighs everything about the moment and names one step. Rain, dark, and a snapped strap are one judgment, not three. Named with the stakes, before Bonus Dice are declared (section 1.3, step 1), so the player can answer it with Help.
  2. **Plus steps are Bonus Dice.** Effortless, Routine, and Easy are one source, `circumstances`, in `bonus-dice-sources.yaml`, and count toward the cap of 4 with Help, Openings, and the rest (ADR-0006). The roller chooses under the cap as today; a plus step the cap leaves out is not spent, since it spends nothing.
  3. **Minus steps are penalties.** Hard, Harsh, and Desperate go through section 1.3, step 5, as one more named penalty: after the Bonus Dice cap, base dice only, adding up with any penalty the soldier carries from a Critical Injury, a Stress Response, or a Fear result, never taking the base dice below 1, and never removing Gear Dice or Stress Dice. However hard the task, a soldier always rolls at least one base die, so a six can always save them.
  4. **Order of pool building** stays section 1.3's: name the roll (with the Circumstances and the stakes), attribute, Talent, Bonus Dice (the plus step among them, capped at 4), penalties (the minus step among them, floored at 1 base die), Gear Dice, Stress Dice.
  5. **Which rolls.** Any attribute roll a soldier or a Squadmate makes: a called roll, a Catalog action, a Reaction, and every roll a rule calls for (the Leg roll, the camp roll, a Requisition, a Sneak, a Parley, Treat Injury, Field Repair, Fly, Leap Clear, a Nape strike, a dodge). A group roll takes one step on its roller's pool. Never: a roll that is not an attribute roll (a Gas Roll, the infirmary roll, a fall roll, any table roll); the Death Roll; the performance roll and the Graduation Exam's Trials (creation, before play); Titan Dice, a Foe's Attack Dice, and Guard (rolled in the open and never adjusted, ADR-0019, ADR-0018). Each excluded roll's `roll_exceptions` row or own file says `circumstances: never`.
  6. **Too easy or impossible.** A task easier than Effortless is not rolled: it happens. A task the GM judges impossible is not rolled: it does not. Anything possible is at worst Desperate. Routine and Effortless exist mainly for a soldier who carries penalties: an injured soldier doing something simple rolls Routine and keeps their dice.
  7. **Circumstances never change a need** (9-3).
  8. **Written before Bonus Dice, applied where the pool step falls.** The step is a fact of the roll like its entry: a Talent, a Push, or a Cover never changes it (9-13).
- **Why:** The owner chose dice over needs and the full Coriolis width. The difficulty research's odds carry the rest: each die removed multiplies the chance of failing by 6/5 whatever the pool, so a step means the same thing for the Medic with 3 dice and the Slayer with 9, which a raised need never does (needs 2 costs about 3 dice at pool 4 and 7 at pool 12); one Help, one Opening, or one Talent level cancels one step, which is the teamwork answer ADR-0010 wants; the floor keeps luck alive at the weakest pools (16.7% unPushed, about 2 in 5 Pushed); extra successes keep flowing to the eight rules that spend them; and a Hard dodge costs 14.5 points of success and 12 points of Push rate where needs 2 would double the Push rate and the Stress economy with it. Plus steps sit under the cap so that the GM's generosity cannot stack past teamwork's ceiling (ADR-0006). Penalties after the cap keep today's order: a penalty shrinks the pool, not what comrades can add. The names: Effortless, Routine, and Easy are Coriolis's (the owner said "as Coriolis"); Hard, Harsh, and Desperate are the field's register and escalate in plain words. "Difficulty" is not used as a term: Severity and Attack Dice both list it under Avoid, and the owner's "adjust difficulty" is what the ladder does, not what it is called. "Circumstances" is unused anywhere in the rules and YAML today and names exactly what the owner described (flying in the rain). Figures: the difficulty research's tables 2.2 and 2.5; nothing new measured.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 (new); ADR-0006 `## Amended` (Circumstances under the cap; a plus step is a Bonus Dice source). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** new **Circumstances** (with Avoid: difficulty, modifier, DC, target number, advantage, disadvantage); Bonus Dice (Circumstances named among the sources). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 section 1.3 (step 1 names the Circumstances; step 4 lists the plus step; step 5 lists the minus step), a new section **1.4a Circumstances** placed between 1.3 and 1.4 (the ladder, the eight rules, one example), section 1.7 (the source), section 1.10 (Titan Dice take none); Chapter 2 section 2.4 (the Trials take none), 2.9; Chapter 3 sections 3.4 (the Death Roll takes none), 3.5 (Treat Injury may); Chapter 4 sections 4.3 (the Gas Roll takes none), 4.6 (Field Repair may); Chapter 5 sections 5.1 and 5.5 (9-4); Chapter 7's "Every roll" tables gain one line under each table: "Circumstances as Chapter 1 gives; Scarcity, Grit, Watch, and the ledger are needs and unchanged". New `data/core/circumstances.yaml` (the ladder as data: steps, dice, the one-step rule, the exclusions, the Titan Engagement default of 9-4, `adrs: [ADR-0024, ADR-0006]`); `bonus-dice-sources.yaml` (the `circumstances` row: `dice_per_unit` 1 to 3 by step, `max_units` 1, `applies_to` every attribute roll not excluded, `spends` nothing, `declared_by` the GM with the stakes); `dice-pool.yaml` (the `penalty` component names Circumstances as a source; `roll_exceptions` rows for the Death Roll and the performance roll gain `circumstances: never`); `data/character/graduation-exam.yaml`, `data/skirmish/foes.yaml`, `data/engagement/titan-format.yaml` (an exclusion sentence each).

### 9-3: needs come only from the fiction (research, both files)

- **Decision:** A roll needs 1 success unless its rule names a number, and every such number is a quantity in the fiction or a tuned value: Nape Depth, Toughness, a Foe group's `parley` value (9-14) and Watch, Scarcity plus the ledger, Break Attention's escalation, Break Free's 2, Call It's 2, the Exam thresholds, and a Titan's or Foe's successes against a Reaction. The GM never raises or lowers a need: not for a called roll (always 1), not for a written roll. Circumstances layer dice on a need and never touch it: a Nape strike against Depth 4 at Hard still needs 4 successes from one fewer die, which the Squad answers with Openings. The audit's "needs by the ask, 1, 2, or 3" for social rolls is not adopted; a called Persuade needs 1 and the size of the ask is Circumstances. Section 1.4 gains one sentence that says so.
- **Why:** The owner chose dice. The difficulty research's section 2.3 shows why the two must not mix: a need of 2 is harsher than any dice step at every real pool and makes the same task a different difficulty for every soldier; the closed needs above 1 were all tuned or are quantities the Squad builds toward, and converting them to dice is impossible at small pools (the Rookie's Break Attention at needs 2 cannot be reproduced by any penalty because the floor stops at 68%). Two languages, both already in the rules: a quantity in the world sets what you need; the circumstances set what you roll. Figures: research section 2.3 and 2.6.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 (the needs limit). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none (Nape Depth, Toughness, Grit, Watch, Scarcity, and Ledger already read as needs).
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 section 1.4 item 2 (one sentence); Chapter 7 sections 7.3 and 7.4 (a pointer under each roll table).

### 9-4: the Titan Engagement: where the owner's "everywhere" reaches (owner; answer 1, interpreted)

- **Decision:** In a Titan Engagement the GM's ad hoc adjustment is the Circumstances of 9-2 on the soldiers' own rolls, and nothing else.
  1. **Standard is the core assumption.** The Anchor Rating, Positions, Openings, a grounded Titan, Call It, the Attention restriction, and every penalty a soldier carries already price the fight. The GM names another step only for something no rule already prices: rain on the rooftops, smoke over a burning district, full dark, ice, a gale. The chapter gives those examples and no exhaustive list, as the owner asked.
  2. **Which rolls.** Every attribute roll a soldier or Squadmate makes in the fight, the dodge and the Nape strike included, under the same default. A step named for a condition applies to every roll that condition touches while it lasts, and the GM says so once (rain makes every ODM roll Hard until it stops), rather than re-judging each roll.
  3. **What the adjustment never touches.** A Titan's Attack Dice and its Titan Dice roll; which entry a Behavior Table resolves and against whom; the Next Behavior once rolled; the Attention Ladder and who holds Attention; the Reaction procedure, the whiff, the rider, and the Grab countdown; Nape Depth, Toughness, Regeneration, and the Heave rating; the Openings an entry creates; steam, the falling Titan, and corpse heat; the Critical Injury, Stress Response, Fear Roll, Scar, Grief, and Death Roll tables; the retreat clock's length; the end tests. A condition that would slow or blind a Titan reaches the table only through the soldiers' dice (a Titan mired to the knee makes the dodge Easy; it does not lose Attack Dice).
  4. **Framing** is 9-15: which Titan, the Anchor Rating, and Background Titans, as a Mission Brief could name them, with the setup table as the default.
  5. **Improvised acts** are 9-8.
- **Why:** The owner opened the Titan Engagement to difficulty and gave one example, flying in the rain, which is a soldier's roll. Every reading of "difficulty" in the owner's answers, in the research, and in the glossary is a property of the soldier's pool; a Titan's card has no difficulty, it has a roll. Reaching past the soldiers' pools would cost four things the design rests on and the owner has not asked to spend: dread (a Titan the GM steers is a combatant, which ADR-0001 rejected); planning (Break Attention, decoys, and Hook and Cut are plans against a public ladder, and a GM pick turns planning into guessing); tuning (every ADR-0014 target and Chapter 6 band lives in the Titan's rolls and tables); and trust (open Titan Dice that stand are the rule players test hardest, ADR-0019). Keeping the adjustment on the soldiers' side honours the owner's words in full, since rain, smoke, and dark all act on soldiers, and keeps the simulator's baseline meaningful at Standard (9-17). The dodge is included because the owner said everywhere and because excluding it would make the rain reach the Fly and not the dodge made with the same gear in the same rain; its lethality cost is real (research section 2.5: a Hard dodge loses 14.5 points of success) and is logged as OQ-167 for the playtest and the retune, not tuned now. Figures: research section 2.5 (the Rookie dodge at -1 to -3).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 (the Titan limit); ADR-0014 `## Amended` (9-17); ADR-0010 `## Amended` (one sentence: no ruling picks Attention or a target). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 preface (the Rulings paragraph of 9-11), section 5.1 (framing, 9-15), section 5.5 (the dodge takes Circumstances; the Titan's roll takes none); Chapter 6 preface (9-11); `data/core/circumstances.yaml` (`in_titan_engagement`: default Standard, examples, the never list); `data/engagement/titan-format.yaml` (`never` gains "and never take Circumstances").

### 9-5: the cost of failure: the closed menu (owner; answer 5)

- **Decision:** A failed roll does what its rule states. A failed called roll, or a retry the GM prices, costs what the GM staked before the roll, from this menu and no other:
  1. **It does not happen.** The default, always available, and often enough on its own.
  2. **It happens at a cost,** or it does not happen and the cost falls anyway. One item per failed roll:
     - **Stress:** the soldier gains 1 Stress (a new `ruling` row in `stress-changes.yaml`, amount 1, one per failed roll, never on a success).
     - **A fall:** by Chapter 4's procedure, with the GM naming the band from the fall's height; the `rule-named` cause reads "or a ruling". Low if the GM names none.
     - **Damage:** 1 to 3, of a named Injury Type (Crush for a blow or a fall's kin, Cut for an edge, Pierce for a point, Burn for fire), through `health.yaml`'s damage kind. Never 4 or more: 4 is a musket, and a musket is a Foe with a roll. Damage reaches the Critical Injury tables only at 0 Health, as all human harm does (ADR-0005).
     - **A lost item or unit:** one carried item lost (dropped in the river, left behind), or 1 unit of one kind of Squad Supply spent. Never wear: wear is the Push's price (ADR-0004).
     - **Time or position:** the window closes, the patrol arrives, the door is barred, the Squad is noticed, a Skirmish begins by the fiction, a Titan Engagement begins by the fiction (9-15). A change in the situation, described, that the next roll must reckon with.
  3. **Never:** a Critical Injury dealt directly, a location, a row, a time limit, a Death Roll, a Scar, Grief, a Fear Roll (9-6 is its own trigger, not a cost), Stress relief, a Health box, healing time, a care window opened or closed, a Titan's or Foe's roll, or a table result.
  4. **Found supplies.** On a successful called search (Spot or Survive), the GM may add at most 1 unit per success of one kind of Squad Supply the place plausibly holds; `squad-supply.yaml` `spending` gains "or added by a ruling on a called search, at most 1 unit per success". Nothing else adds units by ruling.
  5. **Failure moves the story on.** The chapter says so and the GM's Guide expands it: a failure should complicate, not halt.
- **Why:** The owner said yes to a fixed menu and never a direct Critical Injury. The menu is the audit's E10 list, trimmed: damage stops at 3 because a ruling that puts a Health 4 Rookie at 0 and rolls a Critical Injury is the musket's job, and the musket is rolled; a Fear Roll is not a cost because five Scars retire a soldier and a cost the GM names on every failure would be a Scar faucet (9-6 gives it a trigger with its own brake). Stress relief, healing, and care windows stay rules-only: rulings may cost a soldier, only rules heal one (ADR-0008's spiral depends on it). Found supplies are capped so that scarcity, which both parents name as the GM's job (Alien p. 152, Coriolis p. 250), survives a good search roll. Figures: none; the playtest tags every harm a ruling caused (9-17).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0005 `## Amended` (a ruling's harm is damage of 1 to 3 or a fall, never a Critical Injury). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none (Stakes, 9-1, names the menu).
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 section 1.1 item 2 (the menu, printed once); Chapter 3 section 3.1 (harm a ruling names as stakes, with the ceiling of 3); Chapter 4 section 4.6 (a fall a ruling names), 4.10 (a unit staked; found supplies); `data/core/stress-changes.yaml` (`ruling` gain row); `data/harm/health.yaml` (`damage.named_by` gains "a ruling, 1 to 3, typed"); `data/gear/falls.yaml` (`rule-named` reads a ruling; the `gm:` field of 9-11); `data/gear/squad-supply.yaml` (`spending`).

### 9-6: GM-called Fear Rolls outside a Titan Engagement (owner; answer 6)

- **Decision:** A new trigger row, `gm-horror`, in `fear-rolls.yaml`: the GM may call a Fear Roll for an event outside a Titan Engagement that is at least as horrifying as a listed trigger (a village devoured, a comrade's remains found, a Titan seen tearing through a breach from the Wall). Who rolls: every soldier who witnesses it, as the GM names them. `can_arise_outside_titan_engagement: true`; never inside a Titan Engagement, where the closed list governs and the Grab cells and the deaths bands read Fear results. The row's results are the table's, resolved as any Fear Roll, with Resolve and the Drive shrug-off. An event a listed trigger already covers uses its own row and never `gm-horror` too. The once-per-scene limit is not a rule: the GM's Guide advises one at most in a scene and none for a horror the soldiers have already rolled for that day, and the chapter says "rarely". A called roll counts as the soldier's own act for a Drive's act test (`enlistment.yaml` `drive_rules.acts` gains the called roll on the named entry), so a soldier acting on their Drive can shrug off a GM-called result as any other.
- **Why:** The owner said yes and left the cap to discretion, which reads as no hard cap. The brakes that remain are closed: the trigger must match a listed trigger's weight, the closed list governs every Titan Engagement (so no measured Fear figure moves), and the Drive shrug-off applies. Alien lists a truly horrifying event as a GM panic trigger (`research-alien-evolved.md`), so the parent supports it. Fear rows at high totals give Scars, and five Scars retire a soldier; OQ-169 asks the playtest to count GM-called Fear Rolls and the Scars they gave. Figures: none moved; the Fear table is unchanged.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 (ADR-0003 item 1 rewritten: the closed list plus `gm-horror` outside a Titan Engagement). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Fear Roll ("or that the GM names outside a Titan Engagement"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 3 section 3.12 (the trigger, who rolls, the advice); Chapter 2 section 2.5 (the Drive sentence); `data/mind/fear-rolls.yaml` (`gm-horror` row; `event_titan` reads "none, or the nearest Titan the GM names if one is in sight" for the draw-attention effects); `data/character/enlistment.yaml` (`drive_rules.acts`).

### 9-7: human Foes: the GM runs people, with a reason (owner; answer 7)

- **Decision:**
  1. **The foe rule is the default** and the Skirmish probe's baseline. On a Foe's turn the GM may instead direct its target or its action when the fiction gives that Foe a reason, and says the reason aloud before the Foe acts ("the sergeant goes for the medic who shot his man"; "the last bandit runs"). A reason the table cannot hear is not a reason. A Foe directed by ruling still takes only the actions the foe list gives it and its kind's weapons.
  2. **Closed:** a Foe's Attack Dice and Guard, rolled in the open from the Foe row and never adjusted (no Circumstances on Foe dice; Circumstances on a soldier's Fight, Shoot, and Reaction as 9-2); damage and Net Successes; the kill-or-out-cold rule by Injury Type; a Foe never Pushes, Helps, or Covers; a Foe never attacks a Down soldier (8-30); a Held Foe's rule.
  3. **Grit is the latest a group breaks.** The GM may break a group, or have it surrender, sooner, with a reason said aloud (the officer falls; the sergeant sees the flare); never later. A failed threat Parley's +1 Grit stands.
  4. **The Sneak for the Ambush.** The GM rules whether the Squad can approach unseen at all; if so, the roll is made against Watch as written, with Circumstances.
  5. **Table-made Foe kinds** use the Foe row fields, with Attack Dice and Guard no higher than the trooper's 7 and 4, and are played as unmeasured; none ships in the first playtest.
- **Why:** The owner said yes. People act with reasons and Titans without: that split is the design's best idea kept (ADR-0001) and judgment placed where it reads as intelligence. Skirmish lethality is a reported figure, not an ADR-0014 target, and both parents run NPCs by ruling (Alien p. 157; Coriolis p. 48). The stated-reason rule and the Down ban carry trust: "the GM focused me" has an answer the table heard, and the one ruling that would feel like an execution is impossible. Figures: the Skirmish probe's three ranges are the foe-rule baseline and unchanged (8-33).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0018 `## Amended` (the foe rule is the default; the GM may direct a Foe with a stated reason; Grit the latest break; the Considered Option "GM-chosen targets and morale" reversed). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Foe ("run by the foe rule unless the GM directs it with a stated reason"); Grit ("the latest point at which a group breaks"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 7 preface (9-11), section 7.4 (the foe rule's preface; *Grit and breaking*; the Ambush's approach; the "Every roll" table's Sneak row); `data/skirmish/foes.yaml` (`foe_rule` preface: default, direction with a reason, what stays closed; a `directed_by_ruling` note; the trooper cap for table-made kinds); `data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml` (`gm:` field, 9-11; `grit.breaking` "at the latest"; `sneak-for-the-ambush` "if the GM rules the approach possible").

### 9-8: improvised acts in a fight (owner; answer 8)

- **Decision:** In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish a soldier may attempt an act no entry covers. The GM names, before the pool is built, the one existing entry whose effect the act imitates (the **model entry**), and that ruling stands. The act spends the soldier's action, is rolled with the model entry's attribute, gear items, Talents, and needs, takes Circumstances as any roll, and on success produces only the model entry's effect at that entry's size: a wagon cut loose is a decoy under Break Attention's needs; a swung lantern is Draw Attention, never from Distant; a chimney dropped on a leg is a Body Part strike whose successes count toward that leg's Toughness; a thrown rope is Help or Lift Comrade; a hurled object at a person is a Fight or Shoot at damage 1 (the weapons table's "any object that is not a gear item"). It carries the model entry's restrictions (the Attention holder still cannot strike the Nape; a Feint's Position rule; a Foe's Guard still rolls). It never kills a Titan, never changes Nape Depth, Toughness, Attack Dice, or a card, never cancels or delays a card, never creates more Openings than the model entry could, and never gives an effect no entry has. `uncatalogued_actions` gains an `in_a_fight` step that says this; section 2.9's tracked-values list becomes the in-fight effect menu (9-12).
- **Why:** The owner said yes: copy an existing effect, never a kill. This is the audit's E17 with Circumstances now allowed (the owner opened the Titan Engagement to them). It opens creativity while reusing effect sizes the simulator has measured, so every ADR-0014 figure stays valid: a decoy is a Break Attention row and a flag is a Draw Attention row. The model-entry rule closes reskinning (a "falling beam" that is really a free Nape cut), and the Nape-only kill is canon and charter (ADR-0007, ADR-0016 item 4). Figures: none moved.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 (item 9 rewritten). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 section 1.9 (*Turns and actions*: an action is Help, one Catalog entry, or an improvised act under a model entry); Chapter 2 section 2.9 (the fight clause); Chapter 5 section 5.4 (one paragraph with the three Titan examples); Chapter 7 section 7.4 (one sentence with the object example); `data/character/action-catalog.yaml` (`uncatalogued_actions.in_a_fight`).

### 9-9: the rules the GM applies as written (audit section 3, adjusted by answer 1)

- **Decision:** Chapter 1 section 1.1 item 6 prints this list once, and every chapter's Rulings paragraph (9-11) points to it. The GM applies these as written and rules on none of them:
  - the Push procedure, which no ruling forbids or forces (only a written rule or a roll exception does), Covering, and Help's effect and cap;
  - the sources of Bonus Dice (Circumstances is the one source a ruling names) and the cap of 4; the penalty rule; needs (9-3);
  - the fixed rolls and their tables: the Fear Roll, the Stress Response roll, the Gas Roll, the Death Roll, the performance roll, the fall roll, the infirmary roll;
  - the Critical Injury, Stress Response, Scar, and Grief tables, Down, Death Rolls, healing, and care windows; Stress relief, which comes only from a written trigger;
  - gear wear, Standard Issue, the Funding gate, the ledger, and one Requisition per Downtime;
  - the Behavior Tables, the Attention Ladder, Attack Dice and Titan Dice, the Reaction procedure, the Nape-only kill, Openings sizes, steam, the falling Titan, corpse heat, the Grab countdown, Regeneration, the retreat clock, and the end tests;
  - the standard Titans' numbers and the Read-gating of hidden values;
  - the Leg roll, the hazard and Night tables and their modifiers, "always advance", rations, and the Straggler's random victim;
  - Squadmates, which the players direct (the GM may voice one);
  - results stand (9-1, item 3); the Next Behavior once rolled.
- **Why:** The audit's closed list, with the Titan Engagement's soldier rolls opened to Circumstances by the owner's answer 1 and everything Titan-side kept closed by 9-4. Each entry is a tuned number, a table the design measures, a player lever (the Push), or a trust rule (open dice, the random victim). Printing it once keeps the chapters from each carrying a different list. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 (the limits). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 section 1.1 item 6; the packet's GM section (9-19).

### 9-10: secret rolls for passive noticing (owner; answer 9)

- **Decision:** The GM may roll out of sight only a **passive roll**: a Spot or Size Up against something the soldier does not know is there, outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish. The GM builds the soldier's pool as it stands (attribute, the Talent that names the entry, Gear Dice if the entry allows, Stress Dice) with the Circumstances the GM names to themselves; no Bonus Dice, no Help, no Cover, no Push, and a Stress Die showing 1 does nothing, since the soldier does not know they rolled. The soldier learns the result only through what they notice. A `passive-roll` row in `dice-pool.yaml` `roll_exceptions` carries this (`bonus_sources_excluded: all`, `push_allowed: false`, `stress_response: none`, `secret: true`). Every other roll is made in the open, and a soldier who says they are looking rolls Spot in the open as a called roll.
- **Why:** The owner said yes, passive noticing only. Alien p. 44 is the model: a roll the character is unaware of cannot be Pushed and triggers no Stress Response, and the GM may make it in secret. Kept out of fights because everything there is public by design and the Read is a player lever with a Tactician list built on it. The Push and the Stress Response are the roll's two costs, and a soldier cannot pay a cost for a roll they do not know about. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none (Called roll's entry names the passive roll).
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 section 1.1 item 5, section 1.5 (the cannot-Push list gains the passive roll), section 1.6 (no Stress Response from one); `data/core/dice-pool.yaml` (`passive-roll` row).

### 9-11: the "No rulings" paragraphs and the `gm:` fields (audit sections 3 and 7)

- **Decision:** Every chapter's "No rulings" paragraph becomes a **Rulings** paragraph in the same place with the same shape: first what the GM rules in that chapter's scope (with the batch item), then what the GM applies as written, closing with "the list in Chapter 1, section 1.1, item 6". Every `gm:`, `gm_choices:`, and `gm_rulings:` field in YAML either names the ruling it allows or reads `none` and cites ADR-0024. The drafter writes the words; the rule for each is:

  | Where | Rules | Stands |
  |---|---|---|
  | Ch2 2.9 (`02:611`); `action-catalog.yaml` `gm_rulings` | the called roll and its entry, the model entry of an improvised act (9-1, 9-8, 9-12) | the steps as the default; Talents name entries; needs |
  | Ch2 2.10 (`02:710`); `squadmates.yaml:103` | none; the GM may voice a Squadmate | players direct Squadmates |
  | Ch3 3.1 (`03:37`); `health.yaml:27` | damage of 1 to 3 or a fall as stakes (9-5); the `gm-horror` trigger (9-6) | the kind's parameters, the Injury Location, the type's row, every table, Down, Death Rolls, healing |
  | Ch3 3.2 (`03:110`) | none | the type is the rule's |
  | Ch4 4.1 (`04:37`); `standard-issue.yaml:145`; `falls.yaml:104` | a fall's band as stakes; a staked item or unit; found supplies (9-5); an off-list Requisition row (9-16) | wear, Standard Issue, what an object counts as (an improvised object gives Circumstances, never Gear Dice) |
  | Ch4 4.10 (`04:609`); `squad-supply.yaml:45` | a staked unit; found supplies, capped | every other spend and add |
  | Ch5 preface (`05:65`); `05:893`; `engagement-flow.yaml:160`; `behavior-procedure.yaml:92, :122`; `attention.yaml:214`; `anchor-ratings.yaml:117`; `titan-harm.yaml:148, :218, :408`; `titan-format.yaml:64`; `squad-tactics.yaml:30`; `engagement-setup.yaml:71` | Circumstances on the soldiers' rolls (9-4); framing (9-15); the model entry of an improvised act (9-8) | Attention, the behavior and its target, moves and landings, Regeneration, entries, the end, the Squad Tactic conditions, the setup rows once rolled, the Titan's roll |
  | Ch6 preface (`06:34`) | which Titan appears and its Background Titans, as framing (9-15) | which entry resolves and against whom; the standard numbers; Read-gating |
  | Ch7 preface (`07:35`); `hazards.yaml:21`; `skirmish.yaml:396`; the foe rule preface (`07:567`) | the route and Posts as Command; Waypoint scenes (9-15); a Foe's direction with a reason; a group breaking sooner; the Ambush's approach (9-7); an off-list Requisition row and leverage (9-16) | the hazard and Night rolls and rows, the Straggler's victim, the Leg roll, whether Command grants a Requisition (the roll), Foe dice, damage, the Down ban, when a Skirmish ends by its tests |
  | `critical-injuries.yaml:117, :621`; `fear-rolls.yaml` (the triggers' `closed` note) | none; `gm-horror` is a row | as today, citing ADR-0024 |

- **Why:** The audit found six paragraphs and about fifteen fields that would contradict ADR-0024 as they stand. One shape for all of them keeps the chapters legible and lets a reader find, in each chapter, where the GM's judgment starts and stops. ADR-0012 asks for the field convention so the Foundry system reads the same answer. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0012 `## Amended`. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** as the table; every citation of "ADR-0003, item n" in Chapters 1 to 7 and `data/` reads "ADR-0024, limit n" by the map in 9-18.

### 9-12: the Catalog's `rolled` value, section 2.9, and Help on a called roll (audit E2, E6)

- **Decision:**
  1. A new `rolled` value, **`when_called`**: "rolled when a rule calls for it, or when the GM calls a roll for an act this entry fits". Nine entries take it: `spot`, `size-up`, `survive`, `persuade`, `endure`, `sneak`, `recall`, `ride`, `fly`. Six keep `when_a_rule_calls`: `fear-roll`, `stress-response-roll`, `gas-roll`, `death-roll`, `performance-roll`, `leap-clear`. `when_taken` and `never` are unchanged. `render.py`'s `ROLLED` map gains the value ("when a rule or the GM calls for it"); the site's Compendium card reads the same map when the site resyncs (out of scope, OQ-171).
  2. Each of the nine rows' `help_outside_titan_engagement` gains: "On a called roll, up to 3 comrades who are present and able to help concretely, as the GM rules; Help spends nothing unless the GM names a cost from the menu; a comrade who could Help may Cover." Chapter 1 section 1.8 *Outside a Titan Engagement* gains the same sentence for called rolls, and its silent-rule clause ("if the rule says nothing, no Help") now applies to written rolls only.
  3. **Section 2.9** keeps its steps as the default order for an act that changes a tracked value, and the tracked-values list stays as an index for Talents, the Foundry system, and the in-fight effect menu (9-8), no longer as a gate. Step 1's "changes none of them: no effect" and step 5's "no match: no effect" both become "the GM rules (Chapter 1, section 1.1): a called roll, it happens, or it cannot be done". The prayer example stays inert (Stress relief is closed); the bracing-beam example of OQ-35 becomes a called Strength roll. `uncatalogued_actions` gains a `no_value_or_no_match` step and the `in_a_fight` step of 9-8.
  4. `fly` inside a Titan Engagement keeps its Anchor Rating needs; outside one (a rooftop, a Chase when written) it is a called roll at needs 1.
- **Why:** The audit's split, with `ride` and `fly` opened outside a fight. The field and social entries become the everyday called rolls, and the unconditional dice Talents on them (Keen Eyes, Fieldcraft, Judge of Character, Silver Tongue, Book Learning, Long Haul, Quiet Step, Underground Instincts, High Vantage) now read every called roll, which is a real buff to the Tactician, Leader, and Hunter lists outside the fight and is wanted. The fixed rolls keep their old value because their tables are tuned or measured and no GM calls one (the Fear Roll has its own trigger in 9-6). Help on a called roll follows Alien p. 43 and Coriolis p. 49: present and able, GM has final say. Figures: none; ADR-0016's Talent rule says these entries carry no dice in any baseline.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 (items 9 and 12 rewritten). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Action Catalog ("and the entries a called roll is made for"); Help (the called-roll clause). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 1 section 1.8; Chapter 2 section 2.9, Appendix 2A.3 and 2A.4 (re-rendered); `data/character/action-catalog.yaml` (the `rolled` comment, nine rows, `uncatalogued_actions`); `tools/render/render.py` (`ROLLED`).

### 9-13: Talents: two conditions widened; no Talent moves a step (audit E8; difficulty research section 8)

- **Decision:** Menace's condition reads "on a roll made as a threat" (a Parley made as a threat, or a called Persuade the GM rules is a threat, rolled with Strength). Stand Down's conditions read "against a person" for both `persuade` and `size-up` (in a Skirmish or in any called social roll). Route Finder, Know the Stores, Camp Surgeon, and Wagon Master keep their procedure conditions. The naming rule stands: a Talent adds dice only to the entry the GM named for the called roll. A new Talent guardrail in ADR-0016: no Talent changes, ignores, or lowers a Circumstances step; a Talent that wants to help against bad conditions adds dice.
- **Why:** The two conditions were written when a Skirmish was the only social scene; intimidation and talking down should work in an alley too. The guardrail is the difficulty research's warning: a step is worth 20% of failure at every pool, so a Talent that erased one would be the strongest Talent in the list; adding dice is the same help in the language Talents already speak. ADR-0006's reason sentence ("deciding which Talent applies could become GM judgment") is amended to say the GM names the entry and the Talent follows, which keeps Talents computable. Figures: none (neither Talent names a measured roll).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0006 `## Amended`; ADR-0016 `## Amended` (Talent guardrail 10). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 2 Appendix 2A.1 (re-rendered); `data/character/talents.yaml` (`menace.condition`, `stand-down.condition`).

### 9-14: the Parley against the Military Police: a `parley` value per Foe kind (research finding, difficulty research section 2.6)

- **Decision:** Each Foe kind gains a `parley` field: the base successes a Parley against a group of that kind needs. The Parley need in a Skirmish reads "the group's `parley` value plus 1, minus 1 per Foe out, never below 1, plus the ask"; outside a Skirmish "the group's `parley` value plus the ask". Grit no longer sets a Parley need and keeps breaking only. Values: Bandit 1, Garrison sentry 1 (each equal to its Grit, so nothing changes for them), Military Police trooper 2 (its Grit before 8-33 raised it to 4 for breaking). The Foes table renders a Parley column. The Skirmish probe never Parleys (`tools/sim/families.py`), so no figure moves and no simulator check is needed; the YAML load and the render check suffice.
- **Why:** 8-33 raised the trooper's Grit to 4 so that a patrol fights to the last, and the Parley need rode along: Surrender needs 5 in a Skirmish, 2.0% with a Push and two helpers, and 4 outside one. That was a side effect, not a decision that the Military Police never back down; if the owner wants that, `parley` can be set high in one field and the fix stands. Decoupling puts the number in the fiction where 9-3 wants needs (how hard a group is to talk down is not how many of it must fall) and gives Phase 2 Foes a dial. At `parley` 2 the trooper's Stand Down in a Skirmish needs 3 (about 20% Pushed on the research's five-die build, near 40% with three helpers), hard and reachable, and Break Their Nerve and Size Up keep their meaning. A cap on Grit for Parley purposes was the other fix and hides the same number inside a rule. Figures: research table 2.6.
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0018 says foes break by Grit, which stands).
- **Glossary change:** Grit (the Parley clause removed); Parley ("needs the group's Parley value plus the ask"). Applied.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 7 section 7.4 (*Parley*; *Outside a Skirmish*; the "Every roll" table's two Parley rows; the Foes table); `data/skirmish/foes.yaml` (`fields.parley`; three rows; `fields.grit` loses "also the base of a Parley's needs"); `data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml` (`parley.needs`, the `parley-outside-a-skirmish` row); `tools/render/render.py` (the Foes table column).
- **Erratum (WP-G4 drafting; confirmed with 9-22):** in *Why*, "Surrender needs 5 in a Skirmish" reads "Stand down needs 5 in a Skirmish", and "the trooper's Stand Down in a Skirmish needs 3" reads "the trooper's Stand down in a Skirmish needs 3" (the ask, not the Talent). At Grit 4 the need was Grit plus 1 with no Foe out, plus the ask: Stand down 5 and Surrender 6 in a Skirmish, Stand down 4 and Surrender 5 outside one. `docs/research/gm-judgment/difficulty-mechanics.md` table 2.6 prices the need of 5 (2.0% with a Push and two helpers) on its "Grit 4 + 1" row with no ask added, which is Stand down's. No figure, value, or rule changes, and no other file changes: Chapter 7 section 7.4's design note and `data/skirmish/foes.yaml` already read Stand down.
- **Erratum 2 (review round 1, Opus m10; 9-34, item 5):** in *Why*, "about 20% Pushed on the research's five-die build, near 40% with three helpers" reads "28% Pushed with two helpers and 37% with three, for a soldier with Empathy 3 and Stress 1 who Pushes when short (12% alone)". The "five-die build" named the research's two-helper pool at need 3, whose figure is 28.0%, not about 20%; with three helpers it is 36.5% (the decider's simulation, 400,000 trials each, on the research's Push rule). The 2.0% at need 5 with two helpers is the research's table 2.6 figure and stands. No value or rule changes; Chapter 7 section 7.4's design note takes the corrected figures under 9-34, item 5.

### 9-15: the GM as Command: framing a Titan Engagement and an Expedition (audit E14, E19)

- **Decision:**
  1. **Starting a Titan Engagement.** The GM may name what a Mission Brief could: which Titan appears (from Chapter 6, or a table-made Abnormal in Phase 2), the Anchor Rating, and how many Background Titans and their clocks. The setup table fills whatever the GM leaves unnamed and is the default when the GM names nothing, as it is when a hazard row starts the fight. Closed: everyone starts Distant; the retreat clock is 8 unless a rule names its length; no rolled setup row is changed once rolled. The chapter warns that the setup mix is what Chapter 6's bands read, so a table that frames Large Titans with Background Titans every time plays above the tuned lethality (OQ-167).
  2. **Expeditions.** The GM as Command authors the Mission Brief: the route, the Waypoints, the Posts, the Pace, and one complication. Between Legs, at a Waypoint, the GM may frame a **Waypoint scene** (search the abandoned town, find survivors, cross the flooded ford) run on called rolls with the menu of 9-5. Closed: the Leg roll, the hazard and Night tables and their modifiers, "always advance", rations, the Straggler's random victim, and a rolled hazard row, which the GM describes and never softens or swaps; authored dangers belong to Waypoint scenes, never in place of a roll.
  3. **Skirmishes and Titan Engagements by the fiction.** A Waypoint scene or a failed called roll may begin either, as scene framing (9-5, item 2).
- **Why:** The audit's E14 and E19, opened with guidance. A Mission Brief already names a Titan, a route, and a complication by design (ADR-0003 item 10's tables are for Faction Standing and complications; the Brief's content is the GM's), so framing is not new authority, only authority the playtest rules lacked. The ride's engine stays closed because ADR-0009's guarantee and the hazard modifiers are pacing and economy brakes, and because a GM-picked Straggler victim is worse for trust than a player pick (the owner asked that the players not pick whom the Titan takes). Coriolis's "tables inspire" advice (p. 250) is rejected for rolled rows for the same trust reason as the Titan's cards. Figures: Expedition figures were never measured (Chapter 7's design note); the playtest logs them.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0009 `## Amended` (Waypoint scenes between Legs; the guarantee and the hazard roll unchanged). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none (Mission Brief and Waypoint already read this way).
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 section 5.1 (*Framing*); Chapter 6 preface (which Titan appears); Chapter 7 section 7.1 (*Waypoint scenes*; the Brief; the preface of 9-11); `data/engagement/engagement-setup.yaml` (`used_when` adds "or the GM names, filling what is left"); `data/expedition/legs.yaml` (the day steps note the optional scene at the Waypoint).

### 9-16: Requisition, Squad Supply, and gear under rulings (audit E12, E13)

- **Decision:** The GM may place a charter-compliant item (ADR-0016) on the Requisition list at a Scarcity the era rules allow, and may apply Circumstances to a Requisition roll for leverage (a favour owed, a Commendation, a captured specimen). An off-list item that rates a measured roll (ODM Gear, a Blade Set, a horse, a firearm) must be at least Limited and is marked unmeasured. Closed: the Funding gate, the ledger, one Requisition per Downtime, Command's answer standing after the roll, Standard Issue's contents and timing, and wear. An improvised object gives Circumstances, never Gear Dice: Gear Dice stay tied to rated items and to wear (ADR-0004). The packet's Requisition list stays the playtest list, with one line that the GM may add to it.
- **Why:** The audit's E12 and E13. Scarcity and the ledger are the only brake on gear climbing past the reference builds; leverage as Circumstances is the dice language of 9-2 and matches how Faction Standing will work (added to the dice, `CONTEXT.md`). The Limited-or-Rare floor for measured items keeps a GM-placed rating 3 ODM Gear from moving Titan figures at Standard Scarcity. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0016 `## Amended` (items 6 and 7: a table's own items, Titans, and Foes are played as unmeasured and marked so; the charter gates published data). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 4 section 4.1 (an improvised object; the Rulings paragraph of 9-11); Chapter 7 section 7.3 (the list preface; leverage); `data/campaign/requisition.yaml` (the list preface: `gm_may_add` with the charter and the Limited floor); `data/gear/standard-issue.yaml` (the `gm:` field of 9-11).

### 9-17: ADR-0014: targets at Standard, and the playtest log (research, both files)

- **Decision:** Every ADR-0014 target, band, bar limit, and reported row describes the rules as written at Standard Circumstances with no rulings: no called rolls, no framing beyond the setup table, no directed Foes, no GM-called Fear Rolls. No ruling touches the definition of a measured roll; a ruling changes only what the table did on a given night. The simulator is not rerun for this batch, and is rerun only when a closed number changes. Sensitivity rows for Circumstances in a Titan Engagement (every soldier roll at Hard; every dodge at Hard; framed starts off the setup mix) are added with the target B retune (OQ-140), not before the playtest. The playtest log tags: every non-Standard step named in a Titan Engagement and the roll it was named on; every death, Critical Injury, Scar, and Stress point a ruling caused; every GM-called Fear Roll; every directed Foe; every framed start that departed from the setup table. The retune reads rules deaths and ruling deaths apart.
- **Why:** The research's section 6 and the difficulty research's section 5, item 10. Nothing measured has changed, so a rerun would reproduce the committed figures. What has changed is what the figures mean: they are the baseline a table plays above or below by its rulings, and the retune needs to know which. Figures: the committed run stands.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0014 `## Amended`. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** Chapter 5 section 5.13 (one sentence before the verdicts: measured at Standard with no rulings); `docs/playtest/` (the log fields; WP-P3 adds them to the packet's playtest notes); `tools/sim/report.py` (out of this batch; one sentence when next edited).

### 9-18: ADR-0024 supersedes ADR-0003; the surviving items; ADR-0013 (audit section 7)

- **Decision:** ADR-0024 supersedes ADR-0003 in full. Its limits restate ADR-0003's surviving drafting items so that every citation has a home: ADR-0003 items 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, and 13 survive unchanged (as ADR-0024 limits 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, 13); item 1 is rewritten (the closed list plus `gm-horror` outside a Titan Engagement, 9-6); item 8 is kept for Titans and tables and loses its Foe clause (9-7); item 9 is rewritten (the called roll and the model entry, 9-1, 9-8); item 12 is rewritten (tracked values as an index and the in-fight effect menu, 9-12). Numbers are kept so that "ADR-0003, item 8" reads "ADR-0024, limit 8" everywhere by substitution; the drafter replaces every citation. ADR-0003 carries a `## Superseded` section in ADR-0015's style and is kept as history. ADR-0013's reason sentence ("the GM never has to pick a Shifter's target by judgment") no longer binds; the Intent Table stays because a Shifter's fight is Titan-measured content, and the ADR is revisited when Shifters are drafted.
- **Why:** The audit's recommendation 13: supersede, not patch, because the founding sentence is the thing reversed and thirteen items would otherwise sit under a preamble that contradicts them. Keeping the numbers avoids a hundred edits that change meaning. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 (new); ADR-0003 (`## Superseded`); ADR-0013 (`## Amended`, one sentence). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.

### 9-19: one batch, then packet Version 3 (owner; answer 10)

- **Decision:** This batch is the one decision batch before the first playtest. The packet is republished as Version 3 to the same URL after every drafting package: `core-dice` rewritten to section 1.1 (the "Rules decide" bullet at `:193` goes); a **Circumstances** box with the ladder; a GM section on rulings (when to roll, stakes first, the menu, the list of what stands, people with reasons and Titans without, the playtest log fields); the "GM never" lines at `:290`, `:979`, `:1216`, `:1315`, `:1579`, and `:2307` narrowed by the 9-11 table; the actions-outside text at `:1138` rewritten; the Parley line and the Foes table (9-14); the Titan, Grab, and tuning pages unchanged. The packet conventions of `HANDOFF.md` hold (no banned words, no em dashes, links resolve, d6 tables complete).

### 9-20: deferred to Phase 2 (audit E18, E20, E25)

- **Decision:** Three things wait: the Downtime Action *Pursue a Lead* (a called-roll scene inside the Walls with a short outcome menu, the playtest stand-in for Investigate); table-made Abnormals with the Chapter 5 format and the closed lists, played as unmeasured; and the website's dice tray and Compendium (`site/src/lib/dice-rules.ts` lets base dice reach 0 and has no cap or penalty logic, so it needs the floor, the cap, and a Circumstances stepper; the Action card renderer needs the new `rolled` value). Each is an OQ entry (OQ-170, OQ-171). Nothing in this batch touches `site/`.

### 9-21: the passive roll takes its Circumstances in full (WP-G0's provisional reading; decides OQ-173)

- **Decision:** (a). A passive roll takes the Circumstances the GM names to themselves on the whole ladder, as 9-2, item 5 gives every attribute roll it does not exclude: a minus step is a penalty after the cap, and a plus step (Easy, Routine, or Effortless) adds its dice as the `circumstances` Bonus Dice source. No other Bonus Dice source applies, so the cap of 4 never binds on a passive roll. 9-10's "no Bonus Dice" reads "no Bonus Dice but a plus step of Circumstances": the sources it removes are the ones a roller declares, and a soldier who does not know they are rolling declares none. The passive roll stays off `data/core/circumstances.yaml` `never`. Nothing else in 9-10 changes: no Help, no Cover, no Push, and a Stress Die showing 1 does nothing.
- **Why:** 9-2, item 5 excludes a roll from Circumstances whole, by a `circumstances: never` row, and knows no half exclusion; 9-10 names the Circumstances as part of the passive pool without limiting the step. The plus step is the one Bonus Dice source a ruling names (9-9), not one the roller declares, so 9-10's list of what the unaware soldier cannot do (declare Bonus Dice, take Help, be Covered, Push) does not reach it. 9-2, item 6 gives the plus steps their use: a soldier who carries a penalty keeps their dice for something simple, and on a passive roll, where Help is barred, a plus step is the only thing that can offset that penalty. Something in plain sight is not rolled at all (9-2, item 6), so a plus step marks what is easy to notice but not certain. (b) would make the ladder one-sided on a single roll; (c) contradicts 9-10's own words. Figures: none; the simulator makes no passive roll.
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0024, limits 14 and 16, hold as written).
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** the PROVISIONAL markers are removed.
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 5: replace "PROVISIONAL: a plus step adds its dice, and no other Bonus Dice source applies (OQ-173)." with "A plus step adds its dice as a Bonus Dice source, and no other Bonus Dice source applies (decision batch 9, 9-21)."
  - `data/core/dice-pool.yaml`, `roll_exceptions`, `passive-roll`: the comment on `bonus_sources_allowed` reads `# decision batch 9, 9-21 (OQ-173): a plus step the GM names adds its dice`. `bonus_sources_excluded: all`, `bonus_sources_allowed: [circumstances]`, and `decided: [OQ-173]` stay as they are.
  - No other file changes.

### 9-22: a failed threat hardens later Parleys; what a Size Up states holds (WP-G4's provisional readings; decides OQ-174)

- **Decision:**
  1. **A failed threat: (1a).** In a Skirmish, each failed Parley made as a threat raises the group's Grit by 1 and its Parley value by 1 for the rest of that Skirmish, and every later Parley against the group in that Skirmish reads the raised Parley value. The rise is a written rule that names the change, so 9-3 (the GM never changes a need) is untouched. Outside a Skirmish a failed Parley still does nothing. Break Their Nerve is unchanged: it lowers Grit for breaking only, and its sentence that a Parley's need does not change stays true of the Talent.
  2. **Size Up: (2a), with the statement held.** On a success, the GM states, for each Foe of the group, what it will do on the group's next card: the foe rule's pick as things stand, or, for a Foe the GM already means to direct, what it will do and the reason. What the GM states is what that Foe does on its turn on that card, unless something happens after the Size Up and before that turn that changes it:
     - for a Foe stated under the foe rule: a change in what the foe rule's steps test (who last attacked it, who is Engaged with it, who is Down or out, whether its firearm is loaded), after which the foe rule picks again; or a reason the fiction gives that Foe after the Size Up, on which the GM may direct it, saying the reason aloud as 9-7 gives;
     - for a Foe stated as directed: a reason the fiction gives it after the Size Up, said aloud as 9-7 gives; or its stated action becoming one it cannot take (its target Down, out, or gone), after which the foe rule picks unless the GM directs it with a reason said aloud.
     The GM never directs a Foe on that card for a reason that already stood at the Size Up and was not stated. The hold ends when that card ends.
- **Why:** (1) Before 9-14 one number gave both breaking and the Parley need, so a failed threat hardened both. 9-7, item 3 kept the rise, and 9-14 moved the need to its own field without deciding to take a cost off threats; 9-14 also says nothing changes for a Bandit or a Garrison sentry, whose Parley value equals their Grit, which stays true only if the rise follows both numbers. (1b) would quietly make threats cheaper. (2) A Size Up is a rolled success whose result is the GM's statement, results stand (ADR-0024, limit 14), and Chapter 7 section 7.5 has the GM answer a Size Up truthfully. If the GM could turn a Foe on the next card for a reason already in mind, the success would tell the Squad nothing against a GM who directs, and the table could not tell a reason from a changed mind. The foe rule's own "as things stand" already lets the soldiers' acts change a pick; the same test applied to a direction leaves 9-7's authority whole for everything that happens after the Size Up, which is the Squad's to cause and to answer. (2b) would make Size Up a guess. Figures: none; the Skirmish probe never Parleys or Sizes Up.
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0018 as amended and ADR-0024, limits 8 and 14, hold as written).
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** the PROVISIONAL markers are removed.
  - Chapter 7 section 7.4, *Grit and breaking*, third bullet: replace the whole bullet with "A failed Parley made as a threat raises the group's Grit by 1 and its Parley value by 1 for the rest of the Skirmish, so a failed threat hardens later Parleys (decision batch 9, 9-22)." *Parley*'s "On a failure" bullet and the Parley row of *Every roll in a Skirmish* are unchanged.
  - Chapter 7 section 7.4, *Size Up*: replace "PROVISIONAL: for a Foe the GM already means to direct, the GM states what it will do and the reason (OQ-174)." with "For a Foe the GM already means to direct, the GM states instead what it will do and the reason. Each Foe does what the GM stated on its turn on that card, unless something happens after the Size Up that changes it: for a Foe stated under the foe rule, a change in what the rule tests, after which the rule picks again, or a new reason the fiction gives it, said aloud; for a Foe stated as directed, a new reason, said aloud, or an action it can no longer take, after which the foe rule picks unless the GM directs it again with a reason said aloud. The GM never directs a Foe on that card for a reason held back at the Size Up (decision batch 9, 9-22)."
  - `data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml`: the comment above `grit.failed_threat` reads `# decision batch 9, 9-22 (OQ-174): 9-14 moved the Parley need off Grit; a failed threat still hardens later Parleys`. The comment above `size_up.on_success` reads `# decision batch 9, 9-22 (OQ-174): the answer stays true when the GM already means to direct a Foe (9-7)`. `size_up` gains a key `holds`, after `on_success`, carrying the second and third sentences of the *Size Up* text above and ending "(data/skirmish/foes.yaml, gm_rulings, directed_by_ruling)". The top-level `decided` list gains OQ-174. `failed_threat`, `parley.on_failure`, and the `parley` row's `notes` keep their text.
  - `data/skirmish/foes.yaml`, `gm_rulings.directed_by_ruling`: gains a key `after_size_up`, with the comment `# decision batch 9, 9-22 (OQ-174)`: "On the group's next card after a successful Size Up, each Foe does what the GM stated unless something that happens after the Size Up changes it (data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml, size_up, holds)."
  - No other file changes; `CONTEXT.md` is unchanged.

### 9-23: a retreat from a Titan Engagement a Waypoint scene begins (WP-G4's provisional reading; decides OQ-175)

- **Decision:** (a). If a Titan Engagement begun in a Waypoint scene becomes a retreat before it ends (Chapter 5, section 5.10), the scene ends and the Squad falls back as from one a hazard row of the Leg that reached this Waypoint began: to the Waypoint that Leg started from, riding that Leg again from step 1 (*Riding a Leg*), with a new Leg roll, its rations, and a new hazard. The Leg ridden again counts toward the day's Legs as any Leg ridden again after a retreat does. A Waypoint scene is framed before the Night Camp, so this day's fall back applies, never the overnight one of 8-37. When the Squad reaches this Waypoint again, step 7 applies as on any arrival, a Depot's full Standard Issue included, and the GM may frame a Waypoint scene there as after any Leg.
- **Why:** ADR-0009, as amended in batches 7 and 9: a retreat falls back to the previous Waypoint and the Leg is ridden again, and batch 9 leaves the guarantee and the ride's engine as written, so (a) applies the one rule there is. The Depot clause keeps the two retreats equal: a hazard row on a Depot Leg starts its fight before the issue, and the Squad receives the issue after riding again, so either way the Squad leaves the Depot with one full issue after the fight; withholding it would make a fight a scene begins cost more than the same fight on the Leg. (b) would let a retreat inside a scene skip the Leg's cost. (c) would price a retreat by a ruling made after the fight began, against 9-1, item 2 (stakes first). Figures: none; no Expedition figure is measured before the playtest.
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0009 as amended holds).
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** the PROVISIONAL markers are removed.
  - Chapter 7 section 7.1, *Waypoint scenes*, the "A retreat" bullet: replace the whole bullet with "**A retreat.** If a Titan Engagement begun in a Waypoint scene becomes a retreat before it ends (Chapter 5, section 5.10), the scene ends and the Squad falls back as from one a hazard row of the Leg that reached this Waypoint began: to the Waypoint that Leg started from, riding that Leg again from step 1 with a new Leg roll and a new hazard (*Riding a Leg*, step 6). Step 7 applies again when the Squad reaches this Waypoint, a Depot's Standard Issue included (decision batch 9, 9-23)."
  - Chapter 7 section 7.1, *The day*: in "Every Leg ridden counts toward the day's Legs, including a Leg ridden again after a retreat, whether the retreat came on a Leg or at a Night Camp.", "on a Leg or at a Night Camp" reads "on a Leg, in a Waypoint scene, or at a Night Camp".
  - `data/expedition/legs.yaml`: the comment above `day.waypoint_scene.retreat` reads `# decision batch 9, 9-23 (OQ-175): ADR-0009's fall back, applied to a fight a scene begins`. `retreat` carries the Chapter 7 bullet's two sentences, with "(leg_steps, titan-engagement)" in place of "(*Riding a Leg*, step 6)" and "(leg_steps, waypoint; data/gear/standard-issue.yaml)" after "a Depot's Standard Issue included". `day.counted_legs` gains ", or from a Titan Engagement a Waypoint scene began (waypoint_scene, retreat)" directly after its first "(leg_steps, titan-engagement)". The top-level `decided` list gains OQ-175.
  - No other file changes.

### 9-24: Gear Dice on a called roll made with an attribute alone (WP-G1's provisional reading; decides OQ-176)

- **Decision:** (a). A ruling is not a rule that names a gear item, so a called roll made with an attribute alone takes no Gear Dice, whatever the soldier carries. A gear item that helps the act, rated or not, counts toward the Circumstances the GM names, as any object does (Chapter 4, section 4.1; 9-16); it gives no Gear Dice, so a Push cannot wear it on that roll. Gear Dice reach a called roll only through the entry the GM names: of the entries marked `when_called`, `fly` lists ODM Gear and `ride` lists the horse, and a called roll named for either takes that item's Gear Dice as the entry gives. The GM never names an entry not marked `when_called` for a called roll in order to reach its gear.
- **Why:** 9-9 makes Circumstances the one dice source a ruling names, and 9-1, item 2 ties a called roll's gear to the entry the GM names; 9-16 and ADR-0004 keep Gear Dice on rated items through the entries that list them, with wear as the Push's price on those dice. (b) would let a ruling attach Gear Dice to any act beside the one-step ladder, which 9-9 closes. (c) would have a called roll borrow an entry built for a tracked value (Field Repair, Treat Injury), with its Talents and procedure, which 9-12 kept to the acts that change that value. The cost of (a) is that a rating 3 tool kit helps an attribute-alone called roll no more than the step the GM names for it; a fine kit is what a better step is for, in the owner's language of Circumstances as dice. Figures: none; called rolls are not measured (9-17).
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0024, limits 9 and 16, ADR-0004, and ADR-0006 hold as written).
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:** the PROVISIONAL markers are removed.
  - Chapter 2 section 2.9, *Rolls called by attribute*: replace "PROVISIONAL (OQ-176): a ruling is not a rule that names a gear item, so a called roll made with an attribute alone takes no Gear Dice, and a tool or object that helps is part of its Circumstances." with "A ruling is not a rule that names a gear item, so a called roll made with an attribute alone takes no Gear Dice: a gear item or other object that helps counts toward its Circumstances (Chapter 4, section 4.1) and, giving no Gear Dice, cannot be worn by a Push on it. A called roll the GM names for `fly` or `ride` takes that entry's Gear Dice (decision batch 9, 9-24)."
  - Chapter 4 section 4.1, the paragraph beginning "`data/gear/items.yaml` lists every gear item": after "so a rope and a grapnel might make a climb Easy." insert "On a called roll made with an attribute alone, a gear item that helps counts the same way and gives no Gear Dice (Chapter 2, section 2.9; decision batch 9, 9-24)."
  - `data/character/action-catalog.yaml`, `uncatalogued_actions`: the comment `# The last two sentences are PROVISIONAL (OQ-176).` reads `# decision batch 9, 9-24 (OQ-176): the called-roll sentences.` The last sentence of `rolls_called_by_attribute` ("A ruling is not a rule ... part of its Circumstances.") is replaced by the two sentences of the Chapter 2 text above, with "(data/gear/items.yaml, other_objects)" in place of "(Chapter 4, section 4.1)". The top-level `decided` list gains OQ-176.
  - `data/gear/items.yaml`, `other_objects`: append "On a called roll made with an attribute alone, a gear item that helps counts the same way and gives no Gear Dice (data/character/action-catalog.yaml, uncatalogued_actions, rolls_called_by_attribute; decision batch 9, 9-24)."
  - No other file changes.

### 9-25: a Reaction's Circumstances are fixed before the attack is rolled (review round 1, Opus C1)

- **Decision:** A Reaction (a dodge against a Titan; a Block or a Dodge against a Foe) takes the Circumstances step in force when the attacker's card comes up: a Titan's card, before step 1 of *Resolving a card* (Chapter 5, section 5.5), or a Foe group's card, before its first Foe acts (Chapter 7, section 7.4). Until that card ends, no step is named, raised, or lowered for a Reaction against it, and a condition that arises during the card, including one the card's own effects cause, applies from the next card. A dodge whose successes cancel against a later card of the same Titan that round keeps the step it was rolled at. Every other roll keeps 9-2's timing: its step is named before its own pool is built. Help and Bonus Dice on a Reaction are declared as usual, once the step and the Severity are known.
- **Why:** Every timing rule for Circumstances counted from the pool being built, and a Reaction's pool is built only after the attack roll is final (Chapter 1, section 1.9, *Attack and Reaction*), so the GM could see a card's Severity and then name the dodge's step, which decides whether the card lands. That is a ruling after the dice on the roll that settles their effect, against the line of ADR-0024 ("the GM rules before the dice, never after them") and limit 8, and against the owner's rejection of adjusting a result after the roll. Fixing the step when the card comes up keeps everything the owner opened (rain, smoke, a mired Titan, each named when it begins) and closes the only window that opened after dice. Figures: the review's simulation, a Rookie's dodge against 9 Attack Dice lets the card land 75.2% of the time at Hard, 69.1% at Standard, and 63.2% at Easy.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 limit 16 (edited in the list; `## Amended`). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 1 section 1.4a: a new item 9 after item 8: "**A Reaction's step is fixed before the attack.** A Reaction takes the step in force when the attacker's card comes up: a Titan's card (Chapter 5, section 5.5) or a Foe group's card (Chapter 7, section 7.4). Until that card ends, no step is named, raised, or lowered for a Reaction against it, and a condition that arises during the card applies from the next card. ### 9-26: one step per roll under a lasting condition; what "every ODM roll" means (review round 1, Opus M6)

- **Decision:**
  1. **One step still.** A step named for a lasting condition (9-4, item 2) is the step of every roll that condition touches while it lasts. When a further condition touches a roll already under a lasting step, the GM names one step for that roll, weighing both, before its pool is built, or for a Reaction when the attacker's card comes up (9-25). Steps never add. The lasting step applies again to every later roll the further condition does not touch, and a further condition that itself lasts (the Titan stays mired) is weighed the same way, once, for the rolls both touch while both last.
  2. **"Every ODM roll"** reads "every roll whose Gear Dice come from ODM Gear". A Nape strike and a Body Part strike take their Gear Dice from a Blade Set, so a step named for rolls with ODM Gear does not reach them unless the GM names the condition for them too.
  3. **The mired Titan.** A Titan mired to the knee makes the dodge against it Easy, named when the Titan is mired and before its card comes up; if a lasting step already covers that dodge, such as rain named Hard, the GM names one step for it weighing both.
- **Why:** 9-2, item 1 governs ("one judgment, not three"). 9-4, item 2's "rather than re-judging each roll" spares the GM from naming a standing condition roll by roll; it was never meant to forbid weighing a new condition. Adding steps (the review's option 2) would change 9-2, item 1 and make two conditions two steps, which the owner's ladder does not do. Naming the weighed step before the card keeps 9-25. The definition keys the owner's rain example to the gear the rain acts on. Figures: the review's land rates (69.1%, 63.2%, 75.2% against 9 Attack Dice for Standard, Easy, and Hard) show why the step must be settled before the card.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 limit 16 (with 9-25). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 1 section 1.4a, item 1: after "Rain, dark, and a snapped strap are one judgment, not three." insert "A step named for a lasting condition is the step of every roll that condition touches while it lasts (Chapter 5). When a further condition touches a roll already under a lasting step, the GM names one step for that roll, weighing both; steps never add, and the lasting step applies again to later rolls the further condition does not touch (decision batch 9, 9-26)."
  - `data/core/circumstances.yaml`: `one_step_per_roll` gains the inserted Chapter 1 sentences, with "(in_titan_engagement, lasting_condition)" in place of "(Chapter 5)". `in_titan_engagement.lasting_condition` reads: "A step named for a condition applies to every roll that condition touches while it lasts. The GM says so once (rain makes every roll whose Gear Dice come from ODM Gear Hard until it stops) and does not name a step roll by roll. When a further condition touches a roll under a lasting step, the GM names one step for that roll weighing both, before its pool is built, or for a dodge when the Titan's card comes up (reactions); steps never add (one_step_per_roll; decision batch 9, 9-26)." `in_titan_engagement.titan_conditions` reads: "A condition that would slow or blind a Titan reaches the table only through the soldiers' dice. A Titan mired to the knee makes the dodge against it Easy, named when it is mired and before its card comes up; if a lasting step already covers that dodge, such as rain at Hard, the GM names one step for it weighing both. The Titan loses no Attack Dice."
  - Chapter 5 preface, **Rulings**, the *Circumstances on the soldiers' rolls* bullet: replace `("the rain makes every ODM roll Hard until it stops")` with `("the rain makes every roll with ODM Gear Hard until it stops", which is every roll whose Gear Dice come from ODM Gear)`, and after "rather than judging each roll again" insert "; when a further condition touches a roll under that step, the GM names one step for that roll weighing both, and steps never add".
  - Chapter 5 preface, the paragraph after the list: replace "a Titan mired to the knee makes the dodge Easy, and it keeps its Attack Dice." with "a Titan mired to the knee makes the dodge against it Easy, named when it is mired and before its card comes up, or, in rain already named Hard, one step the GM names weighing both; it keeps its Attack Dice (decision batch 9, 9-26)."
  - Chapter 5 section 5.5, *Dodging a Titan*, the *Circumstances* bullet: replace "a Titan mired to the knee makes the dodge Easy, and it loses no Attack Dice (decision batch 9, 9-4)" with "a Titan mired to the knee makes the dodge against it Easy, named when it is mired and before its card comes up, or, in rain already named Hard, one step the GM names weighing both; it loses no Attack Dice (decision batch 9, 9-4 and 9-26)".

### 9-27: improvised acts in a fight: the steps are not used, what a model entry can be, and the one kill (review round 1, Opus M5 and Astra C1)

- **Decision:**
  1. **In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, section 2.9's steps are not used.** A soldier's action there is Help, a Catalog entry the soldier names and takes as that entry is written, or an improvised act. An act the soldier describes that is not an entry taken as written is an improvised act (9-8), even when a tracked value it would change matches an entry's `changes`, and the GM names its model entry. Whether a described act is an entry taken as written or an improvised act is part of that ruling, made before the pool is built. A move stays a move (Chapter 5, section 5.2; Chapter 7, section 7.4). Outside a fight the steps stand as 9-12 gives them.
  2. **What a model entry can be.** An entry of kind `action` that the soldier could take in that fight: in a Titan Engagement, one whose `context` is `titan-engagement` or `any`; in a Skirmish, one Chapter 7 section 7.4 allows. Never an entry of kind `reaction` (the dodge, the Block), `roll`, `fixed-roll`, or `option` (such as Call It, Cover, Reload, or a Downtime option). The act meets the model entry's requirements as 9-8 gives, and an act modelled on an entry that is not rolled (Help, Draw Attention) is not rolled.
  3. **The one kill.** Only a Nape strike the soldier takes as its entry is written kills a Titan. An improvised act modelled on the Nape strike is made with its requirements and needs and never kills; 9-8's other limits apply.
  4. **Taking cover.** In a Titan Engagement, a dive behind a chimney stack is a move if it changes the soldier's Position, and otherwise changes nothing and needs no roll. It is never modelled on the dodge and gives no Circumstances to a later dodge: Positions already price where a soldier stands against a Titan (9-4, item 1).
- **Why:** Two procedures overlapped in a fight. Step 3 makes an act "that entry for every purpose" whatever its description, so a masonry drop the player named as `titan-kill` became a Nape strike with its kill, against 9-8 and the owner's "never a kill" (answer 8); `in_a_fight` applied only to acts the steps left unresolved. Routing every described act through one ruling closes the overlap, and a player who wants the kill takes the Nape strike as written. Nothing limited the model entry's kind: a Reaction as a model turns a whole-turn roll made knowing the Severity into an action rolled before the card, a `roll` or `fixed-roll` answers a rule rather than an act, and an option is a procedural use (reloading, swapping a card, Covering a Push) with no effect an act imitates. The owner's words were "copy an existing action", and an entry of kind `action` in that fight is that set. Taking cover is among the commonest improvised acts; as a free Easy dodge it would be taken every round and move every Titan figure, so it is closed on the Positions that already price the fight. Figures: none moved; 9-8's measured effect sizes stand.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 limit 9 (edited in the list; `## Amended`). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:**

In a fight the steps are not used, and in_a_fight applies." The comment above `uncatalogued_actions` replaces "The steps are the default order for an action meant to change a tracked value." with "Outside a fight, the steps are the default order for an action meant to change a tracked value (9-27)."

Whether a described act is an entry taken as written is part of the GM's ruling on its model entry."
