### 9-28: what Help and a retry cost on a called roll (review round 1, Opus M2 and Astra M1)

- **Decision:** A cost the GM names for Help on a called roll, or for trying a called roll again, is time or position only (9-5's last menu item): a change in the situation, described, that the next roll must reckon with. The GM names it before the dice are rolled, and it falls when it is taken on: when the comrade declares Help, or when the soldier tries again, whatever that roll's result. It is not that roll's stakes and does not count against one cost per failed roll. Help and a retry never cost Stress, a fall, damage, or a lost item or unit. The roll keeps its own stakes under 9-5. In 9-5, "A failed called roll, or a retry the GM prices, costs what the GM staked" reads "A failed called roll costs what the GM staked; what a retry itself costs is 9-28's".
- **Why:** The menu's Stress, fall, and damage routes fire only on the roller's own failed roll (`stress-changes.yaml` `ruling`, `health.yaml` `by_ruling`, `falls.yaml` `rule-named`), and Chapter 1 section 1.6 lets Stress change only through a row, so a helper's Stress, a helper's damage, and a retry's Stress had no trigger, and no rule said when they fell or how they met one cost per failure. Time or position needs no row and no payer: the situation pays it, so it can fall cleanly when named. Adding three trigger rows and a second cost per roll (the review's option 2) builds a second stakes system for side costs; making Help always free (option 3) removes a lever the batch opened, when a crush that exposes the Squad is exactly the price the fiction names. Figures: none; called rolls are not measured (9-17).
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 limit 17 (edited in the list; `## Amended`). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Help (the called-roll clause).
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 4: replace "or time spent at a cost the GM names from the menu in item 2" with "or time spent, at a cost of time or position the GM names before the new roll, which falls when the soldier tries again, whatever the result, and is not the new roll's stakes (decision batch 9, 9-28)".
  - Chapter 1 section 1.8, *Outside a Titan Engagement*, the called-roll bullet: replace "Help spends nothing unless the GM names a cost from the menu (section 1.1, item 2)." with "Help spends nothing unless the GM names, before the dice are rolled, a cost of time or position for it (section 1.1, item 2). That cost falls when the comrade declares Help, whatever the roll's result; it is not the roll's stakes and does not count against the one cost of a failed roll (decision batch 9, 9-28)."
  - `data/core/dice-pool.yaml`, `called_roll.help`: replace "Help spends nothing unless the GM names a cost from failure_menu." with "Help spends nothing unless the GM names, before the dice, a cost of time or position for it (failure_menu, time-or-position), which falls when the comrade declares Help, whatever the result, and is not the roll's stakes (decision batch 9, 9-28)." `called_roll.retries.outside`: replace "or time spent at a cost the GM names from failure_menu" with "or time spent, at a cost of time or position the GM names before the new roll (failure_menu, time-or-position), which falls when the soldier tries again, whatever the result, and is not the new roll's stakes (decision batch 9, 9-28)".
  - `data/core/bonus-dice-sources.yaml`, `help.spends_outside_titan_engagement`: replace "On a called roll, nothing, unless the GM names a cost from the menu of Chapter 1, section 1.1, item 2." with "On a called roll, nothing, unless the GM names before the dice a cost of time or position, which falls when Help is declared, whatever the result (Chapter 1, section 1.8; decision batch 9, 9-28)."
  - `data/character/action-catalog.yaml`, the nine rows marked `rolled: when_called`, `help_outside_titan_engagement`: replace "Help spends nothing unless the GM names a cost from the menu (Chapter 1, section 1.1, item 2)" with "Help spends nothing unless the GM names a cost of time or position for it, which falls when Help is declared (Chapter 1, section 1.8; decision batch 9, 9-28)". Re-render Chapter 2 Appendix 2A.
  - `CONTEXT.md`, **Help**: replace "with Help spending nothing unless the GM names a cost" with "with Help spending nothing unless the GM names a cost of time or position, which falls when Help is declared".
  - No other file changes.

### 9-29: found supplies: one search a place (review round 1, Opus M1)

- **Decision:** A called search that finds Squad Supply is made of a **place** the GM names with the stakes: a house, a wagon, a storehouse. A place yields Squad Supply to one called search only. Once a called search of it has been rolled, whatever its result, no later roll, in this scene or a later one, finds Squad Supply there; the other soldiers present search it by Helping that roll (Chapter 1, section 1.8), and trying again after a change may find information or a way on, never Squad Supply. The cap on the roll stands: at most 1 unit per success, of one kind that place plausibly holds.
- **Why:** 9-5 capped finds so that scarcity survives a good search roll, and a cap on one roll does not survive item 4's one attempt per soldier: four soldiers searching one abandoned town average 6.2 rations against a Funding 3 stock of 8, and a second scene after the next Leg does it again. One roll a place turns four rolls into one roll with three helpers (2.4 units on average), keeps the cap's shape, and adds no number, where a cap per scene (the review's option 2) sets a figure nothing measured. Figures: the review's 6.2 and 2.4 units.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - `data/gear/squad-supply.yaml`, `spending`: replace "or a ruling on a successful called search (a Spot or a Survive): the GM may add at most 1 unit per success, of one kind of Squad Supply the place plausibly holds. Nothing else adds units by ruling (decision batch 9, 9-5)." with "or a ruling on a successful called search (a Spot or a Survive) of a place the GM names with the stakes: the GM may add at most 1 unit per success, of one kind of Squad Supply that place plausibly holds. A place yields Squad Supply to one called search only: once a called search of it is rolled, whatever its result, no later roll finds Squad Supply there, and the other soldiers present search it by Helping that roll. Nothing else adds units by ruling (decision batch 9, 9-5 and 9-29)."
  - Chapter 4 section 4.10, *Kinds and units*, the fourth bullet: replace "or a successful called search, a Spot or a Survive, on which the GM may add at most 1 unit per success of one kind of Squad Supply the place could plausibly hold (`spending`; decision batch 9, 9-5)." with "or a successful called search, a Spot or a Survive, of a place the GM names with the stakes, on which the GM may add at most 1 unit per success of one kind of Squad Supply that place could plausibly hold. A place yields Squad Supply to one called search only: once a called search of it is rolled, whatever its result, no later roll finds Squad Supply there, and the other soldiers present search it by Helping that roll (`spending`; decision batch 9, 9-5 and 9-29)."
  - Chapter 4 preface, **Rulings**: replace "A successful called search may find Squad Supply, at most 1 unit per success of one kind the place could hold (section 4.10; 9-5)." with "A successful called search of a place may find Squad Supply, at most 1 unit per success of one kind the place could hold, and a place yields Squad Supply to one called search only (section 4.10; 9-5 and 9-29)."
  - Chapter 4 section 4.10, the design note (decision batch 9, 9-5 and 9-16): replace "never more than 1 unit a success" with "never more than 1 unit a success, and from one search a place (9-29)".
  - No other file changes: `legs.yaml` `waypoint_scene.runs_on` and Chapter 7's Waypoint scene bullet already point to `squad-supply.yaml`.

### 9-30: a fall a ruling names is low or high (review round 1, Opus M3)

- **Decision:** A fall named as a called roll's stakes is low or high, as the GM names from the height, and low if the GM names none; never extreme. Its damage therefore tops out at 3, the ceiling 9-5 set on harm a ruling names. A fall another rule names keeps the band that rule or the height steps give, the extreme band included.
- **Why:** Outside a Titan Engagement nothing limited the band a ruling names, and the extreme band's D6 plus 4 deals 4 damage one time in three, past 9-5's "never 4 or more", which exists because 4 is a musket's damage and a musket is rolled. The high band's D6 plus 2 tops out at the heavy-landing row, 3. Capping the table for ruling falls (the review's option 2) would give one row two readings; exempting the fall (option 3) would contradict Chapter 3 and 9-5. Figures: `falls.yaml` `damage_table`.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0005 (`## Amended`); ADR-0024 limit 17. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 2, the menu: replace "- **A fall:** by Chapter 4's procedure, in the band the GM names from the height, or low if the GM names none." with "- **A fall:** by Chapter 4's procedure, low or high as the GM names from the height, or low if the GM names none; never extreme (decision batch 9, 9-30)."
  - `data/core/dice-pool.yaml`, `called_roll.failure_menu`, `fall`: replace "in the band the GM names from the height, or low if the GM names none." with "low or high as the GM names from the height, or low if the GM names none; never extreme (decision batch 9, 9-30)."
  - `data/gear/falls.yaml`: `triggers`, `rule-named`, `height` reads "The band that rule names; or, for a ruling, low or high as the GM names from the fall's height with the stakes before the roll, never extreme; low if none is named (decision batch 9, 9-30)." `height.outside_titan_engagement`: replace "or the GM does for a fall a ruling names (triggers, rule-named)" with "or the GM does for a fall a ruling names, low or high only (triggers, rule-named)". `after_the_fall.gm`: replace "with the band the GM names before the roll, low if none" with "with the band the GM names before the roll, low or high and never extreme, low if none".
  - Chapter 4 section 4.6, the **Named by a rule or a ruling** bullet: replace "with the band the GM names from the fall's height, or low if the GM names none (Chapter 1, section 1.1; decision batch 9, 9-5)" with "low or high as the GM names from the fall's height, never extreme, or low if the GM names none (Chapter 1, section 1.1; decision batch 9, 9-5 and 9-30)". *Height*, the last paragraph: replace "or the GM does for a fall a ruling names, and a fall is low if neither names one" with "or the GM does for a fall a ruling names, low or high only, and a fall is low if neither names one".
  - Chapter 4 preface, **Rulings**: replace "A called roll's stakes may name a fall, with the band the GM names, or low if none (section 4.6)" with "A called roll's stakes may name a fall, low or high as the GM names, or low if none (section 4.6; 9-30)".

### 9-31: what success on a called roll gives (review round 1, Opus M7)

- **Decision:** What success on a called roll gives is one of: the act itself (it happens); information; a better position in the situation, which the next roll reckons with; an object that is not a gear item, which counts only toward Circumstances (Chapter 4, section 4.1); or Squad Supply found as 9-5 and 9-29 cap it. Success never gives a gear item, Stress relief, a Health box or healing, a Commendation, a Research Point, or a Faction Standing change: each comes only from its own rule or table, and a gear item only by Standard Issue, a Requisition, or a rule that moves an item between soldiers. The GM names it with the stakes where it is not obvious, and it does not change once the dice are rolled.
- **Why:** The menu closed what failure costs and nothing closed what success gives, so a stake could name a working ODM Gear set found in a wrecked wagon and bypass 9-16's Scarcity and Limited floor, or a Commendation or a Research Point that ADR-0024 limits 5 and 6 give only by table. The one sentence closing found gear lived in a `gm:` field and a design note. Riderless horses and the gear of the dead are the likeliest Waypoint rewards in the setting, so the line is printed where the GM reads the stakes. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 limit 17. Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Stakes.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 2: after "A failure should move the story on, not halt it." insert "**What success gives** is the act itself, information, a better position that the next roll reckons with, an object that is not a gear item, which counts only toward Circumstances (Chapter 4, section 4.1), or Squad Supply found as Chapter 4 caps it (section 4.10). It never gives a gear item, Stress relief, a Health box or healing, a Commendation, a Research Point, or a Faction Standing change: each comes only from its own rule or table (decision batch 9, 9-31)."
  - `data/core/dice-pool.yaml`, `called_roll`: two new keys after `failure_never`, with the comment `# decision batch 9, 9-31`: `success_gives: The act itself, information, a better position that the next roll reckons with, an object that is not a gear item (it counts only toward Circumstances, data/gear/items.yaml, other_objects), or Squad Supply found as data/gear/squad-supply.yaml (spending) caps it.` and `success_never: [a gear item, Stress relief, a Health box or healing, a Commendation, a Research Point, a Faction Standing change]`.
  - Chapter 2 section 2.9, *Outside a fight*, the stakes bullet: after "and, where it is not obvious, what success gives" insert ", which is only what Chapter 1, section 1.1, item 2 lists and never a gear item (decision batch 9, 9-31)".
  - `data/character/action-catalog.yaml`, `uncatalogued_actions.no_value_or_no_match`: after "and, where it is not obvious, what success gives" insert " (data/core/dice-pool.yaml, called_roll, success_gives; never a gear item)".
  - Chapter 4 preface, **Rulings**: after "The GM may add a charter-compliant item to the Requisition list (Chapter 7, section 7.3; 9-16)." insert "No ruling gives a gear item: one comes only by Standard Issue, a Requisition, or a rule that moves an item between soldiers, and a called roll's success never names one (Chapter 1, section 1.1, item 2; 9-31)."

### 9-32: a passive roll is not a called roll (review round 1, Opus M8)

- **Decision:** A passive roll is not a called roll. It has no stakes: failing it means only that the soldier does not notice, and neither the menu of 9-5 nor the `ruling` Stress row reaches it. The `passive-roll` row states its Bonus Dice sources in one field, as the other rows do: `bonus_sources_excluded` lists every source in `bonus-dice-sources.yaml` except `circumstances`, and `bonus_sources_allowed` is removed. 9-21's rule is unchanged; its chapter impact's "stay as they are" for those two fields is superseded.
- **Why:** 9-10's own reason, that a soldier cannot pay a cost for a roll they do not know about, reaches stakes as surely as the Push and the Stress Response. The glossary was the one text calling a passive roll a called roll, against Chapter 1 item 5 and `called_roll.secret`. Two fields that read differently to a tool reading one of them are one field too many; an explicit list matches the Death Roll and performance roll rows. Figures: none.
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0024 limit 14's wording is edited under the round's pure fixes, recorded with 9-25 to 9-34 in its `## Amended`).
- **Glossary change:** Called roll.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 5: after "The soldier learns the result only through what they notice." insert "A passive roll is not a called roll: it has no stakes, and failing it means only that the soldier does not notice (decision batch 9, 9-32)."
  - `data/core/dice-pool.yaml`, `roll_exceptions`, `passive-roll`: replace the two lines `bonus_sources_excluded: all` and `bonus_sources_allowed: [circumstances]   # ...` with `bonus_sources_excluded: [help, opening, grounded-titan, read-from-distant, call-it, medical-supplies, ambush]   # every source in data/core/bonus-dice-sources.yaml but circumstances, whose plus step adds its dice (decision batch 9, 9-21 and 9-32); a later source row is added here`. After `secret: true` add `called_roll: false` and `stakes: none   # failing it means only that the soldier does not notice (decision batch 9, 9-32)`.
  - `CONTEXT.md`, **Called roll**: replace "A passive roll is a called Spot or Size Up the GM makes out of sight for something the soldier does not know is there, outside a fight, with no Push, no Help, and no Stress Response." with "A passive roll is not a called roll: it is a Spot or Size Up the GM makes out of sight for something the soldier does not know is there, outside a fight, with no stakes, no Push, no Help, and no Stress Response, and failing it means only that the soldier does not notice."
  - No other file changes.

### 9-33: a retreat from a Waypoint scene when the day has no Leg left (review round 1, Astra M2)

- **Decision:** The Leg ridden again after a retreat from a Titan Engagement a Waypoint scene began (9-23) takes the day's next Leg slot under *The day*. After the day's first Leg, it is the day's second, at Steady. After the day's second, it is the day's third only if Command orders a Hard Ride that day (*The day*, step 2), and it is then ridden as a Hard Ride. If no slot remains (after a third Leg, or after the second with no Hard Ride ordered), the Squad falls back to the Waypoint that Leg started from and makes the Night Camp there as on any day, and the next day's first Leg rides that Leg again at Steady, counted among that day's Legs. Falling back is not riding a Leg (8-37). Step 7 on arrival is 9-23's.
- **Why:** 9-23 settled the destination and the new rolls, and the day's rules already give the slots, the Hard Ride's Pace, and the ban on riding at night. When no slot remains, 8-37 already shows the pattern: fall back, camp, and ride the Leg again as the next morning's first. Here the fall back is by day, so the Night Camp is an ordinary one, with its camp roll, ration, and night hazard. This applies rules that exist and invents no Pace or ration cost, which the review's option 2 would. Figures: none; no Expedition figure is measured before the playtest.
- **ADR change:** none (ADR-0009 as amended holds; no Leg is ridden at night).
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 7 section 7.1, *Waypoint scenes*, the **A retreat.** bullet: append "The Leg ridden again takes the day's next Leg slot (*The day*): after the day's first Leg it is the second, at Steady; after the second it is the third only if Command orders a Hard Ride that day, and it is then a Hard Ride. If no slot remains, the Squad falls back to the Waypoint that Leg started from and makes the Night Camp there, and the next day's first Leg rides that Leg again at Steady (decision batch 9, 9-33)."
  - Chapter 7 section 7.1, *The day*: after "whether the retreat came on a Leg, in a Waypoint scene, or at a Night Camp." insert "A Leg ridden again as the next day's first after a retreat in a Waypoint scene counts toward that day's Legs."
  - `data/expedition/legs.yaml`, `day.waypoint_scene.retreat`: append the two Chapter 7 sentences above, with "(day, steps)" in place of "(*The day*)" and "(night_camp)" after "makes the Night Camp there". Its comment reads `# decision batch 9, 9-23 (OQ-175) and 9-33: ADR-0009's fall back, applied to a fight a scene begins`. `day.counted_legs`: replace "or from a Titan Engagement a Waypoint scene began (waypoint_scene, retreat)" with "or from a Titan Engagement a Waypoint scene began (waypoint_scene, retreat), in the day's next Leg slot or as the next day's first".
  - No other file changes.

### 9-34: the round's minor readings (review round 1, Opus m5, m7, m8, m9, m10, m11, m12)

- **Decision:**
  1. **An act several soldiers make together (m5).** An act whose one outcome succeeds or fails for several soldiers at once (forcing a gate, finding a way across a river, searching one place) is one called roll, made by one soldier the players choose, using Chapter 2's roll-off if they disagree, with Help from the others as Chapter 1 section 1.8 gives. The GM never calls a roll for each soldier for one such act. A stake of Stress, damage, or a fall falls on the soldier who rolled; a lost item is one of the roller's; a lost unit is the Squad's; time or position falls on the situation. An act each soldier makes for themselves, with an outcome of their own, is that soldier's own called roll.
  2. **"Hard" (m7).** "Hard" alone always names the Circumstances step. The Pace is always written in full as Hard Ride, and the Talents Hard to Kill and Hard Rider keep their names. Nothing is renamed.
  3. **The Straggler's Endure roll (m8).** It takes the Circumstances in force when the hazard roll is made: Standard, or a step the GM named before the hazard roll for a lasting condition of that Leg that touches it (9-26). No step is named or changed for it once the hazard is rolled, and none for the danger the row itself describes, which the row prices. The same holds for any roll a Leg Hazard or Night row calls for.
  4. **A directed Foe never leaves alone (m9).** A Foe leaves a Skirmish only at 0 Health or when its group breaks or surrenders (`skirmish.yaml`, `grit`). A directed Foe takes only a turn the foe rule's steps give. A GM who wants a Foe to run breaks its group sooner, with a reason said aloud (9-7, item 3), and what the fled Foes do after the Skirmish ends, such as raising an alarm, is framing.
  5. **The Parley design note's figures (m10).** Chapter 7's design note names one build for every figure: a soldier with Empathy 3 and Stress 1 who Pushes when short and no Stress Die shows 1. Stand down at the trooper's Grit of 4 needed 5 successes, 2.0% with two helpers (research table 2.6); at a Parley value of 2 it needs 3: 11.8% alone, 28.0% with two helpers, and 36.5% with three (this decider's simulation, 400,000 trials each, on the research's Push rule). The note's "about 20% Pushed on a five-die build and near 40% with three helpers" mixed builds. An erratum under 9-14 corrects its *Why*.
  6. **`gm-horror` and a called roll's stakes (m11).** `gm-horror` is never called for an outcome a called roll's stakes named, since a stake is never a Fear Roll (9-5). A listed trigger that such an outcome later meets, such as a comrade's death in a fight the failure began, still applies.
  7. **Framing Background Titans (m12).** Background Titans the GM frames number none, one, or two, each on a clock of 4, 6, or 8 segments, the counts and lengths the setup table uses.
- **Why:** (1) One roll with Help is the shape of the Leg roll and 9-2, item 5's group roll, and it keeps one cost per failed roll one cost however large the Squad; OQ-168 counts the rolls. (2) The glossary line settles a real table sentence at no cost; renaming the Pace would churn Chapter 7, the YAML, and the packet for a usage note. (3) A step named for the victim after the hazard roll is a ruling after dice on a rolled row that 9-15 says is never softened; the Leg's lasting conditions still reach it, as they reach the dodge (9-25, 9-26). (4) The foe rule's closed list of turns is what 9-7 kept; a lone Foe leaving opens questions (does it return, does it count as out for Grit and the Parley) that the group's breaking already answers. (5) A design note's figures must come from one build. (6) Calling a Fear Roll for a staked outcome is a Fear Roll staked in all but name, which 9-5 closed as a Scar faucet. (7) Framing is what a Mission Brief could name, and the setup table is the measured mix; counts and lengths off the table would be undefined (a clock of 0) or unmeasured past the design note's warning. Figures: item 5's.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** Circumstances (item 2).
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Item 1. Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 1: append "An act whose one outcome succeeds or fails for several soldiers at once is one called roll, made by one soldier the players choose (Chapter 2's roll-off if they disagree), with Help from the others (section 1.8); a stake of Stress, damage, or a fall falls on the soldier who rolled, and an act each soldier makes for themselves is that soldier's own roll (decision batch 9, 9-34)." `data/core/dice-pool.yaml`, `called_roll`: a new key `together` after `when`, with the comment `# decision batch 9, 9-34, item 1`, carrying the same sentence with "(help)" in place of "(section 1.8)".
  - Item 2. `CONTEXT.md`, **Circumstances**: after "...names a step only for something no rule already prices." append "Hard alone always names this step; the Pace is always written in full as Hard Ride, and the Talents Hard to Kill and Hard Rider keep their names." Chapter 1 section 1.4a, the first paragraph: after "Desperate." (the list of steps) insert "Hard alone always names this step; the Pace is written in full as a Hard Ride (Chapter 7)."
  - Item 3. `data/expedition/hazards.yaml`, `rolls`, `straggler-endure`: gains `notes: Takes the Circumstances in force when the hazard roll was made, Standard unless the GM named a step before then for a lasting condition of the Leg that touches it; no step is named or changed for it after, and none for the row's own danger (decision batch 9, 9-34).` Re-render Chapter 7's hazard roll table. Chapter 7 section 7.1, the **Circumstances** paragraph under that table: after "and every called roll in a Waypoint scene." insert "The Straggler's Endure roll, and any roll a Leg Hazard or Night row calls for, takes the step in force when the hazard is rolled, Standard unless the GM named one before then for a lasting condition of the Leg; none is named for it after, and none for the danger the row describes (decision batch 9, 9-34)."

### 9-35: damage a ruling names that brings a soldier to 0 Health (owner; OQ-177, review round 1 Opus M4)

- **Decision:** The rule stands: damage a ruling names that brings current Health to 0 makes the soldier Down with an immediate Critical Injury, as all damage does. When a called roll's stakes name damage or a fall, the GM states the soldier's current Health aloud with the stakes, so the table knows before the dice whether a failure can put the soldier Down. The playtest log (9-17) tags every Critical Injury that damage a ruling named caused. OQ-177 is read again at the retune as a playtest question.
- **Why:** The owner excluded a Critical Injury named as a stake, and this one comes through Health, as all human harm does (ADR-0005); the risk is shown before the dice, which is the batch's line.
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0005's batch 9 review paragraph reads "decided (c), the Health box paragraph applies" in place of its OQ-177 sentence.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 2: after the menu list and before "A stake is never" insert "When the stakes name damage or a fall, the GM states the soldier's current Health with them (decision batch 9, 9-35)."
  - `data/core/dice-pool.yaml`, `called_roll`: a new key after `failure_menu`: `harm_stakes: When the stakes name damage or a fall, the GM states the soldier's current Health with them (decision batch 9, 9-35; OQ-177).`
  - Chapter 3 section 3.1, *Harm a ruling names*: after "The harm is named with the stakes and does not change once the dice are rolled." insert "The GM states the soldier's current Health with it, so the table knows before the dice whether a failure can put the soldier Down with a Critical Injury (decision batch 9, 9-35)."
  - `data/harm/health.yaml`, `harm_kinds`, `damage`, `by_ruling`: gains `stated_with: the soldier's current Health, said aloud with the stakes (decision batch 9, 9-35; OQ-177)`.
  - No other file changes.

### 9-36: a called roll inside a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish (review round 2, Opus M1; OQ-179)

- **Decision:** A called roll can be made anywhere, a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish included, and in a fight it is the last of three answers the GM's one ruling on a described act can give (9-27), tried in this order:
  1. **The order of the ruling.** The act is a Catalog entry the soldier takes as it is written; or it is an improvised act, because an entry of kind `action` the soldier could take in that fight has an effect the act imitates, and that entry is its model (9-8, 9-27); or, only when no such entry does and the act would change nothing the fight tracks, it is a called roll. Otherwise it happens with no roll, or cannot be done, as Chapter 1, section 1.1, item 1 gives. The model entry comes first: an act whose effect any entry of kind `action` in that fight has is an improvised act, never a called roll. "What the fight tracks" is every tracked value of Chapter 2, section 2.9 (the index of section 5.14 in a Titan Engagement; the Skirmish's values in Chapter 7), a Position, Engaged and Apart, Attention, an Opening, a Titan's or Foe's roll or card, a clock, and a Foe's Health, Guard, Grit, or Parley value. A called roll in a fight therefore never gives an effect an entry has, never kills, and never reaches any of these; it is for the act that lives in the fiction beside the fight, such as hauling a granary's doors shut so the Titan cannot follow the civilians through, or cutting a signboard's strap so it swings clear of a comrade's line.
  2. **What it spends and how it is built.** In a fight a called roll spends the soldier's action and is made on the soldier's turn, as an improvised act is. Its entry (from the entries marked `when_called`, or an attribute alone), its Circumstances (Standard by default in a Titan Engagement, 9-4; the step in force for a lasting condition, 9-26), and its stakes are named before the pool is built, as item 2 gives; it needs 1 success; it can be Pushed and Covered. Help on it is the fight's Help, not the GM's ruling on who is present and able: in a Titan Engagement a comrade with an unspent action this round at the same Position as the roller or one Position step away, spending that action (Chapter 1, section 1.8); in a Skirmish up to 3 comrades taking part who are not Down, each spending their action, whether Engaged or Apart (Chapter 7, section 7.4). The GM names no cost of time or position for Help in a fight; the action is its cost.
  3. **The stakes in a fight.** The menu of 9-5 applies, one cost per failed roll, read in a fight as follows. *It does not happen* is the default, and the action is spent either way. *Stress* 1, *damage* 1 to 3 typed, and *a lost item or unit* are as written, with the soldier's current Health stated when damage or a fall is staked (9-35). *A fall* is low or high as the GM names from where the soldier stands, or low if none is named, never extreme, and Chapter 4's Height steps never raise it: the band a ruling names is the band, inside a Titan Engagement as outside one (9-30; review round 2, C1). *Time or position* is a described change in the situation outside what the fight tracks (the doors will not hold, the civilians scatter, the ladder is gone) and never a Position, a card, a turn, a move, an action beyond the one the act spent, Attention, an Opening, or a clock. What success gives is the act itself, information, or an object that is not a gear item (9-31); in a fight it never gives a Position, which only a move changes, nor anything else the fight tracks, and 9-31's "better position that the next roll reckons with" reads there as a change in the situation outside what the fight tracks.
  4. **Trying again.** Item 4's first clause governs, a called roll included: the soldier may try again on a later turn, spending that turn's action and nothing else. The fight's turns are the change item 4 asks for, and no cost of time or position is named for a retry in a fight.
  5. **Drives.** A called roll in a fight counts as the soldier's own act of the entry the GM named, and meets a Drive's turn test there, as Chapter 2's *Acts* paragraph already says; that sentence stands as written, and ADR-0024, limit 3, needs no change.
  6. **What stays closed.** The passive roll is never made in a fight (9-10). A soldier facing an attack dodges or Blocks; no called roll stands in for a Reaction (9-25). Chapter 5's and Chapter 7's Rulings paragraphs name the called roll beside the improvised act, so a GM reading either chapter finds the answer.
- **Why:** The owner said rulings reach everywhere, Titan Engagements included, and asked that the GM be trusted; 9-4 read that as the soldiers' pools and 9-8 as improvised acts under a model entry, and 9-27 then made the GM classify every described act in a fight, which left the act no entry can model with no answer. The review's option 1 would answer it with no dice: the granary doors shut for free or cannot shut, which is a ruling without a roll at exactly the moment the owner wants dice. Option 2 gives the dice and keeps every figure: the model entry is tried first, so nothing that imitates a measured effect escapes its row, and a called roll is confined to what no row measures, the fiction beside the fight. Item 4's first clause, the Help sentence after item 6, Chapter 2's Drive sentence, and ADR-0024 limit 3 were all written for a called roll in a fight, so the reading that makes them true is the one already half in the text. Help is the fight's because the fight already prices presence and ability by Position and by the action, and a GM ruling on it in a Titan Engagement would be a second Help rule beside limit 7's. The fall reading is 9-30 applied where the review found it unapplied. Figures: none moved; called rolls are not measured (9-17), and OQ-168 counts them.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0024 limits 7, 9, and 17 (edited in the list; `## Amended`). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Called roll (one sentence on a fight; the drafter applies it, as the `CONTEXT.md` bullet below gives).
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 1: after "The GM calls rolls sparingly: a roll is for a moment with something at stake." insert "A called roll can be made anywhere, a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish included. In a fight the GM first asks whether the act is a Catalog entry taken as written or an improvised act with a model entry (section 1.9); it is a called roll only when no entry of kind action the soldier could take in that fight has an effect the act imitates and the act would change nothing the fight tracks (no tracked value, Position, Attention, Opening, card, clock, or Foe value). It then spends the soldier's action, and Help on it is the fight's Help (section 1.8; Chapter 7, section 7.4) (decision batch 9, 9-36)."
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 2, the menu, the **Time or position** bullet: append "In a fight, a change in the situation outside what the fight tracks, never a Position, a card, a turn, a move, an action beyond the one the act spent, Attention, an Opening, or a clock (decision batch 9, 9-36)."
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 2, *What success gives*: after "or Squad Supply found as Chapter 4 caps it (section 4.10)." insert "In a fight, success never gives a Position, which only a move changes, nor anything else the fight tracks (decision batch 9, 9-36)."
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 4: replace "In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, a soldier can attempt an action again on a later turn." with "In a Titan Engagement or a Skirmish, a soldier can attempt an action again on a later turn, a called roll included, spending that turn's action and nothing else (decision batch 9, 9-36)."
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, the paragraph after item 6: replace "and outside a Titan Engagement can be Helped as the GM rules (section 1.8)." with "and can be Helped: outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish as the GM rules, and in a fight as the fight's Help rule gives (section 1.8; Chapter 7, section 7.4; decision batch 9, 9-36)."
  - Chapter 1 section 1.3, step 1, *Acts no rule covers*: after "an act that is not an entry taken as written is an improvised act with one entry as its model (section 1.9)" insert ", and a called roll only when no entry of kind action could model it and it would change nothing the fight tracks (section 1.1, item 1; decision batch 9, 9-36)".
  - Chapter 1 section 1.8, *Outside a Titan Engagement*, the **On a called roll** bullet: append "In a Skirmish, Help on a called roll is the Skirmish's Help (Chapter 7, section 7.4): up to 3 comrades taking part who are not Down, each spending their action, and the GM names no cost for it. In a Titan Engagement, Help on a called roll is the Help of *Requirements* above, spending the helper's action, with no cost the GM names (decision batch 9, 9-36)."
  - Chapter 1 section 1.9, *Turns and actions*, the bullet "An **action** is Help (section 1.8), one Action Catalog entry (Chapter 2) other than the entries the Catalog marks as not an action, or an improvised act (below).": replace "or an improvised act (below)." with "an improvised act (below), or a called roll for an act no entry of kind action could model that changes nothing the fight tracks (section 1.1, item 1; decision batch 9, 9-36)."
  - Chapter 1 section 1.9, the *Improvised acts* bullet: append "An act no entry of kind action in that fight could model, that would change nothing the fight tracks, is a called roll instead: it spends the action, Help on it is the fight's, its fall is low or high, and its time or position cost and its success touch nothing the fight tracks (section 1.1; decision batch 9, 9-36)."
  - Chapter 2 section 2.9, *Outside a fight*: after "Once the dice are rolled, the entry, the Circumstances, and the stakes do not change, and the result stands." insert "In a fight a called roll is made the same way, for the acts *In a fight* below leaves to it (decision batch 9, 9-36)."
  - Chapter 2 section 2.9, *In a fight*: after "Whether a described act is an entry taken as written is part of the GM's ruling on its model entry (decision batch 9, 9-27)." insert "When no entry of kind `action` the soldier could take in that fight has an effect the act imitates, and the act would change nothing the fight tracks (no tracked value, Position, Engaged or Apart, Attention, Opening, card, clock, or Foe value), it is a **called roll**, made as *Outside a fight* gives: it spends the soldier's action, Help on it is the fight's Help, its stakes are read as Chapter 1, section 1.1, item 2 reads them in a fight, and it never gives an effect an entry has (decision batch 9, 9-36)."

### 9-37: the Mission Brief's route: what Command sets, what stays closed, the bounds, and the log (review round 2, Opus M3; OQ-179)

- **Decision:**
  1. **What Command sets.** The route the Mission Brief names sets, once, before the Expedition begins, each Leg's Distance Band and Formation Post and the days on which Command orders a Hard Ride (9-15). The Leg Hazard roll then reads the Distance Band, the Post, and the Pace from the route as written, as it reads the night camps made, the Leg roll's outcome, and the Signal Relay's flare. Once the Expedition begins, no ruling changes a Leg's band, its Post, or its Pace: "nothing changes it afterwards" (Chapter 7, *The route*) binds the GM too.
  2. **What stays closed.** The hazard and Night tables and their rows; the D6; the amount each modifier adds (Near 0, Far 1, Deep 2; +1 per night camp made; the Post's and the Pace's amounts in `legs.yaml`; the Leg roll's +1, and -1 per success beyond the first; the flare's -2); and which modifiers apply, which the route, the night camps made, the Leg roll, and the flare decide and no ruling adds, removes, or changes. Every sentence that says "the hazard and Night tables and their modifiers" stay as written (Chapter 1, section 1.1, item 6; Chapter 7's Rulings paragraph and its *Decided* note under *Waypoint scenes*; ADR-0009's batch 9 paragraph; 9-9 and 9-15 above) is read this way: the amounts and their application are closed, and the route's values are Command's. The two sentences are then not in conflict, and the chapter text says so in the words below.
  3. **The bounds.** A Brief's route, in the first playtest: has 4 to 6 Legs, the interim route's D3+3; has no Leg deeper than the interim route gives its place on the route (the first and last Legs are Near, the second and second-to-last are Near or Far, and every Leg between them is Near, Far, or Deep); and has at most one Depot, which is never the gate. Formation Posts and Hard Ride days are Command's to name, a Hard Ride at most once a day and only as the day's third Leg, as *The day* already gives. A route outside these bounds is not a Brief's route in the first playtest: the GM writes within them or rolls the interim route. Phase 2 may widen them (OQ-141).
  4. **The playtest log** tags every route a Brief set, beside every framed start (9-17): the number of Legs, each Leg's Distance Band and Formation Post, the Hard Ride days, and the Depots; an interim route is logged as rolled. The retune reads authored Expeditions apart from rolled ones (OQ-167).
- **Why:** 9-15 gave Command the route because a Brief always carried one, and kept the modifiers closed because ADR-0009's hazard growth is the ride's pacing and economy brake; the review is right that the Distance Band, the Post, and the Pace are one number read twice, so the closed sentence must say what is closed, the amount and its application, or it forbids what 9-15 allows. The bounds are the interim route's own envelope, so no Brief plays above the distribution the tables ship for and the playtest counts on: the review's six-Leg all-Deep route reaches a Titan Engagement on 94.3% of hazard rolls at a mid-Expedition Leg and Overrun on 80.9% by the fifth day, against the interim route's 64.2% and 15.1% on its typical Deep Leg, and no Expedition figure is measured before the playtest (Chapter 7's design note). The Depot bound keeps ADR-0009's attrition: canisters, Blade Sets, and Squad Supply come back only through a full Standard Issue, and a Brief that marks every Waypoint a Depot would remove the one cost a Titan Engagement leaves on the ride. Posts and the Pace are bounded by their own rules and by the fiction (which Post a rookie Squad rides is the mission), and each adds at most +1 to a Leg, inside the interim route's own range for that Leg, so they are tagged rather than capped; the retune can price a Vanguard-every-Leg Brief from the log. The tag is the smallest fix and the one OQ-167 already has a shape for. Figures: the review's simulation, as above.
- **ADR change:** ADR-0009 `## Amended` (the bounds; what "their modifiers" means); ADR-0014 `## Amended` (the log tags a framed route). Applied.
- **Glossary change:** Mission Brief (the bounds; the drafter applies it, as the `CONTEXT.md` bullet below gives).
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 1 section 1.1, item 6: replace "the Leg roll, except its Circumstances (section 1.4a), the hazard and Night tables and their modifiers, "always advance", rations, and the Straggler's random victim;" with "the Leg roll, except its Circumstances (section 1.4a), the hazard and Night tables, their rolls, and their modifiers, whose amounts are closed and which the route the Mission Brief sets, the night camps made, the Leg roll, and the flare decide (Chapter 7, section 7.1; decision batch 9, 9-37), "always advance", rations, and the Straggler's random victim;".
  - Chapter 7 preface, **Rulings**: replace "the route, its Waypoints, its Formation Posts, and its Hard Ride days, writing the Mission Brief as Command, and the Waypoint scenes between Legs (decision batch 9, 9-15);" with "the route, its Waypoints, its Formation Posts, and its Hard Ride days, writing the Mission Brief as Command within the bounds *The route* gives, so that the route sets each Leg's Distance Band, Formation Post, and Pace, which the hazard roll then reads as written and no ruling changes once the Expedition begins, and the Waypoint scenes between Legs (decision batch 9, 9-15 and 9-37);". In the same paragraph replace "the hazard and night hazard rolls and their rows, rolled in the open, described as they come up, and never softened or swapped;" with "the hazard and night hazard rolls, their rows, and their modifiers' amounts, rolled in the open, described as they come up, and never softened or swapped;".
  - Chapter 7 section 7.1, *The route*: after the four-bullet list of what the Brief names and before "When the Squad rides with no Brief", insert a paragraph: "**Bounds.** A Brief's route has 4 to 6 Legs, the interim route's count. No Leg is deeper than the interim route gives its place: the first and last Legs are Near, the second and second-to-last are Near or Far, and every Leg between them is Near, Far, or Deep. At most one Waypoint is a Depot, and the gate never is. Formation Posts and Hard Ride days are Command's to name, a Hard Ride at most once a day as the day's third Leg (*The day*). The route sets each Leg's Distance Band, Formation Post, and Pace, which the hazard roll reads as written; once the Expedition begins, no ruling changes them. A route outside these bounds is not a Brief's route in the first playtest: the GM writes within them or rolls the interim route below. The playtest log records every route a Brief set, with its Legs, each Leg's Distance Band and Post, the Hard Ride days, and its Depots, and marks an interim route as rolled, so that the retune reads authored Expeditions apart from rolled ones (decision batch 9, 9-37; OQ-167)."
  - Chapter 7 section 7.1, *Waypoint scenes*, the **Decided (decision batch 9, 9-15; OQ-141)** note: replace "while the Leg roll, the hazard and Night tables and their modifiers, rations, and the Straggler's random victim stay as written (ADR-0009, as amended)." with "while the Leg roll, the hazard and Night tables, their rolls, and their modifiers' amounts, which the route, the night camps made, the Leg roll, and the flare decide, rations, and the Straggler's random victim stay as written, and a Brief's route keeps within the bounds *The route* gives (ADR-0009, as amended; decision batch 9, 9-37)."
  - Chapter 7 section 7.1, the design note (decision batch 7, 7-14; OQ-141): append "A Brief's route keeps within the interim route's envelope, 4 to 6 Legs, no Leg deeper than the interim route gives its place, and at most one Depot, so that no authored Expedition plays above the distribution these tables ship for, and the playtest log tags every authored route (decision batch 9, 9-37)."
  - `data/expedition/route.yaml`, `route.mission_brief_fields`: `waypoints` appends "At most one is a depot, and the gate never is (bounds)."; `legs` appends "Between 4 and 6, with no Leg deeper than the interim route gives its place (bounds)."; `hard_ride_days` appends "At most one a day, as the day's third Leg (data/expedition/legs.yaml, day)." A new key `route.bounds` after `mission_brief_fields`, with the comment `# decision batch 9, 9-37 (OQ-179): the interim route's envelope; Phase 2 may widen it (OQ-141)`: `legs: 4 to 6, the interim route's D3+3 (interim_route, legs).`; `distance_bands: No Leg deeper than the interim route gives its place (interim_route, distance-bands): the first and last Legs near; the second and second-to-last near or far; every Leg between them near, far, or deep.`; `depots: At most one Waypoint, never the gate.`; `posts_and_pace: Command's to name; a Hard Ride at most once a day, as the day's third Leg (data/expedition/legs.yaml, day). The top-level `decided` list gains OQ-179. The mapping itself is unchanged, so the rendered table does not move.

### 9-38: a fall a ruling names is low or high inside a Titan Engagement too (review round 2, Opus C1; OQ-180)

- **Decision:** Chapter 4's *Height* procedure gains a first step that stops before the Position steps for a fall whose band is already named.
  1. **A fall a ruling names** takes the band the GM named with the stakes, low or high and never extreme, or low if the GM named none, in a Titan Engagement as well as outside one. No later Height step changes it: neither the Position step nor the raise for Giant Forest or a Large reference Titan.
  2. **A fall another rule names with a band** takes the band that rule names, and no later step changes it.
  3. **A fall a rule names without a band** takes the steps below as they stand, and can still reach extreme. The three such falls inside a fight are a knock loose (Chapter 5, section 5.4), letting go (section 5.2), and a release from a lift (section 5.9), each of which reads "the band Chapter 4's height steps give"; a fall from a horse keeps its own step, and a carried comrade keeps the carrier's band.
  Nothing else in section 4.6 changes: the bands, the fall damage table, the procedure, and where a fall lands are untouched.
- **Why:** 9-30 capped a ruling's fall at low or high so that harm a ruling names never reaches 4 damage, and ADR-0024 limit 17 states that cap with no exception for a fight, as Chapter 3 section 3.1 does ("It is never 4 damage or more"). Chapter 4 stated the cap twice but tied the instruction to stop reading Positions to its *Outside a Titan Engagement* paragraph, so inside one the Height steps still read the soldier's Position and then raised the band: a fall staked on a roll made at Blind Spot against a Large Titan read high and was raised to extreme. This item is the review's option 2, the narrow one: it restores exactly what 9-30 decided and leaves knock loose and letting go, the two rule-named falls inside a fight that deliberately use the Height steps, untouched. The review's option 1, a general step for "a rule or a ruling", would invite a reader to stop those two at a band they never name. Option 3 was not available: reversing 9-30 needs an owner answer, not a chapter fix. 9-36 reads a called roll's fall in a fight the same way and depends on this item for the procedure that carries it. Figures: the extreme band rolls D6+4 on the fall damage table, so it deals 4 damage on a 5 or a 6, one roll in three; the high band rolls D6+2, whose ceiling is 3.
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0024 limit 17 already states the cap and now holds everywhere; this item is the chapter fix that makes the procedure obey it.
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 4 section 4.6, *Height*: insert a new numbered step before the present step 1, so the steps run 1 to 5. It reads "1. **A band already named.** A fall a ruling names takes the band the GM named with the stakes, low or high and never extreme, or low if the GM named none, in a Titan Engagement as well as outside one. A fall another rule names takes the band that rule names. No later step changes a band this step gives. A rule that makes a soldier fall and names no band, such as a knock loose (Chapter 5, section 5.4), letting go (section 5.2), or a release from a lift (section 5.9), takes the band the steps below give (decision batch 9, 9-38)." The present steps 1 to 4 keep their words and become 2 to 5.
  - Chapter 4 section 4.6, *Height*, the closing paragraph: replace "Outside a Titan Engagement, the rule that makes the soldier fall names the band, or the GM does for a fall a ruling names, low or high only, and a fall is low if neither names one." with "Outside a Titan Engagement there is no reference Titan and no Position to read, so a fall takes the band step 1 gives, and is low if neither a rule nor the GM names one."
  - Chapter 4 section 4.6, *When a soldier falls*, the **Named by a rule or a ruling** bullet: after "never extreme" insert ", in a Titan Engagement as well as outside one, and the Height steps never raise it (*Height*, step 1)".
  - `data/gear/falls.yaml`, `height.steps`: a new first item, before the present four, reading "A fall whose band a ruling names takes that band, low or high and never extreme, or low if the GM named none, in a Titan Engagement as well as outside one (triggers, rule-named); a fall whose band another rule names takes that band. No later step changes a band this step gives. A rule that names a fall and no band, such as a knock loose (data/engagement/titan-format.yaml, effect_types, knock-loose), letting go (data/engagement/positions.yaml, moves, letting_go), or a release from a lift (data/engagement/grab.yaml, release, lifted), takes the band the steps below give (decision batch 9, 9-38)."
  - `data/gear/falls.yaml`, `height.outside_titan_engagement`: replace its text with "Outside a Titan Engagement there is no reference Titan and no Position to read: the fall takes the band the first step of steps gives, and is low if neither a rule nor the GM names one."
  - `data/gear/falls.yaml`, `triggers`, `rule-named`, `height`: after "never extreme" insert ", in a Titan Engagement as well as outside one, and height.steps never raises it".
  - `data/gear/falls.yaml`, `after_the_fall.gm`: after "low or high and never extreme, low if none" insert ", in a Titan Engagement as well as outside one".
  - No other file changes. Chapter 1 section 1.1 item 2, Chapter 3 section 3.1, `data/core/dice-pool.yaml` (`called_roll`, `failure_menu`, `fall`), and ADR-0024 limit 17 already read the cap correctly and are not edited for this item.

### 9-39: an improvised act modelled on the Nape strike (review round 2, Opus M2; OQ-180)

- **Decision:** An improvised act whose model entry is the Nape strike is resolved as **a Nape strike that falls short, whatever its successes**. Each success creates 1 Opening on that Titan, created by the acting soldier, and Relentless adds 1 more, as `titan-harm.yaml` `nape_strikes` `falls_short` gives; the act sets the hooked-by-strike flag as any Nape strike does. Successes that reach or pass the Titan's Nape Depth create their Openings and nothing else: the act never kills the Titan, never lowers Nape Depth, and its successes never count toward a later Nape strike except as those Openings (`no_carry_over`). Everything 9-27 gives is unchanged: the act is made with the Nape strike's attribute, gear items, Talents, and needs, meets its requirements and the rule that the Attention holder cannot strike the Nape, spends the soldier's action, and takes Circumstances as any roll.
- **Why:** 9-27 keeps the Nape strike in the model-entry set, since it is an entry of kind `action` a soldier could take in that fight, and removes only the kill. The Nape strike is the one such entry whose effect is a threshold rather than a count, so removing the kill left the band above the threshold empty and paid a good roll worse than a poor one: at Nape Depth 4, three successes made three Openings and four or more made nothing at all. Reading the act as a strike that falls short, whatever the count, is the model entry's own other outcome at its own size, which is what 9-8 asks an improvised act to produce; it is monotone, and it keeps the act strictly weaker than the real strike, which alone kills (ADR-0007; ADR-0024, limit 9). Taking the Nape strike out of the model set instead, the review's option 2, would narrow what the owner's answer 8 opened and would need the same carve-out written into three files; option 3, a rule that pays nothing at the threshold, keeps the perverse step the finding is about. Figures: the review's simulation of its scenario build reaches 4 or more successes 25.4% of the time and falls short with at least 1 success 67.7%; under this item both bands create 1 Opening per success, so the act is defined across its whole range. No measured figure moves: no simulator family makes an improvised act, and called rolls and rulings are not measured (9-17).
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0024 limit 9 already reads "only a Nape strike taken as its entry is written kills a Titan".
- **Glossary change:** none.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - `data/engagement/titan-harm.yaml`, `nape_strikes`: a new key after `no_carry_over`, with the comment `# decision batch 9, 9-39 (review round 2, Opus M2)`, reading `modelled: An improvised act whose model entry is this one (data/character/action-catalog.yaml, uncatalogued_actions, in_a_fight) is resolved as a strike that falls short, whatever its successes: each success creates 1 Opening and Relentless adds 1 more (falls_short), and it sets the flag (flag). Successes that reach the Nape Depth create their Openings and nothing else: the act never kills (kill), never lowers Nape Depth, and no_carry_over applies to it.`
  - `data/character/action-catalog.yaml`, `uncatalogued_actions.in_a_fight`: after the sentence that ends "only a Nape strike taken as its entry is written kills a Titan" insert "An act modelled on the Nape strike is resolved as a Nape strike that falls short, whatever its successes: each success creates 1 Opening, Relentless adds 1 more, and successes that reach the Titan's Nape Depth create their Openings and nothing else (data/engagement/titan-harm.yaml, nape_strikes, modelled; decision batch 9, 9-39)."
  - Chapter 5 section 5.4, *Improvised acts*, the paragraph that ends "an act modelled on the Nape strike never does.": append "Such an act is resolved as a Nape strike that falls short, whatever its successes: each success creates 1 Opening, created by that soldier, and Relentless adds 1 more, as a strike that falls short does. Successes that reach the Titan's Nape Depth create their Openings and nothing else, and the act lowers no Nape Depth (decision batch 9, 9-39)."
  - Chapter 2 section 2.9, *In a fight*, the paragraph that ends "an act modelled on the Nape strike never does.": append "Such an act is resolved as a Nape strike that falls short, whatever its successes: each success creates 1 Opening, and successes that reach the Nape Depth create their Openings and nothing else (Chapter 5, section 5.4; decision batch 9, 9-39)."
  - No other file changes. A Skirmish has no Nape strike, so `data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml` and Chapter 7 take nothing from this item.

### 9-40: the Night Camp made at the fallback Waypoint (review round 2, Astra M1; OQ-180)

- **Decision:** Four readings, together.
  1. **The camp's Waypoint.** A Night Camp is made at the Waypoint the Squad is at when it begins: the Waypoint the day's last Leg reached, or, after a Waypoint-scene retreat that left no Leg slot (9-33), the Waypoint that Leg started from. A Titan Engagement the Night table begins takes its Anchor Rating from the kind of **that** Waypoint, in place of "the Waypoint the day's last Leg reached".
  2. **A second retreat.** When the camp is already at the Waypoint the day's last Leg started from, a retreat from a Titan Engagement the Night table begins moves the camp no further: the Squad breaks off where it is, the Night Camp goes on at its day-passes step with no new camp roll, ration, or night hazard, and the night counts as one Night Camp made on this Expedition, the one already counted. No Leg is ridden, and nothing falls back past that Waypoint.
  3. **The Night modifier.** The night hazard reads the Distance Band of the day's last Leg **ridden**, which is the Leg that reached the Waypoint the Squad retreated from. A Waypoint-scene retreat and a fallback camp change it in no way, since neither rides a Leg.
  4. **The queued repeat.** The Leg 9-33 queues for the morning is unchanged by anything at that camp, a retreat at night included: the next day's first Leg rides that Leg again at Steady, from step post, with a new Leg roll and a new hazard, counted among that day's Legs.
- **Why:** The Anchor Rating is the ground the fight is fought on, and after a fallback the Squad is on the camp's ground, not the ground it retreated from; 8-27 item f tied the reading to the day's last Leg only because, before 9-33 existed, the camp was always at that Leg's destination. Reading the camp's own Waypoint is the only answer that matches the fiction the rest of the procedure describes, and it changes nothing in the ordinary case, where the two are the same Waypoint. For a second retreat there is nowhere further back that any rule names: ADR-0009's fall back is always to the previous Waypoint, and a second hop would ride a Leg at night, which ADR-0009 forbids; the retreat's cost is already paid in the Leg that must be ridden again, so a retreat that moves nothing is not a free retreat. The Distance Band measures how deep the Squad rode that day, not where it sleeps, and 9-15 and 9-37 keep the Night table's modifiers and their amounts as written. 9-33's schedule is untouched, so the fallback camp is an ordinary Night Camp in every other respect. Figures: none; no Expedition figure is measured before the playtest (ADR-0014, as amended).
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0009 as amended holds: no Leg is ridden at night, and the Squad always advances.
- **Glossary change:** none.

A Titan Engagement the Night table begins takes its Anchor Rating from the kind of that Waypoint (data/expedition/hazards.yaml, night, anchor_rating; data/expedition/route.yaml, waypoint_kinds). ### 9-41: the round's minor readings (review round 2, Opus m1 to m9; OQ-180)

- **Decision:**
  1. **What a place is, and what a scene yields (m1).** A place a called search is made of is one building, wagon, cache, or the like the Squad can search in one go, named by the GM with the stakes. A Waypoint scene yields Squad Supply to **at most two called searches**, whatever the number of places the GM names in it; no later search in that scene finds Squad Supply, whatever its result. 9-29's one-search-a-place cap applies within that. The playtest logs the units a called search finds.
  2. **Whose item a shared roll loses (m2).** 9-34 item 1's payer clauses, which the applied text dropped, are restored in full: a lost item is one of the roller's; a lost unit is the Squad's; time or position falls on the situation.
  3. **What "a roll the row calls for" reaches (m3).** 9-34 item 3's last sentence, "The same holds for any roll a Leg Hazard or Night row calls for", reads: any other roll the row's **own text** calls for, such as the Straggler's Endure roll. A Titan Engagement or a Skirmish a row begins takes its Circumstances as any fight does, including a step the GM names for a condition that begins during it (9-25, 9-26). This is an erratum on 9-34 item 3.
  4. **The ladder's timing rules in one file (m4).** `circumstances.yaml` gains a `hazard_rows` key beside `reactions`, carrying item 3's rule and pointing at `hazards.yaml`.
  5. **The Skirmish's model entry (m5).** A model entry in a Skirmish is one entry of kind `action` the Skirmish list allows, never a Reaction, a roll, a fixed roll, or an option, which rules out `reload`, `release`, `swap-blade-set`, `shed-load`, and `mount-or-dismount`, each of kind `option`. `skirmish.yaml` also carries 9-27's two clauses the chapter already has.
  6. **The infirmary roll's marker (m6).** The renderer's roll-row schema takes `circumstances` as an optional key it does not render, and the infirmary roll carries the real `circumstances: never` field in place of its comment, so every roll excluded from Circumstances is marked by a field.
  7. **A table-made Foe kind waits for Phase 2 (m7).** As 9-7 item 5 states, none ships in the first playtest: `foes.yaml` says so as part of the rule rather than only in a comment, and Chapter 7 section 7.4 says so once beside the Foes table. The playtest ships the three Foe kinds as written, and ADR-0016 as amended is unchanged.
  8. **`gm-horror` in a Skirmish (m8).** A Skirmish is outside a Titan Engagement, so the GM may call `gm-horror` in one, as owner answer 6 and 9-6 give. Chapter 7 names it in the Skirmish's Fear Roll list with a pointer to Chapter 3, and the Skirmish design note records that the reported figures were measured with no GM-called Fear Roll.
  9. **The Health statement at the fall stake (m9).** Chapter 4 section 4.6's **Named by a rule or a ruling** bullet and `falls.yaml` `after_the_fall.gm` carry 9-35's sentence as a cross-reference. It adds no rule.
- **Why:** (1) 9-29 bound one roll to one place but left the GM naming how many places a scene holds, so a scene of three places paid three searches and the cap was only as tight as the GM's granularity; a cap on the scene keeps the scarcity 9-29 exists to protect without touching the stock table or 9-29's per-place rule, and two is the number the review's own option names. It is new and unmeasured, so the playtest carries the units found and the owner may veto it. (2) An apply gap, not a new reading: the clauses are 9-34's own words. (3) Six of the ten Leg Hazard rows and one Night row begin a fight, so the sentence as written froze every dodge, strike, and Reaction in those fights at the hazard roll and forbade the lasting conditions 9-26 allows; the rule 9-34 item 3 meant is about the rolls a row's own text calls for, which is the Straggler's Endure roll and any later row's like it. (4) ADR-0012 asks the YAML to hold the rule so the Foundry system reads the same answer, and a reader of `circumstances.yaml` found only the Reaction timing. (5) The file a reader is told is the source of truth was weaker than the chapter, and its own list offered five options as models that 9-27 rules out. (6) The renderer's closed row keys were the only reason for the comment fallback that round 1 authorised and asked to have reported; one optional key removes it and makes the exclusion audit readable by field. (7) 9-7 item 5 already decided that none ships, but the YAML states the power as live, so a GM who reads only the YAML gets an unmeasured Foe kind with no chapter guardrail. (8) The owner answered that the GM may call a Fear Roll outside a Titan Engagement and left it to GM discretion, so restricting it in a Skirmish would reverse answer 6 and 9-6; the probe's baseline is recorded instead, since the probe never calls one. (9) 9-35 said "No other file changes", so this prints its sentence where a GM actually stakes a fall and changes nothing. Figures: none. Item 1's cap and item 8's note are logged, not measured.
- **ADR change:** none.
- **Glossary change:** none.

### 9-42: Help on a called roll in a Skirmish: the sentences 9-36 left standing (review round 3, Opus M1 and m6, Fable m1 and m2; OQ-181)

- **Decision:** No new rule. 9-36 item 2 and ADR-0024 limit 7 govern: Help on a called roll in a Skirmish is the Skirmish's Help, up to 3 comrades taking part who are not Down, each spending their action, whether Engaged or Apart, and the GM names no cost for it. Every sentence in the chapters, the glossary, or the YAML that gives Help on a called roll "as the GM rules", or gives it free unless the GM names a cost of time or position, is read and written as scoped to outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish, since a Skirmish is outside a Titan Engagement and the phrase "outside a Titan Engagement" otherwise reaches it. The file `dice-pool.yaml` `called_roll.help` points at for the fight's Help, `bonus-dice-sources.yaml` `help`, carries the Skirmish's clause itself (the review's option 1), and the glossary, Chapter 1's Covering and Help requirements, Chapter 2 section 2.9, and the Catalog's rows are narrowed to match (option 2). Chapter 1 section 1.8 *Requirements* points at the **On a called roll** bullet, so a GM who stops at *Requirements* finds the fight's answer.
- **Why:** The reading is 9-36's own, applied where 9-36's "No other file changes" left the older sentences: 9-36 wrote the Skirmish's Help into section 1.8 and Chapter 7 and did not sweep the sentences that predate it. The direction matters: the stale text hands the GM a judgment a closed rule now names (who may Help, and whether Help costs anything), which limit 7 exists to prevent, and it makes Help free in the action economy where the Skirmish prices it at an action; by the review's simulation a called roll at Empathy 3 and Stress 1 goes from 74.9% alone to 89.2% with three helpers. Option 3 (one pointer in place of every sentence) was not taken, because a reader of any one file, and the Foundry system reading it, should find the rule where they are (ADR-0012). Opus m6's premise was off, since *Requirements* does name the called roll in its last bullet, but that bullet is itself stale for a Skirmish, so its fix is this item's. Figures: none moved.
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0024 limit 7 already reads this way.
- **Glossary change:** Help (the drafter applies it, as the `CONTEXT.md` bullet below gives).
- **Chapter impact:**
  - `data/core/bonus-dice-sources.yaml`, `help`, `condition_outside_titan_engagement`: replace "On a called roll (Chapter 1, section 1.1), the helper is present and able to help concretely, as the GM rules." with "On a called roll outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish (Chapter 1, section 1.1), the helper is present and able to help concretely, as the GM rules. In a Skirmish, on a called roll as on any roll there, the helper is a comrade taking part who is not Down, whether Engaged or Apart (data/skirmish/skirmish.yaml, rolls, called-roll; decision batch 9, 9-36 and 9-42)." `spends_outside_titan_engagement`: replace "On a called roll, nothing, unless the GM names before the dice a cost of time or position, which falls when Help is declared, whatever the result (Chapter 1, section 1.8; decision batch 9, 9-28)." with "On a called roll outside a Titan Engagement and a Skirmish, nothing, unless the GM names before the dice a cost of time or position, which falls when Help is declared, whatever the result (Chapter 1, section 1.8; decision batch 9, 9-28). In a Skirmish, the helper's action, and the GM names no cost for it (decision batch 9, 9-36 and 9-42)."
  - `CONTEXT.md`, **Help**: replace "outside one, the rule that calls for the roll states who can Help and what it spends, and on a called roll the GM rules who is present and able, with Help spending nothing unless the GM names a cost of time or position, which falls when Help is declared." with "in a Skirmish it spends the helper's action and needs a comrade taking part who is not Down; outside both, the rule that calls for the roll states who can Help and what it spends, and on a called roll the GM rules who is present and able, with Help spending nothing unless the GM names a cost of time or position, which falls when Help is declared."

The sentence that follows it ("If a rule that called for a roll allows no Help, no one can Cover the roll.") is unchanged.

### 9-43: Fly named for a called roll in a Skirmish (review round 3, Opus M2; OQ-181)

- **Decision:** The GM may name `fly` for a called roll in a Skirmish, as for any called roll outside a Titan Engagement (`fly`, `requirements`; 9-12), and that roll is the one Fly roll a Skirmish has. It is made as Chapter 4 already reads it, and this item adds no procedure: it takes the ODM Gear's Gear Dice (9-24), as a Dodge made with ODM Gear in a Skirmish does, and a Pushed Gear Die showing 1 wears the gear; it makes no soldier airborne, since a Fly roll makes a soldier airborne only when its calling rule says so (Chapter 4, section 4.2, *Airborne*) and a ruling is not such a rule; it is ODM use that makes no Gas Roll, since outside a Titan Engagement ODM use makes one only when the rule that calls for it says so (section 4.2, *Using ODM Gear*); and ODM Gear that reaches 0 on it Jams with no fall, since the soldier is not airborne (section 4.2, *Jam*). It needs 1 success (`fly`, `needs`, outside a Titan Engagement). Like every called roll in a Skirmish it changes nothing the Skirmish tracks: it gives no Engaged or Apart, which only a move changes, and there is no Position for it to give; the act it is named for lives in the fiction beside the Skirmish, such as reaching a hayloft to cut a rope, and its success gives that act. A soldier whose ODM Gear counts as not had, because it is Jammed, cannot be named for `fly` (`without_gear: not_possible`); the GM names Agility alone, with no Gear Dice (9-24). Chapter 7's "no Fly roll" reads "no Fly roll a rule calls for": the sentence closes the Position machinery (ODM moves, the Fly rolls a Position change calls for, the airborne state), not the harness.
- **Why:** The owner opened the called roll everywhere and asked that the GM be trusted. The review's option 1 would have made the most characteristic act of a Survey Corps soldier the one act the gear a Skirmish already lets them dodge with cannot be used for, and would have priced the same act two dice apart from a Chase or a Waypoint scene next door. Option 2 costs no procedure: Chapter 4's airborne, Gas Roll, and Jam rules already answer every question the review asked, so the ruling names them and adds nothing. Nothing the Skirmish tracks is reached, and the pool is closed by 9-24, so one GM cannot roll Fly at 2 Gear Dice while another rolls Agility alone, which was the finding. Figures: none moved; the Skirmish probe makes no called roll (9-41 item 8's note), and a called roll is not measured (9-17). The owner has not seen this reading and may veto it.
- **ADR change:** none. ADR-0024 limit 9 names the entries marked `when_called` with no exception for a fight.
- **Glossary change:** none. **Skirmish** does not name the Fly roll.
- **Chapter impact:**
  - Chapter 7 section 7.4, *Engaged and Apart*, the first bullet: replace "a Skirmish has no Positions, no Anchor Rating, no ODM move, no Fly roll, and no airborne soldier." with "a Skirmish has no Positions, no Anchor Rating, no ODM move, no Fly roll a rule calls for, and no airborne soldier. The one Fly roll in a Skirmish is a called roll the GM names for Fly (*A called roll*, below), and it makes no soldier airborne (decision batch 9, 9-43)."
  - Chapter 7 section 7.4, the **A called roll.** paragraph: append "The GM may name Fly for it, as for any called roll outside a Titan Engagement (Chapter 2, section 2.9): the roll takes the ODM Gear's Gear Dice (decision batch 9, 9-24), as a Dodge made with ODM Gear does here, and a Pushed Gear Die showing 1 wears the gear; it makes no soldier airborne, makes no Gas Roll, and ODM Gear that Jams on it drops no one (Chapter 4, section 4.2); and it gives no Engaged or Apart, which only a move changes. A soldier whose ODM Gear is Jammed cannot be named for Fly; the GM names Agility alone (decision batch 9, 9-43)."
